But the Knight of the Fish cried out for fresh spears for him and Sir Godrick, and must needs run again, and this time the Knight’s spear brake on Sir Godrick, whose shaft held that he drave the Knight of the Fish clean over the arson of his saddle, and but for the goodness of his shield and double jazerant the spearhead had been in his breast withal.

Then Sir Godrick cast up his spearhead, and lifted the visor of his basnet and looked around, and saw Osberne sitting still upon his horse and the long man in the arms of his fellows, and he cried out: “Now this comes of fools! Here is our journey tarried, and one man or two, who be not of our foes, slain or sore hurt, and all for naught. Ho ye! give my man his spear. And thou, Red Lad, come up before they make us do more hurt.”

===Chinese===

传令官去后，戈德里克爵士转向奥斯伯恩，说道：“你说呢，红衣少年——这事与你有相干么？”“桩桩件件都相干，主公，”奥斯伯恩道，“只是我不像你这般动怒。”骑士和颜悦色地看着他，说道：“你并非非出马不可，红衣少年；锋利的战枪是头不祥的畜生，这些人又无疑都是一等一的好手。”奥斯伯恩道：“这本就是分内的事，主公；求您莫叫我退下。”

“也罢，戴上战盔，快些了结算了，”戈德里克爵士道；“聪明人总免不得要伺候痴人的兴致。”

于是二人出阵，见对方早已对列相候：鱼骑士对戈德里克爵士，一条魁伟的大汉——带甲兵士——对奥斯伯恩。这一合说来话短：戈德里克爵士与鱼骑士双双折了枪，却折得那鱼堡骑士踏脱了马镫，险些儿便栽到地上。至于奥斯伯恩，他应付得极为审慎，一枪正送进那长汉守御的破绽之处，恰在臂膀与肩头相接的地方；枪穿透了他的身子，他跌落尘埃，伤得极重。奥斯伯恩的枪不得不撒手，他便从鞍前弓上取下一柄短斧（他不肯拔断板剑），静候事态。鱼骑士却呼喝着叫人给他和戈德里克爵士取新枪来，非要再驰一合；这一回，鱼骑士的枪在戈德里克身上折断，而戈德里克的枪杆坚牢不折，把鱼骑士整个儿掀过鞍桥；若非他那面盾牌与双重札甲着实精良，那枪头早已扎进他的胸膛了。

于是戈德里克爵士竖起枪头，掀起战盔的面甲，四下张望，见奥斯伯恩端坐马上不动，那长汉已由同伴们抱持，便大声喊道：“瞧这都是痴人惹出来的事！行程被耽搁了，一两个并非我们仇敌的人，或死或重伤，全然无谓之至。喂，你们！把我那人的枪还他。红衣少年，你且过来，趁他们还没逼我们闯更多的祸。”

===Original===

But therewith the Knight of the Fish sat up and had come to his wit, and laughed and said: “Here is a surly one! Why, thou might’st complain more if ye had come to the worse as we have. Come now, all the sort of you, into my house, and drink a cup with us for the washing away of all grudge against the honorable custom of the Fish.” Sir Godrick shook his head, but the wrath ran off him and he said: “Sir Knight, thou art debonnaire in thy folly, and I thank thee; this thy bidding might we have taken with a good will hadst thou not compelled us to waste our time in knocking you off your horses. And I am sorry we have hurt thy champion, and well I hope that he will be clean healed.”

“Dost thou?” said he of the Fish; “now will I tell thee that if he be healed, I will send him on to thee to be thy man, that is if he will go. For well I know thee that thou art the Lord of Longshaw: and as to my champion, he will suit thee to a turn, for he is well-nigh as surly as thou, and as stiff in stour as may be.”

Hereat all laughed, and they bade each other farewell, and so departed with good will. So they rode on, and nought more befell that day, and they guested in a fair thorp in good enough welcome.

===Chinese===

可正在此时，鱼骑士坐起身来，神智已复，笑道：“好一个乖戾的家伙！哼，倘若像我们这样吃了亏落了下风的是你们，你倒更有得抱怨呢。来吧，你们这一行人全都进我宅里来，同饮一杯，把对鱼堡光荣习俗的一切芥蒂都涤荡干净。”戈德里克爵士摇了摇头，但怒气已从他身上消退，说道：“骑士先生，你痴行之中倒见雅量，我谢谢你；若不是你逼着我们把时光虚掷在将你们打落马上，你这番吩咐我们原会满心乐意地领受。我们伤了你的勇士，我心下歉然，只愿他痊愈无恙。”

“是么？”鱼堡那位说道；“那我告诉你：他若痊愈，我便打发他上你那里去做你的人——只要他自己肯去。因为我深知你便是朗肖之主；至于我这位勇士，保管合你的意到了十足：他那乖戾劲儿几乎不输于你，斗起来又刚硬无比。”

众人闻言都笑了，彼此道别，好意而散。于是他们继续赶路，那日再无别事，在一座秀美的村庄里投宿，所受的款待也还殷勤。

## XLII / 四十二

===Original===

They Deliver the Thorp-Dwellers from the Black Skinners

Now they rode that fair well-peopled land, and nought befell them to tell of till the fourth day thence, and then, as they were riding a good highway with a somewhat steep bank or little hill on their left hands, as they turned about the said hill and had all the plain to their right hands before them, they saw new tidings, and it was just about high noon. For there lay in their road, a mile and a half ahead, a thorp so big that it was well-nigh a little town, but quite unfenced, though many of the houses were were goodly and great as for such a place. But now all was going ill there, for they saw smoke and flames coming forth from the windows and roofs of many of the houses, and a confused crying and shrieking came down the wind to them, and Osberne the keen-eyed deemed he could see folk, some a-horseback, fleeing down the highway toward them. Then Sir Godrick cried out: “Prick on, good men of mine! This is no case for tarrying, these be the Black Skinners, and if we make not the more haste, all will be under fire and steel.”

And he spurred withal, and Osberne after him. But now as they drew nigher there was no naysaying but that folk were fleeing desperately along the highway, and some with their hands spread out to the newcomers as if praying for help, young men and old, women and children; and after them came howling and smiting men-at-arms in wild armour, and though they were not in all ways like to those with whom the Dalesmen had fought by the Sundering Flood, yet somehow they called those wretches to Osberne’s remembrance, and he knew at once what had befallen, and wrath flamed up in his heart, for it well-nigh seemed to him as if Elfhild must have been borne off again. And he unknit the peace-strings from about Boardcleaver, and drew him forth so that a clear humming noise went forth into the sunlit air, and spurred on so hard that he outwent every man there.

===Chinese===

他们救村民于黑剥皮帮之手

且说他们骑行在那片丰美人稠的乡野之间，一路无事可述，直到第四日。那时他们正走在一条宽阔的大道上，左手边有一道颇陡的岸坡，或者说一座小山；转过那山，右手前方便是一整片平原，他们望见了新的变故，其时正当晌午。原来前方一英里半的路上横着一座村庄，大得几乎算得上一座小镇，却全无围墙寨栅，虽说以这等地方而论，村中许多房屋也称得上堂皇宏敞。可此刻那里正遭着大难：只见许多房屋的窗里、屋顶上腾出浓烟火焰，一片纷乱的呼号惨叫随风送到他们耳中；目光锐利的奥斯伯恩自忖望得见有人正沿着大路朝他们奔逃，有的还是骑着马的。于是戈德里克爵士高声呼道：“策马疾进，我的好儿郎们！这可不是逗留的时候——这些便是黑剥皮帮；我们若不更加快些，一切都要陷于火与刀之下了。”

说着他便催马加鞭，奥斯伯恩紧随其后。待行得近了，事情已无可置疑：百姓们正沿着大路亡命奔逃，有的朝新来的人摊开双手，仿佛哀告求援——有少年也有老者，有妇人也有孩童；在他们身后，跟着一路呼啸砍杀的带甲兵士，甲械装束杂乱无章。这些人虽然与谷民当年在分离洪流边交手过的那些不尽相同，却不知怎的叫奥斯伯恩想起了那伙恶徒，他顿时明白出了什么事，怒火在胸中熊熊腾起，因为他几乎觉得，仿佛埃尔弗希尔德又遭人劫走了。于是他解下缠在断板剑上的和平绳，拔剑出鞘，一声清越的嗡鸣散入日光朗照的空中，随即狠催坐骑，把在场之人个个都甩在了后头。

===Original===

But when the Skinners saw those riders coming on, they stayed the chase, and some few tarried while they shot from their short-bows, which did but little harm, and so they hustled back into the thorp; and some few, the first of them, gat through and off into the fields; but the fleers drew aside to the right hand and the left, calling blessings on the good Knight and his, and, when the torrent of them was past, followed after timidly towards their wasted dwelling. And as Sir Godrick and his were within the thorp they found a many of the Skinners there (two hundreds of their carcases were buried afterwards) and all about by the houses lay mangled bodies of the country-folk, some few with weapons in their hands, but more of women and children. But when Godrick and his had slain the first plump that they had driven in from the road, the Knight cried out: “Ye thorp-dwellers, look to quenching the fires, while we slay you these wolf-swine.” Thereon the countrymen began to run together with buckets wherever the riders were before them. And there was a pretty stream running down the midmost of the street, and though it were dyed with blood that day, it was no worse for the quenching of the flames. Meanwhile Sir Godrick and his set themselves to the work, and it was not right perilous, for the thieves were all about scattermeal in twos and threes, and most afoot robbing and murdering and fire-raising, so that they made but such defence, when they made any, as the rat makes to the terrier. Shortly to say it, in half an hour there was not one of them left alive, save some few who gat to their horses and fled, having cast away their weapons and armour. Then the riders turned to help the thorp-dwellers in quenching their fires, and in some two hours they had got all under wherein was any hope, and the rest they must let burn away.

Then would Sir Godrick have gone his ways, but the poor folk of the thorp prayed him so piteously to abide till the morrow that he had no heart to naysay them. So they brought him and his what things they might get together after the ravage, and begrudged them nought. Moreover in the morning five stout fellows of the younger sort prayed him to take them with him to serve him in war, since they knew not now how to live; so he yeasaid them, nothing loth, and horsed them on the Skinners’ way-beasts, which were good, and armed them with such of their armour as was not too filthy for decent men to use. The rest of the horses and gear they left to the thorp-abiders, to better their hard case withal.

So they departed, and that same day they came on two other thorps, but not so big as this, which had been utterly ravaged, so that there was neither dog nor cat therein, save in one house two little men-children of two and three years old, whom they brought away with them for pity’s sake.

The next day they came to a cheaping-town, walled and defensible, whose gates were shut for fear of the Skinners. But when Sir Godrick had spoken to the captain of the guard at the gate, and had told him how they had fared of late, and of the slaughter of the Skinners, they opened to them joyfully, and made them kindly welcome, and there they rested a three days, of which rest their way-beasts had great need.

===Chinese===

黑剥皮帮望见那些骑者驰来，便住了追赶，有少数几个留下用短弓放箭，却没伤着什么，随即慌忙缩回村里；他们中打头的那几个，则冲出村去，逃散到田野里。逃难的百姓向左右两边闪开，连声为好骑士一行祝祷祝福；等这股人流涌过之后，才怯生生地跟在后面，朝他们那被蹂躏的家园走去。戈德里克爵士一行进得村来，只见村里黑剥皮帮为数甚众（后来掩埋的贼尸达两百具之多），家家户户近旁横陈着村民血肉模糊的尸身：少数几个手里还握着兵器，更多的却是妇孺。待戈德里克一行把从大路上驱进村来的头一伙贼人杀尽，骑士高呼道：“村中住户们，快去张罗救火，我们替你们宰了这班豺狼猪狗。”于是乡民们纷纷提桶奔聚，到骑者掩在前面的地方救火。恰有一道清溪流过村街正中，那天虽被鲜血染红，用来浇灭火焰却毫不逊色。与此同时，戈德里克爵士一行动手料理他们的营生，倒也不算十分凶险，因为那些贼人四下里东两人西三人散作各处，大半步行，正忙于劫掠、凶杀、放火；他们便是抵挡，也不过是老鼠在捕鼠犬跟前的那点抵挡罢了。长话短说：半个钟头之内，村里便再没有一个活着的贼人，只有寥寥几个抛了兵刃甲胄、抢马逃命的除外。随后骑者们转身帮着村民救火；约莫两个钟头后，凡还有指望抢救的都已将火扑定，其余的只好由它烧个干净了。

事毕，戈德里克爵士本欲就此上路，无奈村中穷苦百姓哀哀恳求他留到明日，他不忍回绝。于是村人把劫后所能凑集的物事都拿来奉待他一行，毫无吝惜。而且次日清晨，有五个年少的健壮汉子恳求他带他们同去，投效麾下，从军打仗，因为他们如今已不知何以为生；爵士便应允了——正求之不得——让他们骑上黑剥皮帮的行路牲口，那些牲口都还优良，又拿那些还不至于肮脏得体面人用不得的甲胄，把他们装备起来。其余的马匹辎重，都留给村中住户，好歹贴补他们艰难的境况。

于是他们启程上路。当日又行至两处村庄，虽不似这一座大，却已被蹂躏得精光，村里连一条狗一只猫也不剩，唯有一户人家里留着两个小男娃，一个两岁、一个三岁；他们出于怜悯，把这双孩子带在身边一同走了。

次日，他们来到一座市镇，城垣完固，堪资守御，城门因惧黑剥皮帮而紧闭。待戈德里克爵士与城门的卫队长交涉，讲说了他们近来的行踪际遇以及屠灭黑剥皮帮之事，镇人便欣然开门，殷勤相待；他们在那里歇了三天——这场歇息，他们的行路牲口也正是大大需要的。

## XLIII / 四十三

===Original===

They Come to the Edge of the Wood Masterless

Now when they went on thence, they came within two days into a country all broken up into little hills and ridges, and beset with scraggy shaws, wherein were but few men and fewer dwellings, and the men either hunters or herders of neat, well-nigh wild, and this lasted them for three days more; but they knew hereof beforehand, and had made provision therefor at that last cheaping.

But at the end of the three days they came to a place where was a narrow stretch of green mead and a few acres in the wilderness, and a little river ran through all that, and above it on a height, steep and well-nigh sheer on all sides save one, was a castle high and strong, and as they drew nigh thereto Osberne saw a banner thrust out from the highest tower, and the Knight said to him: “Red Lad, whose banner is that?”

===Chinese===

他们来到无主林边缘

且说他们从那里继续前行，不出两日，便进入一片丘峦脊岭错落分布的地界，四下杂生着疏瘦的小树林，其间人烟稀少，屋舍更稀，当地之人不是猎户，便是牧牛之民，所牧的牛近乎野性；这般光景又延续了三日。好在他们早知此地情形，已在上一处集市预先备办了应用之物。

三日走尽，他们来到一处地方：荒野之中有一带窄窄的绿草甸，不过数英亩，一条小河贯穿其间；草甸上方是一座高冈，除一面外，四壁陡峭得几乎直立，冈上矗立着一座高峻坚固的城堡。行近之时，奥斯伯恩望见最高的塔楼上挑出一面旗帜，骑士便问他道：“红衣少年，那是谁的旗？”


===Original===

“I wot not,” said Osberne.

“Canst thou see the blazon of it?” said Sir Godrick.

“Yea,” said the other; “it hath a White Hart collared and chained with gold and emparked on a green ground.”

“Sooth is that,” said Sir Godrick. “Now look behind thee over thy shoulder.” Even so did Osberne, and saw a banner borne by one of theirs, and the selfsame blazon on it; and now he called to mind that never erst had he seen Sir Godrick’s banner displayed. And he laughed and wondered, and was some little deal abashed, and he said: “Lord, is this Longshaw?” Laughed the Knight in his turn, and said: “What, thou deemest this no very lordly castle for him who hath to withstand barons and portes and kings? Nay lad, look again and tell me if thou seest the Long Shaw; this is called Woodneb, and therein is a captain of mine who hight Edward the Brown, and therein shall we rest a while ere we enter the Wood Masterless. And hence onward to the Long Shaw is a twelve days’ journey if all go well.”

===Chinese===

“我不知道，”奥斯伯恩道。

“你能看清上面的纹章么？”戈德里克爵士问。

“能，”那一位答道；“绿底上有一头白鹿，颈系金环金链，围在苑栅之内。”

“正是如此，”戈德里克爵士道。“现在从你肩上回头望过去。”奥斯伯恩依言回望，见自己一行人中有一名擎着旗帜，旗上正是那同一副纹章；他这才想起，从前从不曾见戈德里克爵士的旗帜展开过。他又笑又讶，还微微有些赧然，便道：“主公，这里就是朗肖么？”骑士这回轮到他发笑，说道：“怎么，你以为对一位要与男爵、市府、列王相抗衡的人来说，这座堡子不够堂皇么？不是的，孩子；再看看，告诉我你可望得见朗肖。此地名为伍德内布，堡中有我属下的一名队长，唤作褐色的爱德华；我们要在这里歇息些时日，再进入无主林。从这里前往朗肖，若诸事顺遂，还须十二天的路程。”


===Original===

Now when Osberne heard that he was the better content, for in good sooth that desert-hold seemed all too strait to keep within its walls the valiancy of Sir Godrick and his host.

So presently the gates were thrown open, and folk gaily clad and armed came forth to meet their lord and his new men, and before them went Edward the Brown, a short thick man, but very sturdy-looking, his hair cut short to his head; small brown eyes had he and short nose, so that he looked somewhat like a bear; but a valiant man he was, and a trusty.

There then they had good entertainment, as men who were at home again, and they abode there seven days ere they departed, and had good disport of hunting and hawking; and there was much minstrelsy and tale-telling in the hall a-nights: and there must Osberne tell what stories he knew of the war of Eastcheaping and the matters of the Dale, both the tidings of his own day and of the days of his fathers; and therewith were men well content, for a good tale-teller he was.

No little also he talked with Sir Godrick, and especially on one matter: for his mind dwelt much on those same Skinners whom they had overthrown, and he kept weighing them against those evil aliens with whom he had fought across the Sundering Flood, and who, he deemed full surely, had borne away Elfhild. And on a day he asked Sir Godrick concerning it, and if these two sorts of wretches had aught to do with it; and he told him all the story of that battle, and what like his foemen were in body and array, and of their horses and armour and weapons, and of their shrieks and the gibbering of their Latin.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩听了这话，心下方才安然了些；说实在的，那座僻野孤堡看去实在太过局促，围墙之内，容不下戈德里克爵士与其麾下人马的豪武英风。

随即堡门大开，衣甲鲜明的人们出迎他们的主上与新人；走在最前的是褐色的爱德华——一个矮壮的汉子，看去极为结实，头发贴头剪得极短；他生着一双褐色的小眼睛和一个短鼻子，看去倒有几分像头熊；然而他是个勇士，也是个可靠之人。

在那里他们受到殷勤款待，一如归家；一行人在那里住了七日方才启程，日日以狩猎放鹰为乐；每到夜里，堂上多有乐师弹唱、众人讲古：奥斯伯恩便在那里讲他所知的东集之战与深谷诸事，既有他当日的见闻，也有他父辈时代的旧闻；众人听了都甚满意，因为他本是讲故事的好手。

此外他又与戈德里克爵士谈了许多，尤其专谈一件事：他心里时时惦着他们先前剿灭的那伙剥皮帮，不断拿他们与那些邪异的外寇相较——他昔日曾在分离洪流对岸与那些外寇厮杀，并确信正是他们掳走了埃尔弗希尔德。一日，他就此事询问戈德里克爵士，问这两类恶徒彼此之间可有干系；他把那一战的始末都对爵士讲了，讲那些敌人身形装束如何，马匹、甲胄、兵刃如何，又讲他们的尖声嚎叫与他们叽里咕噜的拉丁话。


===Original===

Then said Sir Godrick: “I will tell thee what meseemeth of thy foemen of that day, that they be of the kindred of these Black Skinners, though of another tribe, so that men call them the Red Skinners, though ye shall know that neither the Red nor the Black call themselves Skinners, which is but a name of terror which the country-folk have fixed on them for their evil deeds. Now further, although the Red Skinners be worse than any man else, they are not so bad as the Black. That is, they are more like men and less like wolves standing upright: to wit, they waste not and destroy not everything forthright, but keep it to make some gain thereof. As for example, they slay not and rip not up all their captives whatsoever they may be, but keep such as they may deem likely to sell to the thrall-cheapers. Now as to thy foes being of this ill folk, I deem it more like the more I think thereof, for not only hast thou given me a true picture of their aspect, but it is mostly the other side of the Sundering Flood which they haunt, though whiles we meet them about the borders of the Wood Masterless nigh unto the Flood. Withal I must tell thee, that though I speak of both the Black and the Red Skinners as of nations or tribes, I say not but they be mingled with runagates of divers folks; for whatever is worst or evillest or cruellest will drift toward them; and I wot not but that these men be worse than they of the blood, having in them more malice and grudging. But this I know for sure, that these are they who set them to work on such a business, and spy for them, and sell them their plunder, as they may well do since they are of aspect like other folk and know their tongues﻿—But what aileth thee, Red Lad, to look so wan and so perturbed of countenance? Hast thou aught on thine heart which you wouldst tell me?”

“That have I,” said Osberne: and so shortly as he might he told his lord the whole tale of his dealings with Elfhild, and how she had vanished away before hand might touch hand or face face; and how he deemed that she had been borne off by these same Red Skinners. And when he had done Sir Godrick said: “Poor lad, and this was the cause then that made thee so eager to take service along with me! Well, thou hast done wisely; for first, thou hast got thee a faithful friend; and next, if thou never amendest it nor settest eyes on the maiden again, yet surely the doing of deeds shall ease thy sorrow, till at last it shall be scarce a sorrow to thee, but a tale of the past. And moreover, in coming to my house thou shalt have come to the only place where thou mayst perchance happen on tidings of her; since with these men we have to do, and also at whiles with those who deal with them by way of chaffer. And if we fall in with any of the Red ones, thou shalt make what captives thou wilt, and for the saving of their lives they may tell thee somewhat to further thy search. Hold up thine head then! for surely even now thou art doing all that thou mayst in the matter.”

Herewith must Osberne be content perforce, and in sooth his heart was the lighter that he had told his trouble to so good a friend as was Sir Godrick.

===Chinese===

戈德里克爵士便道：“我告诉你，依我看来，你当日的那些敌人，与这黑剥皮帮本是同族，只是别为一部，故而人们称之为红剥皮帮；不过你当知道，红帮也罢、黑帮也罢，都不自称为‘剥皮帮’——那不过是乡民因其恶行而加诸他们的一个骇人恶名罢了。再者，红剥皮帮虽比世上任何人都坏，却还不像黑帮那样绝。就是说，他们更像人，不那么像直立行走的豺狼：换言之，他们并不一味糟蹋毁尽万物，而是留下些来牟利。譬如，他们并不把俘虏不分皂白一律杀却开膛，而是留下那些看来能卖给贩奴商的。至于你的仇家是否即属这等恶族，我越想越觉得像：你不但给我描画了他们的形貌，真切无误，而且他们出没之地，多在分离洪流对岸，虽然我们时或也在无主林边缘、近洪流之处撞见他们。还有我必须告诉你：我虽把黑、红剥皮帮说成民族或部族一般，却不敢说他们中间没有掺杂各族的流窜匪徒；因为天下最坏、最恶、最残的一切，都会向他们那里漂流而去；我说不定这些外来之徒比血统族人更坏，因为他们心中怨毒与嫉恨更深。但有一事我知道得确凿：正是这些家伙替他们张罗这等勾当，为他们刺探，替他们销赃——他们外貌与常人无异，又通晓各地语言，自然做得来。——可你怎么了，红衣少年，脸色这般苍白，神色这般不安？你心上可有什么事要告诉我么？”

“正是有事，”奥斯伯恩道：于是他尽可能简短地向主上讲述了他与埃尔弗希尔德交往的全部始末，讲她如何在手与手相及、脸与脸相偎之前便倏然消失，讲他如何认定她正是被这伙红剥皮帮掳走的。他讲罢，戈德里克爵士说道：“可怜的孩子，原来这便是你当年急急投到我麾下效力的缘由！好，你做得聪明：其一，你为自己赢得了一位忠实的朋友；其二，纵然此事永无补救、你从此再见不到那姑娘，但建功立事终能宽解你的哀伤，到得后来，它于你几乎不再是哀伤，而只是一段往昔的故事了。况且，你来到我家中，便是来到了唯一可能偶遇她消息的地方；因为我们少不了要同这些人打交道，也时或同那些与他们做买卖的人往来。倘若我们撞见红帮的人，你要掳多少俘虏都随你，他们为求活命，说不定能供出些助你寻访的线索。那么，抬起头来吧！因为在这件事上，你眼下确实已尽了人事。”

奥斯伯恩对此只得勉强满足；而实话实说，他把心事倾诉给了戈德里克爵士这样一位好友，心头的确轻松了几分。

## XLIV / 四十四

===Original===

They Reach Longshaw and Osberne Gets Him a New Name

But the seven days over, they departed on their ways to the house of Longshaw, which well they knew; and they rode first for two days through rough land pretty much as it had been before Woodneb, and they saw all that way but three little houses of hunters or fowlers; and this, they told Osberne, right on from Woodneb was the beginning of the Wood Masterless. Thereafter they came amongst great timber-trees with wood lawns betwixt, and but little underwood, and a goodly piece of the world that seemed unto Osberne. Three days it held so, and then came broken ground, whiles with much tangled thicket and whiles treeless, and this was a two days’ ride; and many were the wild deer therein, so that their cheer was greatly amended. Thereafter was the wood thinner and more plain, and there was a clear road through it; and on the first day of their riding this way they came upon a sort of folk who were sitting on the greensward eating their dinner. They were fifteen all told, all of them with weapons, but Sir Godrick and his came upon them so suddenly that they had no time to rise and flee, so sat still abiding haps. They had a good few of sumpter-horses with them, and it was soon clear to see that, though they were weaponed, they were not men-at-arms, but chapmen. Sir Godrick entreated them courteously, and asked them whence and whither, and prayed them of tidings. They said they were come from the City of the Sundering Flood, and had ridden the Wood instead of taking ship on the river, which was far safer, because they were bound for some of the cheaping towns to which Sir Godrick and his had given the go-by. They said that all was at peace in the City and the Frank thereof, and there was little of strife anywhere anigh. In the end they bade the Knight and his men sit with them and share their feast under the greenwood tree. Sir Godrick yeasaid that with a good will, and they were presently all very merry. Sooth to say, though they made as if they knew him not, and never named his name, they knew him well enough, and were a little afeard of him, and only too well content if he named himself not, for they were of the gilds who were scarce good friends with Longshaw: so that it had been little more than a fair deed of war if he had made them unbuckle and open.

When dinner was over and they were drinking a cup, he called three of the wisest of them apart along with Osberne, and asked them straightway if they knew of any fair maid who had been bought of late by any chapman from the Red Skinners, and he bade Osberne tell closely what like was Elfhild: even so he did, sore abashed the while. But when he was done, the chapmen laid their heads together, and asked one or two others of their company, but could give no tidings of any such.

===Chinese===

他们抵达朗肖，奥斯伯恩得了一个新名号

七日既满，他们便动身上路，往朗肖府邸而去；那地方他们原本熟稔。头两天，他们骑行在崎岖之地，与伍德内布以先的地势大抵相仿，一路只见到三所猎户或捕鸟人的小屋；他们对奥斯伯恩说，打从伍德内布再往前，便正是无主林的起点了。此后他们行到巨木参天之处，林间点缀着一方方林中草坪，树下灌木甚少，在奥斯伯恩看来，这真是一方好天地。如此行了三日，接着地势转为破碎起伏，时而密生纠结的荆丛，时而寸木不生，这段路走了两天；其间野鹿极多，他们的伙食因此大为改观。再往后，林木渐稀渐平，林中有一条现成的大路；循路而行的头一天，他们撞见一伙人正坐在草地上用饭。那伙人共计十五名，个个身携兵刃；只是戈德里克一行来得太突然，他们来不及起身逃走，只得坐着不动，听天由命。他们带有不少驮马；不多时便看得分明：这些人虽有兵刃在身，却并非带甲战士，而是些商贾。戈德里克爵士待他们谦和有礼，问他们从何处来、往何处去，又请他们讲讲消息见闻。他们说，自己从洪流之城来，没有乘船走水路——那本稳妥得多——而是取道林中骑行，因为他们要去的几处市集城镇，正是戈德里克一行此番绕过未去的那些地方。他们说，洪流之城与其自治地界之内一概太平，左近各地也少有争斗。末了，他们请爵士一行同坐在绿林树下，共赴筵席。戈德里克爵士欣然应允，于是不多时众人便欢洽异常。说实话，他们虽然装作不认识爵士，也绝口不提他的名姓，其实心里雪亮，还微微有些怕他，巴不得他也不自报家门；因为他们所属的那些行会，与朗肖算不上什么好朋友：倘若爵士当真逼他们解下行囊、开箱受检，那也不过算得一场名正言顺的战功罢了。

饭罢饮着酒，爵士把其中三位最精明的叫到一旁，奥斯伯恩也随同过去；他开门见山地问他们，可知道近来可有哪位商贾从红剥皮帮手里买下过什么美貌姑娘，又命奥斯伯恩把埃尔弗希尔德的容貌细细形容一遍。奥斯伯恩依言说了，一面说，一面羞窘得厉害。待他说完，商贾们凑头商议，又问了同行的一两个人，却打听不出半点这样的消息来。

===Original===

So therewith they parted, and Sir Godrick and his rode the wood, which was diverse of kind, for six days more; and at last, on a bright sunny afternoon, when after riding a plain not much be-timbered they had made their way through a thick and close wood for some five hours, they came out of the said wood on to a plain of greensward cleft by a fair river, which winded about the foot of a long low ridge where were orchards and gardens a many, and all above them so many buildings and towers and walls of stone, that to Osberne it seemed as if they had before them a very fair town. But even therewith all the company by Sir Godrick’s bidding stayed, and drew up in a line, and the banner of the Hart impaled was displayed; and Sir Godrick spake to Osberne and said: “Lo, Red Lad, my House of Longshaw, and this is the Shaw which we have come through: now how likest thou the house?”

“Well, and exceeding well,” said Osberne; “it is as a town.”

“Yea,” said Sir Godrick; “and therefore if I can but keep it well victualled, and have with me a host big enough of stout men, it shall never be taken.”

===Chinese===

于是两下里分手。戈德里克一行又在林中骑行了六天，林子形形色色，各地不同；最后，在一个晴朗明媚的下午，他们先是骑行过一片林木稀少的平野，又在茂密幽深的树林里穿行了约莫五个钟点，终于出了树林，来到一片绿草平原上；一条秀美的河流将平原剖开，绕着一道长长的低冈山脚萦回流转；冈上果园、花园难以胜数，层叠而上，满目尽是楼宇、塔楼与石头墙垣，在奥斯伯恩看来，他们面前俨然是一座极其秀美的城镇。可就在这时，全队人马奉戈德里克爵士之令停住，列成一行，那面并绘鹿纹的旗号展开擎出；戈德里克爵士对奥斯伯恩说道：“看哪，红衣少年，这便是我的朗肖之家，我们方才一路穿来的那片林子，便是朗肖的‘肖’字。如今你觉得我这家园如何？”

“很好，好得很，”奥斯伯恩说；“这简直是一座城。”

“正是，”戈德里克爵士道；“所以只要我粮草充足，身边又带着一支足够众多的剽悍人马，它就永远不会陷落。”

===Original===

Now Osberne looked again, and he saw that midmost of the towers and walls was a very great hall exceeding fair, with lovely pinnacles and spires and windows like to carven ivory, and beside it a church fairer yet; and then before it and lower down the hill and on either side were huge towers, stern and stout, all without fretwork or ornament; and there were many of these and one to help the other, all about the hill, and down by the riverside a baily such as never was a stronger or a wiser. And Sir Godrick said: “See thou, lad, those fair and beauteous buildings were the work of peace, when we sat well beloved on our own lands: it is an hundred of years ago since they were done. Then came the beginning of strife, and needs must we build yonder stark and grim towers and walls in little leisure by the labour of many hands. Now may peace come again, and give us time to cast wreaths and garlands of fretwork round the sternness of the war-walls, or let them abide and crumble in their due time. But little avails to talk of peace as now. Come thou, Red Lad, and join the host of war that dwelleth within those walls even as peaceful craftsmen and chapmen dwell in a good town. Lo thou, they fling abroad the White Hart from the topmost tower: Blow, music, and salute it.”

Then all their horns blew up, and they set forward toward the baily of the castle. And it is said indeed that five thousand men-at-arms, besides the women and other folk that waited on them, dwelt for the most part in the House of Longshaw.

So that even was high feast holden in the great hall of Longshaw, where by Osberne’s deeming all was fairer and daintier within even than without. There was the Red Lad shown to a good place and all honour done to him, and his lord looked to it that the tales of his valiancy should be known, so that all thought well of him.

There was but little doing in those months which followed the homecoming of Sir Godrick, as he was at peace with his neighbours so to say. But he made Osberne captain over a band of good men, and sent him on divers errands wherein was some little peril; and in all of these he did wisely and sped well. Amongst others he went down with ten tens of men through the Wood and right down to a certain haven on the Sundering Flood, with the errand of warding chapmen and others who were bringing many loads of wares for the service of the house. There then he beheld the great water for the first time since he had left the Dale, and wondered at its hugeness and majesty; and the sorrow of his heart stirred within him when he thought how far they two had come from the Bight of the Cloven Knoll, he and the Sundering Flood. But he had no leisure to grieve overmuch, and his grief was but as the pain of a hurt which a man feels even amidst of his deep sleep. Of those chapmen and others he asked much concerning Elfhild; and they could tell him many tales of the Red Skinners and their misdeeds, but nought that seemed to have aught to do with his love. On the way back with the train of goods, which was great and long-spun-out, a band of the waylayers laid an ambushment against it, hearing that the leader of its guard was but a young man new to war. But they were best to have left it alone, for Osberne was well aware of them; and to be short, he so ambushed the ambushers that he had them in the trap, and slew them every one: small harm it was of the death of them. Now this was the first time in his warfare that his men fell on with the name of him in their mouths, and cried, The Red Lad! the Red Lad! Terrible indeed became that cry in no very long time.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩再仔细看去，只见诸塔诸墙的正中央，是一座极大极美的大厅，尖顶尖塔玲珑可爱，一扇扇窗子宛如雕就的象牙；大厅旁边一座教堂，比它还要秀美；而在大厅前方、沿山势而下及东西两侧，则是一座座巨大的塔楼，森然坚牢，全无雕花镂饰；这样的塔楼为数众多，此呼彼应，环卫全山；沿河一带又有一道外郭，其坚固周备，更无出其右者。戈德里克爵士说：“你瞧，孩子，那些秀美华丽的建筑，是太平年月的产物，那时我们安居在自己的土地上，深受爱戴；落成至今，已有一百年了。后来争端初起，我们不得不在仓促之间役使众人，筑起那厢那些冷峻森严的塔楼墙垣。但愿太平重来，赐我们工夫，在战墙的冷峻四周绕上雕花的环饰与花环；不然，就任它们留待天命，到时候自然倾颓。只是眼下空谈太平，无济于事。来吧，红衣少年，加入住在这些墙垣之内的战斗之群，就像安分的工匠与商贾住在一座好城里一般。瞧啊，他们从最高的塔顶上展开白鹿旗了：奏乐的，吹奏起来，向它致敬。”

于是号角齐鸣，一行人向城堡的外郭进发。据说，朗肖府里大都常住着五千名带甲战士，此外还有伺候他们的妇人仆从等人。

当晚，朗肖大厅里设下盛宴；在奥斯伯恩看来，这府第里面的陈设，竟比外面还要精美雅致几分。红衣少年被让到好座位上，备受礼敬；他的主公又有意使人传扬他勇武的事迹，于是人人对他都存了好感。

戈德里克爵士还家之后的几个月里，营生不多，因为他与四邻可说相安无事。但他封奥斯伯恩做了一队好汉的队长，差他去办各色差事，其中都带些许凶险；这些事他件件办得明智，样样顺遂。其间有一回，他带领百名部下穿过林子，一直下到分离洪流边一处港湾，差事是护卫商贾仆从等人——他们正为府中之用，押着大批货载而来。在那里，他自离开谷地以来头一回重见这条大水，惊叹它的浩瀚壮伟；待想到他与这分离洪流两个，都已行到离裂丘湾何等遥远的地方，心中的哀痛便又翻涌起来。只是他无暇过分悲伤，他的哀恸，不过像人于酣睡之中仍隐隐觉出的伤处疼痛罢了。他向那些商贾等人细细探问埃尔弗希尔德的消息；他们能讲出许多红剥皮帮及其恶行的事，却没有一桩看来与他的心上人有什么相干。归途上，货队浩浩荡荡，前后拖得很长；一伙惯于伏路劫掠的匪徒听说押队之将不过是个初历战阵的年轻人，便设伏要袭货队。可他们还是莫管这闲事为妙，因为奥斯伯恩早已识破他们；长话短说，他反将设伏者设了伏，把他们一股脑儿困进圈套，杀了个干净：这些人死不足惜。这是他行军打仗以来，头一回部下们喊着他的名号冲锋陷阵，齐声高呼：“红衣少年！红衣少年！”没过多久，这一声呐喊果然变得令敌胆寒。

## XLV / 四十五

===Original===

The Red Lad Scatters the Host of the Barons

So wore the seasons into winter, and all was tidingless at Longshaw.

Long were it indeed to tell the whole tale of the warfare of the House of Longshaw, even for those years while Osberne abode with Sir Godrick. For the Knight was not only a fearless heart in the field and of all deftness in the handling of weapons, but he was also the wisest of host-leaders of his day and his land, so that with him to lead them an hundred was as good as five hundred, take one time with another. But of all this warfare must only so much be told as is needful to understand the story of Osberne and his friend of the west side of the Sundering Flood.

But first it must be said that Osberne throughout that autumn and winter spared not to question every wight whom he deemed anywise likely to have heard aught of Elfhild; and heavy and grievous became the words of his questioning, and ever his heart sickened before the answer came. But of one man he gat an answer that was not mere naysay, to wit, that months ago (and it must have been when Osberne first met Sir Godrick at Eastcheaping) he and two fellows were journeying on the other side of the Sundering Flood, but much higher up, and they came across a thrall-cheapener who said that he had a choice piece of goods if he could but get a price for it, and thereon showed them a damsel as fair as an image, and she was like to what Osberne had told of her. And then the thrall-cheapener said that he had bought her of the Red Skinners, who had borne her off from a countryside far and far away, but somewhere anigh the Sundering Flood. That man said that they bought her not of the carle, whereas the price was high and it was not much in their way of business.

===Chinese===

红衣少年击溃众男爵的大军

且说节序流转，渐入冬天，朗肖那边诸事都无消息。

朗肖之家的战事，若要从头到尾讲个详尽，委实太长，纵然单说奥斯伯恩在戈德里克爵士门下的那几年，也是如此。因为这位骑士不但是战阵上无所畏惧的勇者，操持诸般兵器无不娴熟，更是他那个时代、那片土地上最明智的统兵之将；有他率领，通扯而论，一百人可当五百人之用。但这番战事，只须讲到足以使人明白奥斯伯恩和他那位分离洪流西岸之友的故事便罢，其余只得从略。

但先须说明：那年整个秋冬，奥斯伯恩逢着任何一个他认为多少可能听到过埃尔弗希尔德消息的人，都不惮烦劳，一一盘问；他的问话日渐沉重凄楚，而每回答话未出，他的心先自惴惸欲呕。倒是有一人给了他一个并非一味回绝的答话，说的是：数月之前（那必定是奥斯伯恩初在东集遇见戈德里克爵士的时节），那人同两个伙伴在分离洪流对岸行路，地方却在上游远处；途中遇到一个贩奴商，说自己手里有一件上等货色，只愁卖得起价钱，说着便引他们看一位少女，那少女美得如画中人一般，模样正与奥斯伯恩平日所述相合。那贩奴商又说，她是从红剥皮帮手里买下的；红剥皮帮把她从很远很远的地方掳来，不过那地方总在分离洪流左近。那人说，他们终究没有向那汉子买下她，只因价钱太高，而且这也算不得他们路上的正经营生。

===Original===

Now this story was told a little after Yule, and the chapman who told it was going back again presently through the Wood and across the Flood, since the season was mild; and Osberne asked would he take him with him, in case he might hit upon anything in those parts. The chapman was nought loth, as may be deemed, to have such a doughty champion to his fellow farer; so Osberne asked leave of his lord, who would not gainsay him since nought was stirring, but bade him take three good men of his friends with him. So they went, and crossed the Flood a few days before Candlemass; and when they were on the other side they fell to asking questions at the houses of religion and of the chapmen whom they met there. Also they gat them into castles and great houses where many servants are wont to be, and not a few bought at a price; and there they used both tongue and eyes. Thus fared they a twenty days’ journey up the water, keeping ever somewhat nigh; but woe worth, if they gat them no great scathe (though they had some rough passages forsooth, which time suffereth us not to tell of), yet also they gat no good, and were no nearer to hearing a true word of Elfhild than ever.

So back comes Osberne, cast down and somewhat moody, but straightway finds tidings that drive all other things out of his head for a while. It was a little after Marymass that he comes home to Longshaw, and hears tell how war, and big war, has arisen. For the Barons who lay mostly to the east and north of Longshaw (though some help they had from the west and the south) both hated Sir Godrick sorely because he withheld them from the worst deeds of tyranny, and also, though they owed not service to the King of the Great City or the Porte thereof, yet were they somewhat under their power; at least each one of them was. These then had met together and made a great league, and had sworn the undoing of Sir Godrick and the House of Longshaw forever. And all the world knew that they were but the catspaw of the King and the City and the tyrannous Porte, though neither of them would let themselves be seen therein.

Now Godrick sends for Osberne, and talks long with him, and the end of that talk is that he sends him on the errand to go seek the hosting of them of the Barons’ League who dwelt furthest north, and to fall on them as fast and fierce as he may, so as to break up the said hosting, so that he may not have these men on his flank when he marches against the main host, which he will do with all speed. All of which he deems may be done, because he wotteth that the Barons deem of him that he will abide their coming to Longshaw, and that when they have shut him up there, they shall then have the open help of all the strength of the King and the Porte.

===Chinese===

这番话是岁末冬至节后不久说的；说这话的货郎因时令温和，不久便要仍旧穿过林子、渡过洪流回去。奥斯伯恩便问他，可肯携带自己同去，说不定能在那一带撞出些踪迹。那货郎有这般一位勇武士同行赶路，可想而知正求之不得。于是奥斯伯恩向主上告假；其时四境无事，主上也不阻他，只吩咐他从相与的好汉中带三名得力之人同行。于是他们启程，在圣烛节前几日渡过洪流；到得对岸，便在各处修道院宇里探问，又向途中所遇的货郎们打听。他们还进入城堡和大家宅第——那样的地方照例仆役众多，其中不少是花钱买来的——在那里口问眼察，两下并施。他们就这样沿河上行了二十天的路程，始终傍河不远；可是，唉！他们虽没吃什么大亏（途中确也经过几番险恶的遭遇，只是光阴不许我们细表），却也毫无所得；关于埃尔弗希尔德，依旧听不到一句确实的话，与先前无异。

奥斯伯恩回到朗肖，垂头丧气，郁郁寡欢；但转眼便有消息传来，一时把旁的事都从他头脑里赶了出去。那是圣母节后不几日，他回到朗肖，听得人传说：战事起来了，而且是大战。原来盘踞在朗肖以东、以北的那些男爵（虽说也从西面南面得了几分助力），一来恨戈德里克爵士入骨，因他拦着他们，不容行那最暴虐的恶事；二来他们虽不欠洪流之城之王或其市府的役务，却多少在那两家的权势之下——至少他们各个是如此。于是这伙人会盟结约，立誓要永远倾覆戈德里克爵士和朗肖之家。而天下无人不知：他们不过是那国王、那洪流之城和那暴虐市府的爪牙，只不过两家都不肯在这桩事里公然露面罢了。

且说戈德里克召奥斯伯恩前来，与他长谈一番；谈定的结局，是派他前去寻访男爵同盟中住家最北的那些人的兵马集结，叫他尽可能迅猛地扑将上去，把那处集结击散，免得他自己挥军进击敌军主力时，有这些人马梗在侧翼——而进击主力一节，他也就要火速施行。这一切他都料定办得到，因为他深知男爵们对他的料想：他必坐守朗肖，以待他们前来；待他们把他闭锁在城中，那时便可公开得到国王和市府全部实力的援助。

===Original===

Now Osberne heard and understood all, and the men are all ready for him, a thousand and three hundred by tale; so he makes no delay and leads them by ways unkenned so diligently that he breaks forth on them before they be duly ordered, though they be all out in the fields drawing together. Shortly to say it, his thirteen hundred men are more by a great deal than their six thousand, and they scatter them to the winds so that they can never come together again, and all their munitions of war and matters for feeding and wending are destroyed. Then turns the Red Lad and wendeth, not back again to Longshaw, but thither whereas he wots the great battle shall be, and on the very eve thereof he rideth into Sir Godrick’s camp; and such an outcry of joy there was when he bears in the taken banners and such spoil as was not over-heavy to ride with, as that no man there was of Sir Godrick’s but he knew full surely that the victory would be theirs on the morrow. As for Osberne, all men praised him, and the good Knight embraced him before all the host and the leaders thereof, and said, “Here is one shall lead you when I am slain.”

Even so it went. Of a sooth stiff was the stour, for the Barons and theirs were hardy men and of great prowess, and were three to Sir Godrick’s one. But they knew that they should not have the help they looked for, for they had seen, ere the battle was joined, those taken banners, and the others had mocked them and bade them come across to serve under such and such a banner. So it was not long ere a many of them fell a-thinking: What do we to perish here, when at our backs are those so mighty castles and strengths of ours? Let us draw away little by little and get behind our walls, and there gather force again little by little. But soon they found that they would have no such leave to depart but as broken men fleeing at all adventure, for their foemen had entered too far in to them, and had cleft their array in many places. And their banners were thrown down and their captains unheeded, and at last there was no face of them against the foe; nought but heaps of huddled men, who knew not where to turn or whom to smite at: and the overthrow might be no greater, for at noontide there was no host left that at matins had been as great and goodly an host as ever was seen in those parts.

And now was the purpose of the King and the Porte broken, and they must sit still and do nothing; nay, have got to be well content if the Small Crafts take not the occasion to rise against them. But to say sooth these knew their own opportunity and took it, as ye shall find hereafter.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩听罢，一切都了然于心；人马也已为他备齐，点检名数，共一千三百。于是他不再耽搁，率众专取人所不识的小路，兼程疾进，趁敌方虽已尽数出到田野、正在聚拢、却未整列停当之际，猝然杀出。简而言之，他那一千三百人，抵敌他们的六千人还绰绰有余，把他们杀得四散飘零，从此再不能重新聚合，他们所有的军械战具、粮草行装也都尽数毁去。于是红衣少年掉转马头，并不再回朗肖，径投他料定将有大战之地；大战前夜，他驰入戈德里克爵士的营中。他捧着夺来的旗号、带着一批乘马携带不至过重的掳获进营时，营中一时欢声雷动，戈德里克爵士麾下无一不由此确信：明日之战，胜利必属他们这一边。至于奥斯伯恩，众人交口称誉；好骑士当着全军和众首领拥抱了他，说道：“等我阵亡之后，率领你们的就是这个人。”

事情果然如此了结。说实在的，这一仗煞是惨烈：男爵们和他们的人都是惯战的健儿，武艺高强，人数又是戈德里克方面的三倍。但他们知道，盼望中的援力是得不到了，因为开战之前，他们已望见那些被夺去的旗号，对方还嘲弄他们，叫他们干脆投到某面某面旗号之下去听用。于是不多时，他们中间便有许多人心下寻思：我们何苦死在此地？我们背后不是有那些坚固的城堡寨垒么？不如一点一点抽身出去，退到自家墙垣之内，再一点一点重聚力量。但他们很快发觉，再没有这样从容退去的许可了，只能像亡命的溃兵一般没头没脑地奔逃，因为敌军已经深深楔入他们阵中，把阵列劈开了多处。旗号纷纷倒地，队长们无人理睬；到末了，他们再没有面朝敌军的一分阵势，只剩一堆一堆挤作一团的汉子，不知该转向何方、该砍杀何人：这一败再没有更彻底的了——晨祷时分还是那一带见所未见的一支最盛大雄壮的大军，到正午已一支不剩。

国王和市府的图谋就此破灭，他们只得安坐不动，无所施为；不，还得暗自庆幸，庆幸小行会没有趁机起事来反对他们。但说句实话，小行会这些人深知自家的机会，也果然把它抓住了——后文自见分晓。

===Original===

That great battle was fully foughten on the first of May, and ere a half month was fully worn the Barons’ League sent a herald to Longshaw praying for peace; but Sir Godrick straightway sent back answer that he would grant the Barons peace when they had delivered up all their strengths into his hands, then and not before. Such answer the herald bore back. But their proud stomachs had not yet come down so far, and they but sent back their defiance renewed: for they though that, though they were not strong enough to meet Longshaw in the field, yet they might hold their strengths in despite of it, and so dally out the time until the King and the Porte were strong enough to come to their help. Now was this put to the test; for straightway, when Sir Godrick had their answer, he rose up and led a host against the castle of the greatest of these Barons, and took it in ten days, after much loss of his men. Then went he against the next greatest and took that, with less pain. And meanwhile the Red Lad to the north, and another captain to the south, had the business of riding here and there and making nought of any gathering if they heard of the beginnings thereof. And this they did, with much labour and no little battle; but thoroughly they did it, so as Sir Godrick might carry on his sieges of the strongholds without let or hindrance, so that before the winter came he had all he wanted, and most of the Barons captive at Longshaw. As to the strongholds, into some he put his own men, and some he threw down.

So noble Yule they kept at Longshaw that year, with all those great men feasting at the table. But a day or two after Yule came a herald riding through the snow (for that season was hard), on behalf of the Barons’ League, what was left of it, craving for peace, and Sir Godrick said that peace they might have if they would, or not as they would, but the terms were that he should keep what he had got, but ransom his captives duly; or else they might dwell at Longshaw all their lives long if they would. Now there was no help for it but such terms they must take, and be glad that it was no worse.

So peace was made, and all was quiet till after Marymass. Osberne had somewhat of a mind to get him into the Wood, and seek through the strengths and other houses that were scattered about in the Wood itself, and the edge thereof toward the Sundering Flood; but partly he was sick at heart of forever asking questions to which came evermore but one answer, and partly there was very much work come to his hand that he might scarce turn over to another, of visiting the captured strongholds, and seeing to the men-at-arms therein and their captains, and suchlike matters; for now he was closer to the rede and mind of Sir Godrick than any other.

===Chinese===

那场大战在五月一日打完。未及半月过尽，男爵同盟便遣一名传令官到朗肖乞和；戈德里克爵士却即刻回话：须待男爵们把所有堡寨尽数交到他手中，那时方许言和，此前断无商量。传令官把这样的回话带回。但他们的骄横之气尚未低伏到这般地步，只是回书重新下了一道战书：他们心想，虽然兵力已不足以在旷野与朗肖对阵，却还守得住自家的堡寨，与之顽抗，如此迁延时日，直到国王和市府强大到足以赶来相救。这番盘算随即便受了检验：戈德里克爵士一得他们的回音，立即起兵，直捣这些男爵中最强大者的城堡，苦战折损了不少部众之后，十日之内将它攻下。接着又进兵次强的一家，费了较少的气力，也把它拿下。与此同时，红衣少年在北面，另一位队长在南面，专管四下巡驰，一听闻何处有人马开始啸聚，便驰往将其扑灭。这差事他们办得备尝劳苦，大小恶战也着实打了几场；但办得干净彻底，好教戈德里克爵士得以一无阻挠地围攻各处坚垒；以致冬来之前，他要的都到手了，大半男爵都成了朗肖的阶下之囚。至于那些坚垒，有的他派了自己的人进驻，有的索性夷平拆毁。

那一年，他们在朗肖过了一个极尽隆重的岁末冬至节，众大人物都欢宴于席上。但节后一两日，一名传令官踏雪而来（那年冬令严酷），代表男爵同盟——或者说它的残余——乞求和平。戈德里克爵士说，和与不和，一凭他们自愿；只是条件是：他已到手的尽归他有，俘虏们则须依礼缴纳赎金，方可放还；再不然，他们若情愿，尽可在朗肖住到老死。事已至此，无可奈何，这样的条件他们只得领受，还得庆幸结局不比这更糟。

于是和议告成，四境清静，一直到圣母节之后。奥斯伯恩本有些意思要到无主林里去走一遭，把散在林子腹地、以及林子朝分离洪流一边边沿的各处堡寨和别的宅院都搜访一遍；但一来他心灰意懒——问来问去，永远只得同一个回答；二来也实在有太多事务落到他手上，几乎无从转托他人：巡视那些攻克的坚垒，点验其中的带甲兵士和他们的队长，以及诸如此类的事项。因为如今在戈德里克爵士的谋议与心意跟前，再没有谁比他更亲近的了。

## XLVI / 四十六

===Original===

Osberne Enters the City of the Sundering Flood

So, as aforesaid, the time wore till Marymass was over, and then came fresh tidings, to wit that the men of the Small Crafts and the lesser commons were risen against the Porte and the King, and had gotten to them the North Gate of the City, and were holding it against their foemen, together with that quarter of the city which lay round about it. The news hereof was sure, for it was brought to Longshaw one night by three of the weavers who had ridden on the spur to tell it to Sir Godrick, and these three men he knew well, and that they were trusty.

Now so it was both that it had not been easy at any time that war should find Longshaw not duly prepared, and also that at this time there was no tidings which Sir Godrick looked for more than this. Speedy therefore was his rede. For he gave into Osberne’s hands fifteen hundreds of his best men, and bade him ride to the City and the North Gate and see what the fields without the City looked like; and the very next morning the Red Lad and his rode out of Longshaw, having with them two of the said weaver-carles, but the third abode with Sir Godrick.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩进入洪流之城

且说，如前文所言，日子挨到圣母节过后，便又传来了新的消息，道是小行会的工匠与下层平民起事，反抗市府与国王，已把城中的北门夺到手里，连同北门四周那一坊市区，一并据守着抵御他们的敌人。这消息确凿无疑，因为一天夜里，有三名织工快马加鞭赶到朗肖，把它报知戈德里克爵士；这三人他都相熟，深知他们可靠。

原来事情恰是如此：一来，无论何时，要教战争寻见朗肖不曾妥为戒备，原不是件容易的事；二来，这时戈德里克爵士所盼的消息，再没有比这更切的了。因此他的方略定得极快。他把一千五百名精兵交到奥斯伯恩手里，命他驰往那城与北门，看看城外的原野是何等光景；次日一早，红衣少年便率部骑出朗肖，带着那几名织工汉子中的两个，第三个则留在戈德里克爵士身边。


===Original===

Now so good were the Red Lad’s wayleaders and knew all the passages and roads so inly, and so diligent was the Red Lad himself and his men so good and trusty, that by the second day about sunset he was but five miles from the North Gate, and he and his covered by some scattering woodland that lay thereabout.

Straightway Osberne sends a half score of spies to get them to the City and see what was toward, and come back, they that were not slain, and tell him thereof. Straightway they went, and had such hap that all they came back unscathed, and this was their story: That the men of the Small Crafts were not by seeming hard pressed, for still their banners hung out of the North Gate and the wall and towers thereabout; but that both within the City had been bitter battle against them all day long, and also a host of men of their foes had come out from the East Gate, and were now lying round the North Gate in no very good order, because they looked for no peril save from them within the North Gate, and deemed that as for them they had enough on their hands to keep them within their walls, and least of all things did they look for any onfall from without.

Thereon the Red Lad called to him his captains and host-leaders and asked them of rede, and to be short therewith. Some said one thing, some another, as to send back news hereof to Sir Godrick, or to array them in the best wise to fall on these men on the morrow. Nay, some were for hanging about till they should have news of Sir Godrick.

===Chinese===

红衣少年的引路人这般得力，对条条小径大道无不烂熟于心；红衣少年本人又这般勤勉，他的人马又这般精良可靠，因此到第二天日落时分，他离北门已不过五英里，一行人便借当地的一片疏林掩蔽了身形。

奥斯伯恩随即派出十名斥候，命他们潜进那城，探看动静，凡不曾被杀的，都回来报他。他们即刻去了，又得了好运，一个个毫发无伤地回来，所报如下：小行会的人看去并未被逼得很紧，因为他们的旗帜依旧悬在北门外和那一带的城墙塔楼上；只是城内整日恶战不断，矛头正对着他们；此外还有一大队敌人从东门出城，此刻正散乱地屯在北门四周，因为他们只道危险来自北门里的人，又想那些人光是困守墙内，已自顾不暇，万万料不到会有人从外面杀来。

于是红衣少年把手下的众队长与领兵头目召到跟前，问他们的方略，并要他们说简短些。有说这个的，有说那个的：或主张把这消息回报戈德里克爵士，或主张好生整顿阵伍，明日便向这些人扑杀。甚至还有人主张原地盘桓，等得了戈德里克爵士的消息再动。


===Original===

But when they were done, spake the Red Lad: “Sirs, many of these things are good to be done, and some not; for sure am I that we be not sent hither to do nothing. But now if ye will, hearken my rede: it is now well-nigh dark, and in two hours or somewhat more it will be pit-mirk, and these men outside the walls will be going to their rest with no watch and ward set outward toward the upland. Wherefore I say, let us leave our horses here and do off so much of our armour as we may go afoot lightly; for if we win we shall soon get other horses and gear, and if we lose, we shall need them not. But meseemeth if we do deftly and swiftly, all these men we shall have at our will.”

Now they all saw that it would do; so there was no more said, but they fell to arraying their men on foot, and in an hour they were on the way; and going wisely and with little noise, in two hours thence they were amidst the foe and doing their will upon them; and when they were well entered in amongst them and had slain many, they fell to the blowing of horns and crying out, The Red Lad! the Red Lad! Longshaw for the Small Crafts! Then both there was no aid to come to the men of the Porte, whereas they were far away from the East Gate, and also they of the North Gate heard the horns and the cries, and guessed what was toward; so they issued out with torches and cressets and fell upon the foe crying their cries, and so it befell that none of that host of the Porte escaped save they who might make the night their cloak. Then was the gate thrown open, and the Red Lad and his entered, and ye may think whether the townsmen were joyous and made much of them. But when the tale of his men was told, Osberne found that but three of his were missing. And so soon as it was light, he sent back a band of his men to bring on their horses and armour. Thuswise first came Osberne to the City of the Sundering Flood.

===Chinese===

待众人说罢，红衣少年开口道：“诸位，这些主意有许多是可行的，也有一些不然；因为我确知，我们不是被派到此地来无所事事的。如今你们若愿意，且听我的方略：眼下天色将黑，再过两个钟头或略多一些，便要黑得伸手不见五指；城外这些人将要安歇，朝着高地那一面并不设一兵一卒的岗哨。因此我说，我们且把马匹留在此地，卸下身上碍事的甲胄，好教步下走得轻快；因为若得胜，我们很快便会有别的马匹和装备；若战败，也用不着它们了。依我看来，只要我们做得干净迅捷，这些人尽可由我们摆布。”

众人都看出此计可行，于是再无多话，各自把人马列为步队；一个钟头后，他们便已上路。他们走得机敏，动静极小，又过两个钟头，便已杀入敌群之中，任意施为；待深深突入敌阵、杀伤众多之后，他们便吹响号角，高声呐喊：“红衣少年！红衣少年！朗肖为小行会！”这一来，市府的人马既远离东门，无从往援；北门里的人又听得号角与呐喊，猜出了外间的情由，便举着火炬与篝灯呐喊杀出，扑向敌人。于是事情的结果是：市府那支人马无一逃脱，除非有谁能拿黑夜当遮身的斗篷。随后城门大开，红衣少年率众入城；城中百姓如何欢欣、如何厚待他们，不消说得。及至清点人马，奥斯伯恩发现自己部下只少了三名。天刚放亮，他便遣一队人回去接应马匹甲胄。奥斯伯恩就这样头一遭来到了洪流之城。

## XLVII / 四十七

===Original===

The Battle in the Square

On the morrow’s morn, the leaders of the town met Osberne and his captains in council, and their rede was that they should do warily and not throw the helve after the hatchet. This they deemed best, that they should now, while they might, make strong with mound and wall their quarter of the town, since, until Sir Godrick was come to them, they might even now look to it to have much might against them. This reded the Red Lad nowise gainsaid, knowing well how valiant and stout these men would be behind walls; but he said: “Yet, my masters, the more leisure you have for this spade and mattock work, the better it shall be for you and the work. Wherefore my rede is that some of your chosen men go with the best of mine, and that we issue out of our quarter and fall upon the others, and make a good space clear of foes of the streets and carfaxes that march unto your quarter, which forsooth shall serve you as an outwork to your castle until Sir Godrick comes with a great host and fills up all that and more. And, sooth to say, now at once is the best time to do this, while the foe is all astonied at what befell last night.”

That seemed good to one and all; so when they had eaten and were duly arrayed they issued forth into the streets, and at first indeed wended those that were truly of their quarter, only on the day before they deemed them not big enough to hold all that; but now it was their mind to bring it within their defences. So the Red Lad and his rode on warily, taking heed that they should not be cut off by any at their backs. So at last they came unto a great carfax with a wide square round about it. There they drew up their folk in a long line with a wide face to the foe, well furnished of bows and other shot-weapons; for the townsmen were archers exceeding good.

===Chinese===

广场之战

次日清晨，城中的首领们与奥斯伯恩及其众队长聚议军情，众人的计议是：行事务必谨慎，切莫丢了斧头，又把斧柄也搭上去。他们认为上策乃是：趁眼下还来得及，用土垒与墙垣把城中自己那一坊筑得坚固；因为在戈德里克爵士来到他们这里之前，他们随时都得提防有大股兵力压到自己头上。对这条方略，红衣少年绝无异议，因为他深知这些人凭墙据守时会如何骁勇坚劲；但他开口说道：“然而，诸位头领，这等锹镐挖掘的活计，你们手头越是宽裕，于你们自身、于这活计都越好。因此我的方略是：你们拣选一些精壮，同我最得力的弟兄一道，杀出我们这一坊，去扑杀其他几方的人，把通连你们那一坊的街巷和十字街口，清出好大一片没有敌人的地面；在戈德里克爵士率大军前来、把那一片以及更多地方统统填满之前，这片地面确乎可充你们这座堡寨的外围工事。而且说句实话，眼下正是动手的最好时机，因为敌人昨夜遭了那场变故，此刻还惊魂未定呢。”

这主意人人称善。于是众人用过饭食，装束齐整，便开出街去。一开初所走的，确实还都是本坊的街巷；只不过前一日他们还嫌这一坊不够大，容不下这一切；如今却存心要把这一带统统圈进自家防线之内。红衣少年一行于是谨慎骑行，时时提防有人从背后截断归路。末了，他们来到一处大十字街口，四周环着一片宽阔的广场。他们便在那里把人马列成一条长阵，宽宽的正面朝向敌人，弓弩和其他射远的家伙一应齐备；因为城中的百姓都是顶好的弓手。


===Original===

There was nought in the square or on the carfax at first but themselves; but after a little there entered by the east way and the west a rout of archers, and fell to shooting at Osberne’s, and they back again. The archers of the Porte did not dare to show much face to the Red Lad, but were gathered together in plumps at each incoming into the square. Said the Red Lad to himself: Let us make an end of this folly. And he bade his men leave shooting, and then gave the word, and they rode at the carles right and left with spear and sword. Straightway the archers ran all they might, yet not so fast but that the Red Lad and his captains got amidst them ere they could take to the narrow byways, so that a many were slain. And this was a matter of but ten minutes. But when the horsemen had been along with the bowmen a little while, they heard great horns blowing from the south, and therewith great noise of horse, and presently a great rout of men-at-arms in the best of armour began to come in by the southern road, and the Red Lad’s men were all agog to fall on them straightway, but he made them forbear till they had filled the square overfull. They were not long about it, but meanwhile the townsmen shot all they might; and so nigh they were that, despite their armour, not a few fell, both of men and horses; yet did they fall not on till the square was full of them, so that it looked far bigger than might have been deemed. Then they thrust on, but so close that they might scarce handle their arms, and the Red Lad and his cried their cry, The Red Lad for Longshaw! and rushed forward, smiting and thrusting, till the front of the foemen began to try to turn about if they might; but scarce they could, though if they might not flee they might not fall. And they behind strove to get forward to smite, for they said they were many more than the others; but they could get but little done, for their forward men who had been overthrown were hindering them. Now also the carles of the town laid aside their bows and entered among them with short swords and axes, and hewed and slew and took none to mercy, and it seemed hard to know how that would end, save by all those men-at-arms falling in the place.

Now, as ye may deem, Osberne was more thrust forward than any other, and somewhat of a space he had cleared before him, and his yellow hair came down from under his basnet, and his long red surcoat streamed all rent and tattered in the wind, and Boardcleaver was bare and bloody in his fist, and his face was stern but not exceeding fierce; for he would the slaughter of the day were over. Now he hove up Boardcleaver, and before him was a tall man in gilded armour and a gay yellow surcoat of silk, and his armour was little rent and his sword unscathed in his hand; a stark man he was of aspect, but terror was come into his soul because of the slaughter of the press and that there was no escape therefrom. So when he saw Boardcleaver arising he cried out, “O Red Lad, Red Lad, O thou seeker, let me live, that I may tell thee what thou wouldst give many lives to know!”

Then Osberne restrained Boardcleaver and let him fall to his wrist, and stretched out his hand to the gilded man. But even therewith his hand was thrust aside, for many a man there was mad and drunk with the slaying: and a short, dark, long-armed man of the weavers’ craft, armed with nought else save a heavy short-sword cutting on the inner edge, drew him on to the gilded man’s horse, and brought his sword backhanded across his face and neck, and fell with him as he fell, and mangled him that he was more than dead, and then got up again amidst the horses and fell to work again. Then Osberne, when he saw the tale was done, groaned aloud; but none heeded him, for it was to them but as a cry of the wounded. Then he uphove Boardcleaver again and cried out shrilly: “The Red Lad, the Red Lad for Longshaw and the Crafts! On, on at them!” And that all heard, both his and theirs. And now they of the foemen began to cease pressing forward, and many fled without a stroke stricken, till there was somewhat more room for the rest to flee, but little leave, for even so was more room for the pursuers, and soon was the square clear of all but dead and sore hurt; and the chase endured all up and along the carfax, and mad-fierce it was, and that mostly at the hands of the townsmen, who deemed that they had much to pay back to the men of the King and the Porte.

===Chinese===

起初，广场上、街口处除了他们自己，空无一人；可是不多时，便有一股股弓手从东西两面涌入，朝奥斯伯恩的人放起箭来，这边也回射过去。市府的弓手不敢在红衣少年面前露多少脸面，只在通入广场的各个路口，一丛一丛挤作堆儿。红衣少年心中自语：这等蠢事，我们来给它收场罢。他便命部下停止放箭，随即一声号令，众人挺矛举剑，向左右两边的汉子们驰杀过去。弓手们撒腿便逃，却终究不够快，红衣少年和他的众队长赶在他们钻进窄巷之前便已杀入队中，因此结果了不少人。而这前后不过十分钟光景。骑兵们与弓手周旋了不多时，便听得南面大号角齐鸣，接着是浩大的马蹄之声，不久，一大股甲仗精良的甲士开始从南路开进广场。红衣少年的人马个个跃跃欲试，要立时扑上去，他却教众人按捺不动，直等他们把广场挤得满满当当。他们填广场倒没费多久；这其间城中百姓拼命放箭，因双方相距甚近，任凭甲胄在身，倒下的仍复不少，人马皆有；可他们偏要等广场塞满了人，方才挥军来攻，于是那广场望去竟比人们所能料想的大出许多。随后他们挤压向前，却挤得几乎施展不开兵器；红衣少年一行高呼他们的战号：“红衣少年为朗肖！”便冲杀上前，挥砍刺击，直杀得敌阵前锋想法掉头；却几乎掉转不得——既逃不出去，便连倒也倒不下去。后面的人还拼命向前挤着要砍杀，自恃人多势众；却收效甚微，因为前面被砍翻的自家人正挡着他们的道。这时，城中的汉子们也撂下弓，提着短剑板斧杀入阵中，砍的砍，剁的剁，不留半分怜悯；看那光景，这事除却那些甲士尽数葬身原地，已难有别个了局。

如今，如你们所能想见，奥斯伯恩冲得比谁都靠前，身前已清出了一小片空地；他的金发从战盔底下披散下来，那件长长的红罩袍撕裂褴褛，在风中翻卷；断板剑裸着锋刃、血渍斑斑，握在他的拳头里；他面容严峻，却不十分狰狞，因为他但愿这一日的杀伐早些完结。这时他举起断板剑，只见面前立着一个高大的汉子，身披镀金的甲胄，外罩一领鲜亮的黄绸罩袍；那人甲胄少有破损，手中长剑也完好无损；论相貌原是个凛然剽悍之人，无奈这一场人堆里的屠戮、又兼身陷其中无从脱逃，早教他魂胆俱丧。他见断板剑扬起，便高声叫道：“红衣少年啊，红衣少年，你这苦苦寻索的人啊，饶我一条性命，我好告诉你一件你拿几条命去换也甘愿的事！”

奥斯伯恩于是收住断板剑，任它垂落腕边，向那镀金甲的汉子伸出手去。可恰在此时，他的手却被人拨开了，因为场中多少人早已杀得如痴如狂：一个织工行会的汉子，身材矮黑，长臂膀，除一柄沉甸甸、刃口开在内侧的短剑外别无兵器，抢上前将那镀金甲的汉子拖下马来，反手一剑横斩过他的脸和脖颈，又随着他一同栽倒，把他剁得死上加死，随后从群马之间爬起，接着又杀上前去。奥斯伯恩见这事已成了定局，不由出声呻吟；却无人理会他，因为在众人听来，那不过是伤者的哀号罢了。随后他再度高举断板剑，尖声高呼：“红衣少年，红衣少年为朗肖与小行会！上啊，向他们杀啊！”这一声，敌我双方全都听见了。于是敌军渐渐不再向前挤压，许多人一刀未交便逃了；余下的人固然多了些逃窜的空当，却难得逃出性命，因为追杀的人也同样多了空当。不多时，广场上除了死者和重伤之人，已空无一人；追杀沿着十字街口一路上下延续，凶狂已极，动手的多半是城中百姓——他们自道欠下国王和市府人马的旧账，正有许多要清算。


===Original===

Now after this Osberne and his drew not back from the carfax, but by the rede of him the townsmen made trenches and walls to strengthen them right up to the said carfax. And for three days the King’s men durst not fall upon them there, save that they tried a little arrow-shot from afar, but did not much hurt thereby.

But the next day thereafter comes Sir Godrick with his host to the help of the townsmen, and rides into the North Gate amidst the joy of all men. And the next day they push on to their outworks and fall on. Three days of battle they have thereafter, wherein Sir Godrick will not suffer the Red Lad to deal: “For,” saith he, “it is thou that hath won, and now we have little to do, but as it were the woodwright’s and the carpenter’s work. Wherefore now I bid thee to rest.” Laughed Osberne, and tarried in the North quarter, while Sir Godrick and his with all deliberation set to work on clearing the quarters on that side of the river; and they were four days about the business albeit the men of the Porte and the King were scarce so stubborn and enduring as they looked to find them.

But Osberne did all he might to keep good order and good heart amongst his men, and they made their strongholds strong to the letter, and looked to it that all their forward places should be ready for battle at a moment’s notice.

===Chinese===

这之后，奥斯伯恩一行不曾从十字街口后退一步；依照他的方略，城中百姓挖壕筑墙，把防线一路加固到那处街口。整整三天，国王的人马不敢前来攻打，只从远处放箭稍稍试探，却没造成多大伤害。

可是再下一天，戈德里克爵士便率大军来援城中百姓，在万众欢腾之中驰入北门。次日，他们便推进到外围工事，发起攻击。此后又打了三天，这三天里，戈德里克爵士说什么也不让红衣少年出手：“因为，”他说道，“功业已经是你立下的了，如今我们没多少事可做，不过是细木匠和木匠的活计罢了。因此如今我吩咐你歇息去。”奥斯伯恩哈哈一笑，便留在城北那一坊歇着；戈德里克爵士则率众从容不迫地动手清剿河那一边的各坊市区。这事他们费了四天工夫，尽管市府与国王的人马远不如他们预想的那般顽韧耐战。

而奥斯伯恩则竭尽全力，使部下之间保持严整的军纪与旺盛的士气；众人把各处壁垒修得坚固无比、丝毫不苟，并且留心做到：所有前沿阵地，一闻号令，顷刻间便可投入战斗。

## XLVIII / 四十八

===Original===

Sir Godrick Is Chosen Burgreve of the City
But on the third of those four days came a man to Osberne early in the morning, and told him that the foe were holding the East Gate somewhat heedlessly, and that they had lost many in those last battles. Wherefore Osberne looked to it, and gat three hundreds of picked men, and passing through byways of the streets came to the townward end of the said gate but a little after sunrise, and without more ado made at the doors of the gate, which were but half shut. There they drave the few guards in, and followed on them pell-mell; and to make a long story short, they presently won the gate utterly with but little loss, and all those inside, who were scarce three hundreds, slain or taken. Now you may judge if this were good news for Sir Godrick, when with mickle labour and not a little loss he had won the town on the east side of the Sundering Flood.
But now, when they had won so much, they had yet to carry the war into the west side of the Flood, where was forsooth the chief strength of the King and the Porte. For there was the King’s palace and the great gildhall, both whereof were buildings defensible, and moreover they had full command of all the haven and the ships therein, for they had all the quays and landing-places and warehouses; so that both the sea and the river was under their wielding. Two bridges, made of great barges linked together, crossed the Flood, one near to the haven, the other a good way higher up; nor had the King and his thought it good to break either of them down. Both had fair and great castles to guard them at either side.
So now when Sir Godrick and the Council of the Lesser Crafts had met in divers motes with Osberne and other captains of the Longshaw host, it yet seemed a great matter that they had to deal with; and that if they had won many victories, they had yet to win the great one. And all men saw what would have befallen if the Barons’ League had not been so utterly broken up the year before. But now the greatest gain which Sir Godrick and the Lesser Crafts had was that they by no means lacked men, and those of the best; and though they were shut out from chaffer with the merchants of the City, yet whereas the whole countryside was open to them because of the riders of Longshaw, they were not like to fall short of victuals. Though true it is that the King’s men set swift keels on the Sundering Flood stuffed of men-at-arms, and these would land on the eastern bank so far as a twenty or thirty miles up, and plunder and ravage the country-folk, or whiles would come upon trains of victuals and suchlike wending towards the eastern city; and many fierce deeds they did, which made them no better beloved, so that men got to saying that the King’s men were but little better than the very Skinners themselves. Moreover, it is not to be said but that often these reivers and lifters were met by the riders of Longshaw or the weaponed men of the countryside, and put to the worse by them, and such as were taken at these times had nought for it save the noose on the tree.

===Chinese===

戈德里克爵士当选城守
且说到了那四天中的第三天，一清早便有人来见奥斯伯恩，禀说敌人守东门守得颇为大意，又道他们经前几番恶战，折损已多。于是奥斯伯恩留了心，集结起三百名精兵，穿街走巷，专拣僻静小路，日出才不多时，便到了那座门朝向城里的一头，当下更不多话，径直扑向城门的门扇——那门扇不过半掩着。他们把寥寥几名守兵往门里驱赶，乱哄哄随后掩杀；长话短说，不多时他们便以区区损失将那门尽数夺下，门内人马本不足三百，非死即俘。你想，戈德里克爵士费了偌大气力、又折损了不少人，方才夺得分离洪流东岸的这座城，此刻得了这等消息，其喜何如，自不消说得。
然而他们虽赢得这么多，还须把战事打到洪流的西岸去；国王与市府的主力，实实在在都屯在那一头。因为国王的宫室与那座大行会堂都在西岸，两处皆是堪资据守的建筑；况且港湾连同湾中的船只也全归他们号令，因为码头、泊岸与货栈尽在他们手里；如此一来，海上河上，都操于其掌中。横跨洪流的有两座桥，都以大驳船连缀而成，一座靠近港湾，另一座在再往上去颇远的地方；国王与他的一党也不曾觉得拆毁哪一座为好。两座桥的两岸桥头，都各有堂皇坚固的城堡环卫。
于是，戈德里克爵士与小行会的议事会屡屡集会，同奥斯伯恩及朗肖军中其他队长们会商过后，仍觉得摆在面前的是一桩大事；他们虽已赢得许多场胜利，那最大的一场却还在后头。众人也都看得明白，倘若男爵同盟不曾在前一年被摧散得那样彻底，眼下更不知要落到何种地步。不过此时，戈德里克爵士与小行会所享的最大一桩便宜，是兵力绝不匮乏，且尽是上上之选；他们虽被隔绝了与城中商贾的买卖，但既有朗肖的骑队纵横四野、整个乡野都向他们敞开，粮秣是不愁短绌的。只是实情也有另一面：国王的人把满载甲士的快船放上分离洪流，这等船会在远至上游二三十英里的东岸靠岸，掳掠荼毒乡野之民，有时还撞上运往东岸城中的粮队辎重之类，便径行劫夺；他们干下许多凶暴的勾当，愈发不得人望，以致人们渐渐传开一句话：国王的人比那剥皮帮自己也差不了多少。再者，这些抄掠抢掳之徒也常常迎头撞上朗肖的骑队或乡间持兵的丁壮，被他们杀败；这等时候被拿住的，除了树上那一圈绞索，再没有别的下场。

===Original===

Thus then these two hosts looked across the Sundering Flood on each other; and surely, unless the Craftsmen had been valiant and stubborn beyond most, they had lost heart, whereas war was not their mystery. Skirmishes there were a many. Whiles Sir Godrick would gather such boats and barges as they had, and thrust over into the haven, and lay hold of some good ship and strive to have her over to their side. Whiles they might do nought therein, and whiles they prevailed; but even then the King’s men contrived to set fire aboard the craft and spoil their play. Again, from time to time the King’s men would set certain ships and barges across the Flood, and strive to land and skirmish on the east side. But herein they but seldom gained aught, but they in turn would have their ships burned and their men slain or taken. Thus then it went on, and now one now the other came to their above; but neither might make an end of it.
At last, on a day when September was well worn, the King’s folk came to the midmost of the upper bridge with a white shield held aloft and a herald, and craved safe conduct for three of theirs, an old knight to wit, and two aldermen of the Porte; this was granted, and they came all to the North Gate, and the council-chamber of the Lesser Crafts therein. There they set forth their errand, which was in short that they would have peace if it might be had on such terms as were better than war and destruction. The men of the Small Crafts took their errand well, and asked them how long they might tarry, so that they might bear back conditions of peace. The messengers said that they were not looked for back that day, and the others said that by the next day at noon they would be all ready to send three of theirs back across the water with the terms of peace. Then were the messengers handed over to the guest-masters and made much of, and the masters of the Crafts fell to close council with Sir Godrick and his captains.
Now whatever other terms they bade need not be told, but the heart of the matter was this: First that so many of the masters of the Small Crafts should sit on the Great Council of the City, and that enough of them to make them of due weight in the Council. This they doubted not to gain since the war had gone with them. But the other was a harder matter, to wit, that a Burgreve should be appointed to govern the City, and that he should be of might to hold a good guard, and eke it at his will and the will of the Great Council; the said Burgreve to be chosen by all the Gilds of Craft, voting one with another, and not by the Great Council; which, as things went, would give the naming of him into the hands of the Lesser Crafts, who were more than the great ones, though far less rich and mighty. This indeed seemed like to be hard to swallow, whereas it was much like putting the King out of his place. Yet some said that belike by this time the Porte was grown mightier than the King, and if they would have it so, then would he have to give way. Herein they were doubtless right; but another thing had happened of which they knew nought, which was driving the King and Porte both toward peace, to wit that a king from oversea had sent heralds defying the King, and that his host was to be looked for in no long while, and the King and the Porte both knew that they might make no head against him, so divided as they of the City then were. Wherefore when on the next day the three King’s men bore back the terms of peace, they tarried by a little while, and came back in two hours with safe conduct for as many as Sir Godrick and the Small Crafts would send. Whereon Sir Godrick and two of the Crafts were chosen, and went back across the water straightway, and without any tarrying fell to council with the King and the Porte. There they soon found what had befallen, and that their matter was like to be carried through with a wet finger, for the others were in hot haste both to make peace and to get the swords of Longshaw on their side against the Outland men. Nor did they gainsay any one condition on which the Small Crafts had put forward, but added only this one thing, that the host of Longshaw should join with them in defending the City against the Outland men. Hereto Sir Godrick accorded well, for he had no mind that all his battle for the Small Crafts of the City should have been of no avail, as it would be if Outlanders were to conquer the city and play the tyrant there.
The very next day then was peace signed and sealed on the terms abovesaid. And three days thereafter the Porte and the Crafts went about the choosing of the Burgreve. As none doubted it would be, Sir Godrick was chosen, and, which had scarce been looked for, none else was named; both big crafts and little would have none but he.

===Chinese===

于是这两支大军便这样隔着分离洪流面面相望；说实在的，若不是众工匠勇毅坚忍远过常人，早该灰了心——毕竟战争原非他们的本行。零星的接战却是不计其数。有时戈德里克爵士会把手头所有的船只驳艇聚拢起来，一鼓作气杀入港湾，夺下某条好船，竭力把它弄到自己这一边来。有时他们在此一事无成，有时也能得手；可即便得手，国王的人也会设法在船上放起火来，坏了他们的好事。反过来，国王的人也不时把船只驳艇摆渡过洪流，想在东岸登岸厮杀一场。但在这上头他们很少占到便宜，倒往往轮到他们的船被烧、人被杀被俘。事情便这样僵持下去，时而这方占先，时而那方占先；谁也奈何不得谁。
末了，到了九月已过大半的一天，国王那边的人来到上桥正中，高举着一面白盾，带着一名传令官，请求许他们三人安全通行——便是一位老骑士，和市府的两名参事；这一请求得了允准，于是一行人来到北门，进入门内小行会的议事厅。在那里他们陈明来意，简而言之：只要和平的条件好过战争与毁灭，他们便愿意言和。小行会的人善意接纳了这一来意，问使者们可停留多久，好教他们把和平条款带回。使者们说，对方并不指望他们当日回去；主人这边便说，到次日正午，他们自会一切备妥，也遣三人携和平条款渡水而回。于是使者们被交托给宾客总管，受了盛情款待；众行会的师傅们便与戈德里克爵士及他的众队长闭门密议起来。
至于他们提出的其余条款，无须细表；事情的要旨是这样：其一，小行会的师傅们应有若干人入坐城中大议事会，且人数须足以使他们在会中占得应有的分量。这一条他们自知稳能到手，因为战局一直站在他们这边。其二却是桩难事：须委任一位城守治理全城，此人须有权豢养一支得力的卫队，并可依其本人与大议事会的意思加以扩充；这位城守由各行会不分彼此一体投票选出，而不由大议事会指定——照眼下情势，这便把提名之权送进了小行会手里，因为他们人数多于大行会，只是财势远远不及。这一条看来着实难以下咽，因为它几乎等于把国王从他的位子上撵了出去。但也有人说，多半到这时节，市府的势力已比国王更强；倘若市府定要如此，国王便不得不让步。这话无疑说对了；只是另有一桩他们全然不知的事，正把国王与市府一齐推向和平：原来一位海外之王已遣传令官前来向国王挑战，其大军不日可至；国王与市府都心中有数，以城中彼时那般四分五裂的光景，决计无力与外邦之敌抗衡。因此，次日那三名国王的人携和平条款回去后，只稍待了片刻，两个钟头之内便带着安全通行之证回来，凡戈德里克爵士与小行会要遣多少人过去，一概听凭尊便。于是选出戈德里克爵士与两名行会中人，随即渡水而回，一刻也不耽搁，与国王和市府开议。到了那里，他们很快便知晓了底蕴，明白此事唾手可成：对方急如星火，既要求和，又想把朗肖的刀兵拉到自己一边，共御外邦之敌。小行会提出的条件，对方一条也不曾驳回，只增添了一项：朗肖的大军须与他们合力卫城，抵御外邦之敌。这一项戈德里克爵士欣然应允，因为他断不愿自己为城中众小行会打的这一场仗全然白费——倘若外邦人攻陷此城、在那里作威作福，那一切便真要白费了。
就在第二天，和约便依上述条款签署用印。三天之后，市府与各行会着手推选城守。果如众人所料，戈德里克爵士当选；而颇出人意料的是，竟无一人另被提名——大行会也罢，小行会也罢，都非他莫属。

## XLIX / 四十九

===Original===

Of the City King and the Outland King

Now then was great feast and glee in the City of the Sundering Flood. The gates were thrown open, the bridges made free, the country-folk flocked in, and the markets were thronged and gay; neighbour held merry converse with neighbour, and there was marrying and giving in marriage. Of the Outland foes none thought, save it were the King and one or two of his councillors; for all men trusted in Sir Godrick that he would look to the safeguarding of the city. But as for Sir Godrick, like a wise man of war he set to work looking to all points of defence, both the castles of the town and especially the ships in the haven, that they were as defensible as might be.

And after all the Outland king came not at all that year, whereas he had fallen sick when he was just at point to take ship with his host; so that all was put off till the next spring, and there was time and to spare for Sir Godrick to do all he would strengthening the defences of the city. But none the more for that was he sluggish, but did so much that he made the City of the Sundering Flood exceeding strong, so that it might scarce be stronger; and all things flourished there: old foes became new friends, and all men were well content, save it were the King and his faitours, who rued it now that they had sold themselves so cheap.

Amidst all this, Osberne was somewhat more at Longshaw and the borders of the Wood Masterless than in the city. Of numberless folk did he ask his old questions, and gat ever the same answer, that they knew nought of it; and indeed now it was less and less like that they should know aught as time wore. So that at last he began to get ungleeful at whiles, and few-spoken with men.

===Chinese===

论洪流之城之王与外邦之王

这时候，洪流之城里大开筵宴，欢腾一片。城门尽数敞开，桥路任人往来，乡民蜂拥入城，市集上熙熙攘攘，满目喜气；邻里相与笑谈，嫁娶之事不绝。至于外邦之敌，除了国王和他的一两名谋臣之外，谁也不放在心上；因为人人信托戈德里克爵士，信他必会照管城池的守御。至于戈德里克爵士本人，则像深谙兵机的智者一般，着手查察各处守御要点：城中诸堡固不待言，尤其是港湾里的船只，务使它们尽可能经得起攻打。

到头来，那位外邦之王这一年终究没有来：原来他正要携大军登船之际，忽然害起病来；于是诸事都展延到次年春天，戈德里克爵士便有的是宽裕工夫，凡是他想做的加固城防之事，尽可从容办妥。但他并不因此懈怠半分，反倒大加经营，竟把洪流之城造就得异常坚固，坚固得几乎无以复加；城中百事兴隆：旧日的仇雠化作新交的朋友，人人皆大欢喜——只除开国王和他那一班佞幸，此刻追悔莫及，悔的是当初把自己卖得太贱了。

在这一片热闹之中，奥斯伯恩倒有多半时光是在朗肖和无主林的边地度过的，不常在城里。他向数不清的人问起他那老一套的问题，所得的永远是同一句回答：此事一无所知；而且说实在的，随着时日推移，这些人还能知道些什么的指望，也愈发渺茫了。于是到后来，他不时变得郁郁寡欢，同众人也少言寡语起来。


===Original===

Came the spring, and therewith the mighty Outland conqueror; but the shortest tale to tell of him is, that there he conquered nothing, but was held aloof at all points, save here and there he was suffered to break through to his great scathe. But his host was so big, that he hung about till the autumn. He gat but one gain, such as it was, that ere he brake up his host the King of the City fled to him and became his friend. And they two took rede together as to what they should do the next year to fall upon the land which was his, as he said.

Meantime, his back being turned upon his once subjects, many men began to think belike they might do without him once and for all, when they cast up the use he had been to them in times past. And this imagination grew, until at last a great Mote was called, and there it was put forward, that since the City had a Porte and a Great Council, and a Burgreve under these, the office of King was little needed there. So first with one accord they escheated their runaway, who they well knew would henceforth be their foe, and gave out that all they who had held of him should now hold of the Porte; and next, with little gainsaying, they did away with the office of King altogether, and most men felt the lighter-hearted therefor. And the City throve as well as ever it had done. So wore that year to an ending.

The next year the two Kings did in very sooth bring a great host against that folk; but fell not on the city itself, but gat a-land some twenty miles to the east thereof; and this they did easily, because Sir Godrick, with the rede of the Great Council, let them do so much, whereas he deemed it were well if he might be done with them once and for all. So he gat the very pick of his folk together, of whom was the Red Lad in high place, much dreaded of all his foemen.

Then Sir Godrick by his wisdom chose time and place for the battle, whereas the others must fight when and where he would. Such an overthrow they gat, that they might not draw to a head again. The old City King, fighting desperately, was slain by the Red Lad in the beginning of the rout; but the other King escaped by sharp spurring and the care and valour of his best knights, who rode about him in a plump. He stayed not till he came to his ships, where he gat aboard and sailed away to his own land, whence he came back again never to trouble the City of the Sundering Flood.

===Chinese===

春天到了，那威势赫赫的外邦征服者也随之而来；但说起他来，最简短的讲法便是：他在那里一无所克，处处被拒于外，只偶尔被容他突破进来一阵，便教他大吃其亏。只是他的人马实在庞大，竟一路盘桓到秋天。他只落得一样进账——也不过尔尔——便是在他散去兵马之前，洪流之城之王投奔到他那里，与他结成了朋友。于是二人共同定计，商议来年如何杀奔那片国土；照那洪流之城之王说来，那本是他自家的疆土。

其时，那国王既已掉头背弃了旧日的臣民，许多人便开始寻思：把往日他对他们的用处盘算盘算，这位国王未始不能从此一去不回、再不要了。这个念头愈滋愈长，末了终于召开了一场民众大会，会上有人提议：洪流之城既有市府与大议事会居上，其下又设一城守听命，国王这一职位在城中原没有多少用处。于是他们先是一致废黜了那个弃城而逃的人——大家心里有数，他从今往后必是他们的仇敌——并传谕四方：凡先前受封于他的，今后一概改受封于市府；接着，又几乎没有多少人异议，索性把国王这一职分整个儿废除了，多数人都为此觉得心头轻快了些。那城也一如既往地兴旺。这一年便这样挨到了尽头。

第二年，那两个国王当真引领一支大军来攻打这邦百姓；却不直扑城下，而是在城东约二十英里处舍舟登陆；此事他们做得轻易，是因为戈德里克爵士依着大议事会的方略，故意容让到这一步——他心想，若能就此一劳永逸地了结他们，岂不甚好。于是他把麾下人马中的精锐之选尽数聚齐，其中红衣少年位列高位，令他所有的敌人闻风胆寒。

随后，戈德里克爵士凭他的智慧，拣定了交战的时与地，教敌人只得在他愿意的时地应战。他们遭了那样一场大溃败，从此再也聚拢不起来。那洪流之城的老王拼死鏖战，在溃败方起之际便丧生于红衣少年之手；另一位王则靠狠命催骑，又靠他麾下最出色的骑士们簇拥环护的尽心与骁勇，逃得了性命。他马不停蹄，直逃到自家船边，登船扬帆，归本国去了；从此再没有回来搅扰洪流之城。

## L / 五十

===Original===

The Red Lad Speaks Privily with Sir Godrick
This befell in April, and toward the latter days of it Osberne came before Sir Godrick and would talk with him apart, and Sir Godrick received him with all kindness, and spake to him privily, and asked him what he would. Said Osberne: “Lord and dear friend, thou art now become a mighty lord far greater than most kings. So busy have our two lives been with deeds that might not be set aside, that now for a long time we two have had but little converse together, such as friends desire. Yet nevertheless through it all I have felt thy love unto me, as mine unto thee, wherefore this word that I must say irks me sorely, to wit that now at last we must presently part.”
Said Sir Godrick: “If I am become a mighty ruler, thou hast become a warrior such that I well think the worlds holds none other so mighty; and true it is that I love thee no worse for all the hard and troublous days. And hard and troublous have they been forsooth; so that oft have I bethought me of that old man the king of the kine, and his welcome and his bidding, in the wide green valley by the river whereby we passed when we were wending to Longshaw that first time, though well I wot that earth has no such refuge for me. I say thou art great, and I love thee; wherefore thou hast a right to make thy choice, and least of all would I balk thee in thy desire. Belike we may meet again. Now wilt thou tell me what thou wilt do?” Said Osberne: “With a good will. For this is true, lord, that having been now five years amongst all sorts of folk, and some of them being such as might tell me some tidings of what I seek, I have had no tidings, and now needs must I say that lost is lost. But first, before I give all up, I will go to Longshaw and abide there, and hang about the Wood for one month, to give me one last chance; and then if nought befall, I shall ride straight to my folk in the Dale beside the Sundering Flood, and there shall I live and die in such content as I may. And I do thee to wit, my friend, that the picture of the grey bents and the long houses, and the sheep and beasts going to and fro, and the few folk of the stead, and the hall within its shining black timbers, all this comes before me and softens my heart. For hast thou not noted how bitter and surly I have grown in these latter days?”

===Chinese===

红衣少年与戈德里克爵士密谈
话说这事出在四月；到了四月末梢的几天，奥斯伯恩来见戈德里克爵士，要与他说些背人的话。戈德里克爵士殷勤尽礼地接待他，屏人与他私语，问他有何见教。奥斯伯恩道：“主公，亲爱的朋友，你如今已成了一位大领主，尊荣远过世间多半君王。你我两条性命，一直被那些撒不开手的大事缠住，忙了这许多年，以致许久以来，你我朋友之间所渴望的那等倾心之谈，竟很少有过了。然而这一路走来，我始终觉得你对我的情义，一如我对你的情义；所以我此刻必得说出口的话，着实教我难受——便是说：到头来，你我眼前就要分手了。”
戈德里克爵士道：“纵然说我成了大的主宰，你也成了一员战将，我深深相信，普天之下再没有第二个这般雄武的人；实情也确是如此：任凭这许多艰难忧患的日子过去，我对你的爱毫不减损。这些日子委实艰难忧患；以致我常常想起那位老人家、那牛群之王，想起他怎样相迎、怎样相邀——就在我们头一回往朗肖去的路上、所经过的那条河边的开阔青谷之中；虽然我深知，人世间再没有那样一个容我的归宿。我说你是了不起的人物，我爱你；所以你有权自作抉择，而我最不愿做的事，便是拦阻你的心愿。兴许你我后会有期。如今你可愿告诉我，你打算做什么？”奥斯伯恩道：“甘愿奉告。主公，实情是：我在各色人等中间混迹五年了，其中未尝没有那种或能告诉我一点所寻消息的人，我却始终一无所闻；如今我只得说一句：失去的终究是失去了。但在彻底撒手之前，我要先到朗肖去住下，在无主林左近盘桓一个月，给自己最后一个机会；到那时倘若毫无音耗，我便径直投分离洪流边谷地里的亲族去，在那里度此余生，能得几分安适，便安于几分。朋友，我且教你知晓：那幅图景——灰色的草坡，长长的屋舍，羊群牲口来来往往，庄上寥寥几个人丁，黑亮木料围成的大堂——这一切一一来到我眼前，把我这颗心都软化。你可曾留意，近来这些日子，我变得何等尖刻乖戾么？”

===Original===

“I have seen thee sad,” said Sir Godrick.
“Nay,” said Osberne, “it is worse than that; but let it be. Well now I shall tell thee another thing that hath got hold of me, and thou wilt think it wild folly belike. But this it is: When I am in my own Dale again, then the first morning when I arise I shall hie me straight to that old trysting-place, and look across the Sundering Flood; and then it may be that a miracle of God will betide, and that I shall see my maiden there in her old place, and then shall we be no more utterly disunited, as though each for each we were neither of us in the world.”
Said Sir Godrick: “This is a hope of no great things, nor is it like to come about. Were it well for this to leave thy fellows and thy friends and all the fame of thine that shall be?”

===Chinese===

“我看你是郁郁伤怀。”戈德里克爵士道。
奥斯伯恩道：“不，比那还要糟些；只是由它去吧。如今我再告诉你一件攫住了我的心的事，你多半要当作狂愚之举。可事情便是这样：等我回到自家的谷地，头一个清晨一起身，我便径直赶到那处旧日的相会之处，隔水眺望分离洪流；那时节，兴许上帝会降下一个奇迹，教我看见我的少女还立在她那老地方；那样，我们便再不至于彼此隔绝净尽——仿佛在彼此眼里，你我哪一个都不曾在这世上一般。”
戈德里克爵士道：“这指望算不得什么大端的事，也不像会成真。为着这个，便撇下你的同袍与好友，撇下你日后必将赢得的那一切名声，值得么？”

===Original===

Osberne laughed. “Ah yes,” he said, “some deal I know it now, that fame; when we draw together before the foemen, and our men cry out, ‘The Red Lad! the Red Lad!’ in no faltering voice, and even therewith the foeman’s ranks quaver, as the trees of the wood when the wind comes up from the ground amongst them; and then I ride forward with Boardcleaver in my fist, and the arrows fly away about me for fear, and the array opens before me, and we plunge in and find nought there, and the rout goes down the green meadows. Yea, so it is, and many deem it fair. But then comes the quiet of the night, and my comrades are as though they were dead, and my praisers are voiceless, and I am alone; and then meseems it is I that have been overthrown and thwarted, and not thine enemies and mine, my friend. Nay, let me go back to my folk, and the land that I know and that endures before me when others have faded out. There will I abide whatso may come to me.” Then he said: “Moreover there is this last month at Longshaw; who knows what may there betide? I shall keep my eyes and ears open I promise thee.”
“Ah!” said Sir Godrick, “but beware, Red Lad, beware! Thou knowest how much hatred thou hast drawn upon thee for thy dealings with the rascaile of the Wood. Be sure that traps will be laid for thee, and look to it that thou walk not into one! And now I will say to thee farewell! It may be many a long day ere I see thy face again; and yet methinks I shall. And now I tell thee, that hitherto I have had more than enough gain out of thee, and scarce enough of joy. Maybe in days to come it shall be otherwise.”
So they kissed and departed each from each. And Osberne made no farewells to anyone else, and said that he was for Longshaw, and should abide there a month or so. And thus he rode his ways.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩笑了。“是啊，”他说，“那名声，如今我也略尝着些滋味了：当我们临敌列阵，自家儿郎放声高呼‘红衣少年！红衣少年！’，声气没有半点迟疑，便在那一刻，敌人的行伍颤动了，像风从地皮上刮进林中、树木簌簌打颤一般；于是我手握断板剑纵骑向前，羽箭也吓得从我身畔四散飞开，敌阵在我眼前裂开，我们纵身杀入，内里却空无一物，溃兵便顺着绿草场一路败逃下去。正是这样，许多人也当它美妙。可是随后夜深人静，同袍们酣睡得恰似死人一般，赞颂我的人声息全无，我只身独在；那时我倒觉得，被打垮、遭挫败的是我自己，而非你的仇敌、我的仇敌，我的朋友。不，让我回到我的人民那里去，回到那片我认得的土地——别的一切都黯淡消散之后，它还长存于我眼前。我就在那里等候，无论何事临头，我都承当。”随后他又道：“况且还有朗肖这最后一个月呢；谁知道那里会生出什么事来？我答应你，我自会睁开眼睛、竖起耳朵。”
