“啊！”戈德里克爵士道，“可要当心，红衣少年，当心！你知道，为了你与无主林中那帮流窜匪徒的干系，你招下了多少仇恨。你尽管相信，人家必定会给你设下陷阱，千万留神，莫要一头闯了进去！如今我便与你话别了！或许要过许许多多日子，我才能再见你的面；然而我想，总还有见得着的一天。如今我告诉你：直到今日，我从你身上得的益处早已绰绰有余，得的欢喜却还嫌不足。往后的岁月里，也许会倒转来也未可知。”
于是二人亲吻而别，各奔前程。奥斯伯恩也未向旁人一一辞行，只说自己要往朗肖去，在那边逗留一月上下。他便这样策马登程去了。

## LI / 五十一

===Original===

Osberne Is Beguiled by Felons

Now he took up his abode there; and presently he took to going day after day along a certain path, which was just well within the borders of the Wood. And there he would walk well-nigh all day, sometimes going further, sometimes stopping short and going to and fro, and this became known to all men, and such times he was unarmed, save that he was girt with Boardcleaver under his gown.

Now on the thirteenth day of his sojourn he walked this path, and had gone somewhat further than usual, and was beginning to think of turning back, when there came a man toward him from the Wood and hailed him, and he took his greeting. The man was clad in black, and had a buckler at his back and sword and dagger by his side, a white sallet on his head: a long-nosed, dark-haired man, beardless and thin-lipped, whose eyes came somewhat too near to each other each side of his head. He looked as if he might be some chapman’s servant.

Osberne looked for him to pass by him, and stood a little aside; but the man stopped and said: “O famous warrior, might a carle of no worth speak with thee a few words this noon?”

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩遭恶徒诓骗

于是他便在那里住了下来；不久，他养成习惯，日复一日地沿着一条小径走去，那小径恰恰就在无主林的边界之内。他在那里一走便几乎是一整天，有时走得远些，有时中途停下，来回踱步。这事渐渐弄得人尽皆知；而在这种时候他不带兵刃，只是长袍底下束着断板剑。

且说他客居的第十三日，他又走上这条小径，走得比往常略远，正开始盘算折返，忽有一个男人从林中朝他走来，向他招呼，他应了那人的礼。那人一身黑衣，背上背着小圆盾，腰间挂着长剑和匕首，头上扣着一顶白色轻盔：是个长鼻子、黑头发的男人，无须，薄唇，两只眼睛在头颅两边挨得略近了些。看模样，他像是哪个货郎的仆役。

奥斯伯恩只道他会打身边走过，便略往旁边让了让；那人却停下脚来说道：“赫赫有名的壮士，可容一个不足挂齿的汉子，在这晌午同你说几句话么？”


===Original===

“Why not?” said Osberne, smiling, for never might he bring himself to the fashion of great men to be rough and short with common folk. Said the newcomer: “Thou art far from the host today, and hast no angry look on thee, wherefore I shall risk thy wrath by saying that thou lookest somewhat less than gleeful, great warrior.” Said Osberne: “I have a trouble on me, and I have been forced to let many men know thereof.”

“Wilt thou tell me thereof?” said the newcomer; “maybe I shall be the last to whom thou shalt tell it.”

Osberne looked on him a while doubtfully and anxiously; at last he said: “This it is. Five years ago a maiden was stolen from me, and I have sought her since in many places, and have heard no word concerning her of any avail.” Said the carle: “Dost thou remember the battle in the square by the carfax of the great City, and how there was a man before thy mighty hand who cried out to spare his life, for that he could tell thee of the said maiden; and thereon thou wert about to give him peace, but ere thou couldest take him to thy mercy he was slain by one of the carle-weavers?”

“Yea,” said Osberne, “I remember it.”

===Chinese===

“有何不可？”奥斯伯恩微笑道；他从来学不来大人物那套对寻常百姓粗声粗气、三言两语打发的做派。那新来的说道：“壮士今日远离军旅，脸上又无愠色，因此我斗胆冒犯你的雷霆，说一句：瞧你的气色，似乎不怎么欢悦。”奥斯伯恩道：“我心头压着一桩愁事，不得已已让许多人知道了。”

“你肯说给我听么？”那新来的道；“说不定，我会是你告知此事的最后一个人呢。”

奥斯伯恩疑惑而又焦灼地打量了他半晌，终于说道：“是这样。五年前，一位少女被人从我身边劫走，此后我在许多地方寻访她，却没听到一句有用的音信。”那汉子道：“你可记得洪流之城十字街口广场上的那一仗？记得在你那神威的手下，曾有一个人高声求饶，说他能告诉你那位少女的下落；你正要饶他性命，可还没等你对他开恩，他就被一个织工汉子结果了？”

“记得，”奥斯伯恩道，“这事我记得。”


===Original===

“Now,” said the carle, “I shall make no mystery of it, but shall tell thee at once that that same man was the brother of the master whom now I serve. And I have an errand from him unto thee, and he saith that what his brother knew, he knows, and somewhat more; and thy maiden is yet alive, and that he can tell thee how to find her surely if thou wilt. And he is not far hence.”

Osberne looked somewhat wildly, and he caught the carle by the hand and cried out: “Good fellow, bring me to him at once and I will well reward thee.” “Nay,” said the carle, “but there comes something before that; my master is a chapman, and liveth by selling, not by giving; and he will take of thee two hundred nobles before thou hast his tale. Thou and I may call that weregild for the slaying of his brother.” “Yea,” said Osberne, “but I carry not two hundred nobles in my pouch.”

“Well then,” said the carle, “I will be here tomorrow or the day after if thou wilt.” “O nay, nay,” said Osberne, “but abide thou here, and I will go up to the castle and fetch the gold.” “So be it,” said the carle; and he sat him down by the wayside, and pulled out victuals and wine from his scrip and fell to dining.

But Osberne put forth all his swiftness of foot, and was speedily in his lodging, and came to his treasury and took forth the gold and set it in a bag, and hastened back again, and found the carle where he had left him. “Thou art swift-foot indeed,” said the carle, “but belike thou shalt not often again run so fast as thou hast e’en now. But thou art breathed; wilt thou not sit down a while till thou come round?” “No,” said Osberne shortly, “I will on at once.” “Well then,” said the carle with a grin, “suffer me to carry thy bag.” “Take it,” said Osberne, and reached it out to him. The carle handled the bag and said: “Plump are the nobles, lord, if there be but two hundred herein.” “There is more in it,” said Osberne, “for there is the gift for thee. But lead thou on straightway.” So the carle led on, and they went by divers woodland paths for some two hours, and then they heard the sound of a little water falling. Quoth the carle: “It is down in this ghyll that my master promised to abide me.” And therewith he began to go down the side of a ghyll well bushed and treed, and somewhat steep, and Osberne followed him. When they got to the bottom there was a fair space of flat greensward underneath a little force of the water; but no man awaited them.

===Chinese===

“如今，”那汉子道，“我不与你打哑谜，这就直说：那人正是我现今所服侍的主人的兄弟。我奉他之命来见你；他说，他兄弟知道的，他都知道，还多知道一些；你那位少女还活着，只要你愿意，他能告诉你寻到她的确凿门路。他离此地不远。”

奥斯伯恩的神色有几分狂乱，他一把攥住那汉子的手，喊道：“好伙计，快带我去见他，我必重重赏你。”“不成，”那汉子道，“这之前还有一节；我家主人是个货郎，靠卖货过活，不靠白送；你要听他的话，得先付他二百枚诺勃勒金币。你我不妨把这唤作他兄弟被杀的血金。”“好吧，”奥斯伯恩道，“只是我身边可没带着二百枚诺勃勒金币。”

“那么，”那汉子道，“你若愿意，我明日或后日再在此地等你。”“啊不，不，”奥斯伯恩道，“你就守在这儿，我上城堡去取金子。”“就这么办，”那汉子说；他在路边坐下，从褡裢里掏出吃食和酒，自顾自吃起来了。

奥斯伯恩却拿出他飞毛腿的全副脚力，不多时便奔回寓所，径到他的藏金处，取出金子装进一只袋子，又急忙赶回，在原处找到了那汉子。“你这脚程可真快，”那汉子道，“不过往后你怕是难得再跑得像方才这么快了。可你也喘透了气；要不要坐一会儿，缓一缓再说？”“不，”奥斯伯恩简短地答道，“我要立刻上路。”“那么，”那汉子咧嘴一笑，“让小人替你背袋子吧。”“拿去，”奥斯伯恩说着，把袋子递了过去。那汉子掂弄着袋子道：“老爷，这里头若当真只有二百枚，这些诺勃勒可真够沉实的。”“里头不止呢，”奥斯伯恩道，“还有赏你的那份。不过你只管头前带路。”于是那汉子领路，两人沿着各条林间小径走了约莫两个钟头，随后听见一点小小的落水之声。那汉子道：“我家主人约定在这下面的涧谷里等我。”说着他便开始往一道涧谷的侧坡下走去；谷坡灌木丛生，林木繁茂，还有些陡，奥斯伯恩跟在他身后。到了谷底，一道小小的瀑布底下有一片平整好看的青草坪；可是并没有人等他们。


===Original===

“Where is thy master, good fellow?” said Osberne. “He will scarce be far,” said the carle; “I will call him.” And therewith he set two fingers to his mouth and whistled shrilly.

Now Osberne was all beswinked with his run to and fro the castle and his two hours’ walk thereafter, and he was sore athirst, so he went down on his knees to drink of the clear little pool beneath the force. And now, what with the failing day and the tall trees well-nigh meeting overhead, it was dusk in the ghyll; and moreover as Osberne drank (and he was in no hurry about it) with his face to the force and his back to the length of the ghyll, the tinkling and splashing of the force deafened his ears to any sound but a somewhat big one. So he drank and thought no evil; but of a sudden he felt a sharp pain in his left side, and ere he could say that he knew he had been smitten, another and another, and he rolled over on the greensward and lay still, and there stood above him three men, the carle-messenger to wit and another of like sort, and a third clad in white armour.

“The end of the Red Lad!” quoth the messenger. “Nay,” said the other carle, “draw thy sword and smite the head from him, lord; make sure of him.” The knight half-drew his sword from the scabbard; but then stayed his hand and said in a quavering voice: “Nay, nay! let us begone. Dost thou not see? There is one sitting by him!” “It is a bush in the dusk,” said the other; “give me thy sword.” But the knight for all answer ran swiftly down the ghyll, and they two that were left shrank and trembled, for there verily sat one by the wounded man in a scarlet kirtle, as they deemed, and a bright steel basnet. So they ran also after their master, and all three fell to climbing the side of the ghyll.

Now about a mile thence was a certain hermitage in a clearing of the wood, and when the night was growing dark the door was smitten on, and when the hermit opened, there was before him a tall noble-looking man in scarlet kirtle and bright steel basnet, bearing in his arms another man dead or grievously hurt. And the tall man said: “Canst thou leechdom?”

===Chinese===

“你家主人在哪儿，好伙计？”奥斯伯恩道。“他总走不远，”那汉子说，“我来唤他。”说着他把两根指头搭在唇上，打了一声尖厉的呼哨。

奥斯伯恩来回奔了一趟城堡，又接着走了两个钟头的路，浑身累乏，口渴得厉害，便跪下身去，就着瀑布底下清澈的小水潭喝水。这时，天色向晚，高树在头顶几乎枝叶相接，涧谷里已是暮色昏昏；加之奥斯伯恩喝水时（他喝得不慌不忙）面朝瀑布，背对涧谷的纵深，瀑布的叮咚飞溅之声充塞两耳，除了稍大的动静，什么声响也听不进去。于是他只顾喝水，心中不疑有他；蓦地，他左肋一阵剧痛，还不等他明白自己已遭了毒手，又是一下，又一下，他便翻倒在草坪上，躺着不动了。他头顶上站着三个人：正是那传信的汉子，另一个同样货色的，还有第三个，周身披着白甲。

“红衣少年的下场到了！”那传信的说道。“不，”另一个汉子道，“大人，拔出剑来，砍下他的头，作实了再说。”那骑士将剑从鞘中抽出一半，却又缩住手，颤声道：“不，不！我们走吧。你难道没瞧见？他旁边坐着一个人！”“那是暮色里的一丛灌木，”那汉子说，“把你的剑给我。”那骑士却一言不答，撒腿飞奔下涧谷去了。剩下两人又惊又抖：千真万确，伤者旁边坐着一个人，照他们看来，那人穿一袭猩红长袍，戴一顶亮钢战盔。于是他们也拔腿追着主人逃去，三人一齐攀上了涧谷的侧坡。

距此约一英里，林中一块空地上有一座隐士庐。夜色渐浓时，有人叩响了庐门；隐士开门一看，面前立着一个身材高大、仪表堂堂的男子，穿猩红长袍，戴亮钢战盔，怀里抱着一个人，看去不是死了，便是伤得极重。那高个子男子说道：“你懂医道么？”


===Original===

“Yea,” said the hermit, “therein have I been well learned.”

“See here then, here is a man grievously hurt, but he is not dead. Now I have done all I might for him, for by my craft have I staunched his blood; but I wot that he needeth long leechdom to be made whole. Now I may not come under thy roof, so take him of me, and lay him on thy bed and look to him, and do thy best: for if thou heal him thou shalt thrive, and if thou heal him not thou shalt dwindle.” “Fair sir,” said the hermit, “I need neither promise nor threat, for God’s love and Allhallows’ I will heal him if it may be.”

So he took Osberne from Steelhead’s arms, and being a stark and big man got him on to the bed and did off his raiment. Then he searched his grievous hurts according to leechcraft, and presently looked up from the wounded man and said: “Since this man is not yet dead, I deem not his hurts deadly, and I think to heal him with the help of the Holy Saints.” Said Steelhead: “Thou hast in thy mouth, my friend, a deal of holiness that I know nought of. But I thank thee, and if thou heal my friend verily I will call thee Holy. Now shall I depart, but tomorrow forenoon I shall come here again and learn tidings of him.”

===Chinese===

“懂，”隐士道，“这门学问我学得精熟。”

“那你看：这人伤得极重，却还没有死。我能做的都做了——凭我的法术，我止住了他的血；但我知道，他要痊愈，还须长久的医治。我进不得你的屋檐，所以把他从我这儿接过去，放到你床上，好生照看，竭尽全力：你若医好他，必得兴旺；医不好他，必遭衰败。”“好先生，”隐士道，“我既不图许诺，也不惧威吓；看上帝的慈爱和诸圣堂的份上，只要人力可为，我必医好他。”

于是他从钢首怀中接过奥斯伯恩；他本是条强健的大汉，把奥斯伯恩抱上床，褪去他的衣裳，随后依着医道察验那些重伤，片刻之后，从伤者身上抬起头来，说道：“这人既还没有死，我瞧他的伤便非致命；我想凭着诸位圣徒之助，把他治好。”钢首道：“我的朋友，你满口的圣洁名号，是我一概不知的。不过我谢你；你若果真医好我的朋友，我便唤你一声圣徒。如今我该走了，明日上半天我再来此地，探问他的消息。”


===Original===

“Go in peace, and God and Allhallows keep thee,” said the hermit.

“Well, well,” said Steelhead, “we will not contend about it, but I look to it to keep myself.” And therewith he strode off into the night.

Then lay Osberne between life and death a long while; but after a time he began to mend, and came to his right mind, and remembered the felon-strokes in the ghyll; but of Steelhead’s being there he knew nothing, for Steelhead had charged the hermit to say no word of it to Osberne. The hermit was a good and kind man and a well-learned leech, and after a while Osberne began to mend speedily. And he would have amended speedier, but he was sick at heart that his sudden hope had so failed him, and said within himself that now all hope was gone. Albeit the Dale and Wethermel drew him to them without ceasing.

===Chinese===

“愿你平安地去，愿上帝和诸圣堂保佑你。”隐士道。

“罢了，罢了，”钢首道，“这个我们不必争执；我只指望自己保得住自己。”说罢，他大步跨进夜色里去了。

此后奥斯伯恩在生死之间躺了许久；过了一阵，他渐渐见好，神志复原，记起了涧谷里恶徒的毒手；至于钢首曾在那里，他却毫不知情，因为钢首叮嘱过隐士，不许对奥斯伯恩吐露半个字。那隐士是个善良仁厚之人，又是精通医道的医师；又过了些时日，奥斯伯恩便迅速好转起来。他本会好得更快，无奈心中愁苦——那突如其来的指望竟这般落了空——他心里自语：如今一切指望都断绝了。只是，谷地和韦瑟梅尔始终不住地牵动着他。

## LII / 五十二


===Original===

The Meeting of Osberne and Elfhild

At last, when it was some six weeks from the time of that felony, and Osberne was on his legs again, and had gone to and fro in the wood nigh to the hermit’s cell, now he began to think he must get him home to the House of Longshaw, and thence away to the Dale with a trusty guide; and the hermit would not say him nay, whereas his strength was but just come back to him.

On a time he went abroad from the cell, and was girt to Boardcleaver lest he should come across aught ill; he went somewhat further than he had been wont, till the day was beginning to draw toward sunset. It was now the latter end of May, and the leafy boughs were at their fairest; the sky was bright and blue, and the birds were singing in heavenly choir, and he scarce thought it good to go back speedily to the dark cell. So he went on a little further and a little further, till he was ware in the glade before him of one whom, as she drew nigher to him, he saw to be a seemly dame as for her years, straight and tall; neither was she clad in rags, but in a comely black gown and white coif. Nevertheless, as ’tis said, Once bit, twice shy, so it was with him, and he was for giving her the go-by. But she would not have it so, and she greeted him and said: “Hail to thee, noble; whence art thou last?” Her voice was clear and good, and now as he looked in her face he deemed he saw no evil in it, but goodwill rather. But he said: “Hail to thee, dame; I am last from a sickbed, where guile and felony laid me.”

“Well,” said she, “but there is something else than guile and felony in the world, is there not?”

“I know not,” said he shortly.


===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩与埃尔弗希尔德相逢

终于，自那桩恶行过去约莫六个星期，奥斯伯恩已能重新起身行走，也在隐士静室近旁的树林里来回走动过了，这时他开始寻思，自己该回朗肖之家去了，再从那里由一名可靠的引路人带着回谷地去；那隐士也不拦他，因为他的气力本也才刚刚复原。

有一日，他从静室出门走动，腰间束着断板剑，以防撞上什么歹事；他走得比往常略远，直到白日开始偏西，渐渐朝日落处沉去。其时已是五月末，枝头的绿叶正当最美好的时节；天空明净湛蓝，群鸟鸣啭，俨然天上的唱诗班，他简直不想就此匆匆回到那幽暗的斗室里去。于是他再往前走了一程，又走了一程，直到望见前面林间空地上有一个人影；待她渐渐走近，他看出是个以年纪而论颇为体面的妇人，身板挺直而高挑；她也并非衣衫褴褛，而是穿着一身齐整的黑袍，戴一顶白色头巾。然而，诚如俗话所说，一朝被蛇咬，十年怕井绳，他此时正是这般光景，便想同她错身而过，不加理会。她却不肯如此，先向他见礼，说道：“贵人安好；敢问你近来打从何处来？”她嗓音清朗和悦，这时他端详她的脸，觉得那上面不见凶恶，倒是一片好意。但他答道：“夫人安好；我近来来处是一张病榻，是奸谋和凶行把我撂倒在彼处的。”

“呃，”她说道，“可这世上除了奸谋和凶行，总还有别样的东西，不是么？”

“我不知道。”他简短生硬地答道。


===Original===

“I have seen somewhat else, if only once,” she said. “I have seen truth and good-faith and constancy and hope without reward; and five years have worn no whit of that away.”

“Hah,” said he; “was it a man, a warrior? Meseems I know one such, were it not for the hope.”

“Nay,” said she, “it is a woman.”

“And what like is she to look on?” said he. She answered: “If thou wilt come with me, she is no great way hence abiding my homecoming.” Said Osberne: “But what or who is it she is true to? or for whom doth she long, hoping against hope? Is it father, brother, son, sister, or what?” Said the carline: “It is her troth-plight man; and verily I, as well as she, deem that he is worthy of it; or was, when she saw him.”


===Chinese===

“我见过别样的东西，哪怕只见过一回，”她说。“我见过真诚，见过信义，见过坚贞，还见过不求回报的指望；五年光阴，也没能把这一切磨蚀掉一丝一毫。”

“哈，”他说；“那是个男子么，是个武士？依我看，我倒认得这么一个——若不算上那份‘指望’的话。”

“不是，”她说，“是个女子。”

“她生得何等模样？”他问。她答道：“你若肯随我来，她就在离此不远的地方，正等着我回家呢。”奥斯伯恩道：“可她以真心相守的是什么、是何人呢？她又在渴念着谁，明知无望却仍存指望？是父亲，是兄弟，是儿子，是姊妹，还是别的什么人？”老婆子道：“是与她订了婚约的夫君；说实在的，不但她这样想，连我也认定他当得起这份坚贞——或者至少她见他的时候是当得起的。”


===Original===

Osberne laughed, and said: “Good dame, if this be so, what profit were it to me to see her? I am not her troth-plight man, and if it be as thou sayest, I shall be unto her as one of the trees of the wood.” “There will be this profit,” said the carline, “that thou wilt set eyes on one of the fairest creatures that God ever made.” “Small profit therein,” said Osberne, laughing again, “if I set eyes on her beauty and am ensnared thereby; then maybe shall be another tale for this woodland. For belike thou deemest me old, but I am a young man, only I am haggard with the battle between life and death as I lay wounded yonder.” Therewith he pulled aback his hood, and the carline came close up to him and looked him hard in the face, but said nothing. Then he said: “Dame, to be short with thee, I have walked into the trap once, and will not again, if I may help it. Now I know not what thou art; for all I know thou mayst be a bit of bait of my foes, or even a sending from evil things. Nor hast thou said any word why specially I should come with thee.”

She was still standing close to him, and now she laid her hand on his breast and said: “This I say as a last word, and thou must take it how thou wilt. If thou dost not come with me now, thou shalt rue it only once, to wit, all thy life long.”

He looked on her and knit his brows, and said at last: “Well it is little to throw away the end of my life, and there may be some tidings or tracks of tidings to be found. I will go with thee, dame. Only this time,” he muttered, “let there be no coming to life again.”

“Thou art wise,” said the carline; “let us lose no time.” So they set off, and up and down by rough and smooth, till the wood was quite dark, and the stars were overhead when they came to a clearing, and sweet was the peace of the May night. At last they saw before them a glimmer of light, which as they wound about became presently a little window, yellow-litten, and casting its light upon a space of greensward and a little tinkling brook.


===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩笑了，说道：“好夫人，倘若当真如此，我见她又有什么益处？我又不是她订婚的夫君；倘若真如你所说，我在她眼里，便同林中一棵树木没有两样。”老婆子说：“益处倒有这么一条：你可以亲眼见一见上帝造过的最美的人儿之一。”奥斯伯恩又笑了，说道：“这益处可真不大——倘若我见了她的美貌，就此堕入彀中，那这片树林子里兴许又要添出一桩新的故事来了。因为你想必把我看老了，我却是个年轻人，只不过那回在那边带伤躺着，生死交战了一场，把我熬得形容枯槁。”说着，他把兜帽向后一褪；老婆子便走近前来，紧盯住他的脸看，却一言不发。他又说道：“夫人，长话短说：我已经一遭踏进过圈套了，但凡自己做得了主，决不再踏第二回。如今我并不知道你是什么人；据我所知，你也许是我仇家放下的一块饵，甚至是邪祟之物差遣来的。况且你也没有说出半句话，讲明我为何偏偏该随你去。”

她仍旧紧挨着他站着，这时她把一只手按在他胸口，说道：“这话我只说一遍，作为最后的话，你怎么领会，随你的便。你若此刻不随我去，将来你只悔恨一回——也就是说，悔恨整整一生。”

他望着她，皱起眉头，末了说道：“也罢，把我这条命的末尾抛掷出去，本也算不得什么，何况兴许还能寻到些消息，或是消息的踪迹。我便随你去，夫人。只是这一回，”他喃喃道，“可别再教我死而复生了。”

“你是个明白人，”老婆子说；“我们莫再耽搁。”于是两人动身上路，高高低低，坎坷坦夷，一路穿行，直走到林中一片漆黑；待他们到得一处林间空地时，星斗已经悬在头顶，五月之夜的安宁甜美极了。末了，他们望见前面透出一点微光；随着他们转来转去，那微光顷刻间变成了一扇小窗，窗里透出昏黄的灯光，把亮投在一方绿草地和一条淙淙轻响的小溪上。


===Original===

So came they to a little cot, seemly enough thatched with reed from the woodland meres. Osberne made up toward the door, but the carline put forth her hand and thrust him back, and said: “Not yet; abide where thou art a minute;” and straightway fell to going withershins round the house. This she did three times, while Osberne gat his anlace bare in his hand.

At last the carline came to him, and spake softly to him in his ear: “All is free now, Dalesman, come thou!” And she took him by the hand and opened the door, and lo, a little hall like many another cot, but clean and sweet and comely. Now Osberne had pulled his hood about his face again, and looked round; for as often happens when one enters a chamber, the child of Adam therein is the last thing one sees. Then he drew back a little, and stood there trembling. For what was in the chamber besides the simple plenishing was a maiden who stood up to receive them; tall she was and slender, clad in a dark blue gown; her hair dark red and plenteous, her eyes grey, her chin round and lovely, her cheeks a little hollow, and in the hollow of them entreaty and all enticement: she stood looking shyly at the newcomer, of whose face she might see but little. The carline seemed to note neither her nor Osberne, but cried out in a cheerful voice: “Now, child, if I be somewhat later than I was looked for, yet I have brought the gift of a guest, seest thou; a good knight who hath of late been brought to death’s door by felon’s deed, but is now grown whole and fight-worthy again. So let us bestir us to get him meat and drink and all that he needeth.”

So they fell to, while Osberne stood where he had first come in; and he scarce knew where he was, but looked down on the floor, as though the Sundering Flood of the Dales rolled betwixt him and the maiden; for indeed when his eyes first fell upon her he knew that it was Elfhild. Now the two women had not been long at dighting the supper ere there came a rough knock on the door, and straightway the latch was lifted and in strode three men-at-arms; two in jack and sallet with bucklers and sword and dagger, the third a knight clad in white armour with a white surcoat. This stirred Osberne out of his dream, and he sat down on a stool nearer in than he had been. The Knight cried out: “Ho, dame, I see thou hast one guest, and now here be three more for thee; we have stabled our horses in thy shed already, so thou hast nought to do save getting us our supper: dispatch I bid thee. And now who is this tall carle sitting there?”

Osberne knew them at once as they came in, that they were the three felons who had smitten him in the ghyll. He answered nought, and kept his hood about his face. “Roger,” quoth the knight, “and thou, Simon, cannot ye get an answer from the lither loon?” Roger lifted up his foot and kicked Osberne roughly, and Simon laid hold of his hood to pull it off him, but found it held tight enough; and Osberne spake in gruff and hollow voice: “I am a living man, ye were best to let me be.”


===Chinese===

他们就这样来到一座小小的茅舍前；屋顶用林中湖泽里割来的芦苇苫盖，颇为齐整。奥斯伯恩径直朝门口走去，老婆子却伸出手来把他推住，说道：“还不到时候；你先在原地站一会儿。”说罢便径自绕着屋子，逆着日头的方向走起来。她这样绕了三遭，奥斯伯恩则把匕首拔出鞘，握在手中。

末了，老婆子走到他跟前，凑在他耳边轻声说道：“如今百无禁碍了，谷民，随我来吧！”她携起他的手，推开了门；看哪，一间小小的堂屋，同别的许多茅舍里的一样，只是干净、清爽、体面。这时奥斯伯恩已重新把兜帽拉来遮住了脸，正环顾四周；因为正如人走进屋内时常见的情形，屋里的那个亚当之子，往往是最后才落入眼帘的。随后他微微退了一步，站在那里，浑身发颤。原来这屋子里，除了简朴的家什之外，还有一位起身迎接他们的少女：她身材高挑苗条，穿一身深蓝色的长裙；头发深红，丰厚稠密，眼睛灰色，下巴圆润可爱，双颊微微下陷，而在那陷窝里，含着哀恳，含着说不尽的动人心处。她羞怯地望着来客，来客的脸，她却几乎看不大见。老婆子似乎既不曾留意她，也不曾留意奥斯伯恩，只扬声欢然喊道：“好啦，孩子，我虽说比你们盼的时辰晚了一些，可我带回了一位客人来做礼物，你瞧；一位好骑士，新近遭恶徒毒手，几乎被送到死门前，如今却已康复，又能上阵厮杀了。咱们快些动手，给他备下饮食，还有他需用的一切吧。”

于是她们动手忙活起来，奥斯伯恩却还站在初进门的地方；他几乎不知自己身在何处，只顾低头望着地板，仿佛谷中的分离洪流又滚滚横在他与那少女之间；因为实实在在，他的目光初次落到她身上时，他就认出了那是埃尔弗希尔德。两个女子张罗晚饭还没多久，门口便传来一阵粗暴的敲门声，紧跟着门闩一挑，大踏步进来三名甲士：两个穿甲衣、戴轻盔，带着小圆盾、长剑和匕首；第三个是位骑士，周身白甲，外罩白罩袍。这一下把奥斯伯恩从梦境里惊醒了过来，他便在一张凳子上坐下，比先前更靠里了些。那骑士扬声喊道：“喂，夫人，我看你这里已经有了一位客人，如今又给你添上三位；我们的马已经拴进你的棚子里了，所以你只消给我们开晚饭就是：快着些，我吩咐你。咦，坐在那边的这个高个汉子是谁？”

三人一进门，奥斯伯恩立刻认了出来：这正是那日在涧谷里暗算过他的三个恶徒。他一言不答，只把兜帽遮着脸。那骑士道：“罗杰，还有你，西蒙，你们就没法叫这懒骨头吐出一句话来么？”罗杰抬起脚来，狠狠踢了奥斯伯恩一下，西蒙伸手揪住他的兜帽要扯下来，却觉得那兜帽捂得紧紧的；奥斯伯恩便用粗哑低沉的嗓音开口道：“我是个活人，你们最好别来招惹我。”


===Original===

Then had there been battle at once, but even therewith comes in Elfhild bearing a pewter measure of wine and beakers withal, and the newcomers stood staring at her beauty, silent for a minute. Then the Knight did off his basnet and spake in a loose, licorous voice: “The liquor we hoped for, but not the cupbearer; and so it is, that I would liefer have the cupbearer than the cup. Fair maid, will not a kiss go before the pouring out? or never shall I have heart to drink.” And he rose up and went toward the maiden, who stood confused and trembling, and turned pale. But Osberne had risen also, and with a quick turn had thrust between the White Knight and Elfhild, and now stood with his back to her, facing the felons.

“What, cur!” cried the White Knight: “shall we have thee out and flay thy back with our stirrup-leather?” Said Osberne, speaking slowly: “That is the third question too much thou hast asked in the last few minutes. Lo thou!” And he shook his hood from his face, and had Boardcleaver bare in his hand straightway. Then those three set up a quavering cry of, The Red Lad! the Red Lad! and ran bundling out of the cot; but Boardcleaver was swifter than they. One of the serving-men lost his head just outside the threshold; the Knight stumbled at the brook and fell, and never rose again. The messenger strove hard for the thicket, but the moon was up now, and it was but a few strides of the swift runner of the Dale ere Boardcleaver had taken his life.

The two women stood looking toward the open door the while, and the maiden said faintly and in a quavering voice: “Mother, what is it? what has befallen? Tell me, what am I to do?” “Hush, my dear,” said the carline, “hush; it is but a minute’s waiting after all these years.” Even therewith came a firm footstep to the door, and Osberne stepped quietly over the threshold, bareheaded now, and went straight to Elfhild; and she looked on him and the scared look went out of her face, and nought but the sweetness of joyful love was there. And he cried out: “O my sweet, where now is the Sundering Flood?” And there they were in each other’s arms, as though the long years had never been.


===Chinese===

这一来，眼看当场便要动起手来；恰在此时，埃尔弗希尔德捧着一白镴壶的酒走进来，还带着几只杯子；那几个新来的一见她的姿色，都直勾勾瞪着她，一时哑然无声。随后那骑士摘下战盔，用一种轻佻邪浪的声调说道：“酒是我们指望的，捧酒的人儿却没指望到；如今看来，我宁要这捧酒的人，不要这酒。美人儿，斟酒之前，不肯先赏一记亲吻么？不然我可没有心肠喝酒呢。”说着他站起身来，朝那少女走去；少女站在那里，又惶惑又发抖，脸色都白了。但奥斯伯恩也已起身，一个急转身，便抢到白甲骑士与埃尔弗希尔德中间，此刻背朝着她，面向那几个恶徒。

“怎么，狗才！”白甲骑士喝道，“要把你拖出去，拿马镫皮带抽烂你的脊背么？”奥斯伯恩缓缓说道：“方才片刻之间，你多问的这已经是第三个问题了。你自己瞧！”说罢他一抖头，兜帽从脸上褪落，断板剑转眼间已光闪闪握在手中。那三个登时发出一声发颤的喊叫：“红衣少年！红衣少年！”连滚带爬地涌出茅舍；然而断板剑比他们更快。一个仆役刚跨出门槛便丢了脑袋；那骑士在小溪边绊了一跤，扑倒在地，从此再没有起来。那传信人拼死命朝树丛奔逃，可此刻月亮已经升起，谷地的飞毛腿不过追出几步，断板剑便取了他的性命。

这番光景，两个女子始终站在那里望着敞开的门口；少女声音微弱，颤颤地问道：“娘，这是怎么了？出了什么事？告诉我，我该怎么办？”老婆子说：“嘘，我的乖，嘘；熬了这些年头，不过是再等这一小会儿。”恰在此时，门口传来一记沉稳的脚步声，奥斯伯恩悄没声儿地跨过门槛——此刻他头上已无遮无盖——径直走到埃尔弗希尔德面前；她望着他，脸上的惊惶褪尽了，只剩下一片欢喜情爱的柔甜。他高声喊道：“啊，我的甜人儿，如今分离洪流又在哪里？”于是两人就此相拥入怀，仿佛那漫长的岁月从来不曾有过。

## LIII / 五十三

===Original===

Strangers Come to Wethermel
Now turns the tale to Wethermel, and tells how that on the morrow of Midsummer, five years to the day since Osberne had bidden them farewell, the folk once more sat without-doors about the porch in the cool of the evening; neither was there any missing of the settled folk of those to whom he had said farewell. For all had thriven there that while. There sat the goodman, more chieftain-like than of old; there sat the goodwife, as kind as ever, and scarce could she be kinder; there sat Bridget, not much aged in the five years; forever she deemed it a certain thing that her nursling would come back to her. Lastly, there sat Stephen the Eater, wise of aspect and thoughtful, as if he were awaiting something that should happen which should change much in him; and there were the carles and the queans (with some few children amongst them who had not been there five years ago) who had been familiar to Osberne ere he left the Dale for warfare. It was growing late now, and the twilight was creeping up under a cloudless sky, when those folk saw newcomers wending the lane betwixt the outbowers, and making straight for the house-porch. They were but three, and as they drew nigh it could be seen that they were hooded and cloaked despite the warm night; and one was tall and seemed a stalwarth man, and another was jimp and went daintily, as if it were a young woman, and the third, who forsooth had her face but little hidden, seemed a carline of some three score years and ten.
None of the folk stirred save Stephen the Eater, who rose up as if to welcome the guests; and the tall man spake in a strange high voice that seemed as if it came from the back of his head: “May we three wayfarers be here tonight? For we saw this stead from afar, and it seemed a plenteous house, and we deem it guest-kind.” Quoth Stephen: “A free and fair welcome to you; ye shall eat of our dish, and drink of our cup, and lie as the best of us do. Ho, ye folk! now were we best within doors; for our guests shall be both weary and hungry belike.”
So into the hall they wended, and the three were shown to a good place amidmost thereof, so that all might see them; and there they sat, the tall man innnermost, nighest to the dais, the young woman by him and the carline outermost. Then came in the meat, which was both plenteous and good, and when all were fulfilled the drink was brought in, and the tall man arose and called a health on Wethermel, and that it might thrive ever. But some men thought that, as he lifted his hand to put the cup to his lips, a gleam of something bright came from under his wayfarer’s cloak. And Stephen the Eater called a health on the wayfarers; and then one drank to one thing, one to another, and men waxed merry and gleeful.

===Chinese===

生客来到韦瑟梅尔
如今话头转回韦瑟梅尔。且说仲夏次日——奥斯伯恩向众人道别，恰恰五年，一日不差——一家老小又趁傍晚的凉气，坐到门廊四周的露天里；当年他道别时安住庄中的那些人，如今一个也不曾缺少。这些年里，阖家都过得兴旺。庄主坐在那里，比往昔更有一派首领气象；女当家坐在那里，和善一如既往，再和善也难以更甚了；布里奇特坐在那里，五年之间不见怎么见老；她始终笃信，她奶大的孩子必定会回到她身边。最后坐着饕客斯蒂芬，神色明睿而若有所思，仿佛正等待一件必将发生、且要令他改变许多的事；还有那些汉子们和妇人们（其中还夹着几个五年前尚不在人世的孩子），都是奥斯伯恩离开谷地从军之前厮熟的人。这时天色渐晚，无云的天空下暮色悄悄爬升，众人忽见有新客沿着外舍之间的小径走来，径直奔向这家的门廊。来者只有三人：近前看时，虽是暖夜，却个个兜帽罩头、斗篷裹身；一个是高个子，像是个魁伟的男人；一个身量纤秀，脚下轻巧，仿佛是个年轻女子；第三个其实脸面几乎不曾遮掩，看去是个年约七旬的老婆子。
众人中只有饕客斯蒂芬起身，像是要迎客；那高个子开口了，嗓音又高又怪，仿佛是从他后脑勺里发出来的：“我们三个旅人，今夜可在贵府借宿么？我们打远处望见这处地方，看去是个丰足之家，想必待客殷勤。”斯蒂芬答道：“敞怀奉迎，欢迎之至；你们将吃我们盘中的食，喝我们杯中的酒，睡处同我们中间最体面的人一样。喂，众人！我们还是进屋去的好；客人多半又乏又饿了。”
于是众人进了大厅，那三位被引到堂中一处好座上，人人都看得见他们；三人落座：高个子坐在最里首，紧挨着上首的高台，年轻女子挨着他，老婆子坐最外。随后饭食端上来，既丰盛又精美；大家用饱之后，酒又送了上来。高个子起身，举杯祝韦瑟梅尔，愿它长盛不衰。却有人瞧见，他抬手举杯就唇之际，旅人斗篷底下闪出一道亮光。饕客斯蒂芬也举杯祝旅人们；接着你为此事祝酒，我为那事举杯，众人渐渐欢畅快活起来。


===Original===

But at last rose up Stephen the Eater and spake: “Meat and drink and lodging is free without price to every comer to Wethermel, and most oft, as here it is, our good will goes with it; yet meseemeth that since these friends of ours come belike from the outlands and countries where is more tidings than mostly befalleth here, it might please them to make us their debtors by saying us some lay, or telling us some tale; for we be not bustled to drink the voidee cup now, these nights of Midsummer, when night and day hold each other’s hands throughout the twenty-four hours.”
Then rose up the tall, high-voiced man and said: “It is my will that each one of we three should say something, be it long or short, to make the folk of Wethermel glad. For they have treated us wayfarers as though we were lords and kings, and their words go to their hearts. Now I will that thou, mother, begin, and that I make an end of this saying.”
Then he sat down, and the carline said: “I am all the more willing to this, as meseemeth I can tell you a tale such as ye have never heard the like of, and which will move every heart of you. And yet I must pray your patience, as belike it may be somewhat long for a tale of one night’s hall-glee: and on this night must the tale be begun and ended. Hearken then!”

===Chinese===

末了，饕客斯蒂芬起身说道：“来到韦瑟梅尔的客人，饭食、酒水与宿处一概分文不取，而且十有八九——今晚正是如此——还奉上我们的一片好意；只是依我看来，这几位朋友既多半来自外邦异域，那里的音信比我们这僻乡惯常听到的多，那么若蒙他们为我们唱一支歌谣、讲一个故事，教我们欠下他们一份情，想必他们也是乐意的；何况眼下是仲夏时节的夜晚，昼和夜在整整二十四个钟头里手拉着手，我们还不用急着喝那饯别的杯酒。”
于是那高嗓门的高个子男人起身说道：“我们三人各须献上一段话，或长或短，好教韦瑟梅尔的众人欢喜——这是我的意愿。因为他们待我们这些旅人，竟如同待王侯一般，他们的话句句都说进人心坎里。老妈妈，请你先起头，由我来为这段说话作结。”
说罢他坐下了，那老婆子便道：“这件事我愈发情愿应承，因为依我看来，我能讲一个你们从未听过可与之相比的故事，一个能动你们每一颗心的故事。只是我还得求诸位耐一耐性子，只怕它对一个夜晚的堂中之欢来说略嫌长了些：而这故事，必须今夜起头，今夜讲完。那就都请听好！”

## LIV / 五十四

===Original===

The Carline Beginneth Her Tale

There was an old woman, yet not cripple, who dwelt in a stead beside a great river, which none might cross, either by bridge or ford or ferry. But she dwelt not alone, neither was the house her own: for with her abode a damsel young of years, who was the owner of the said house, but had no kindred, for father and mother and all else had passed away from her. Therefore it is like that the Carline came to dwell with her because she loved the Maiden, and would serve her and do good to her. And no wonder was that, for not only was the Maiden now grown so beauteous that she was the pearl of all beauty, but also she was merry and kind, and loving as might be. So that none that saw her but must love her if they had any good in them.

Now ye will ask, since it was so with her, was there no young man who was drawn into the net of her love. But I must tell you that the stead where these twain dwelt was lonely, and there was but little recourse of folk thither. Yet I say not but that there was more than one young man of the dwellers thereby who thought it better than good to come to the house and sit and talk with her, and would have kissed and caressed her had they durst. But they durst not, for not one of them touched her heart; and though she was kind and friendly with every one of them, there was nought in her words or her mien by which they might anywise deem that she would suffer the toys of love from them. Sooth to say, the Maiden had a love, a fair youth and stalwarth, and a glorious man, and many were the words they had spoken together, but never had her hand touched his hand, nor his lips her lips; because betwixt these two was a river such as are few upon the earth, unbridged, unfordable, unferryable. And few might think that it was anywise like to betide that ever their two bodies should touch each the other; but the Carline, who was somewhat wise in lore, had an inkling that, despite this terrible hedge of water, the twain should one day meet.

===Chinese===

老妪开讲她的故事

从前有一个老婆婆，手脚却还不残废，住在一条大河边上的一处庄舍里；那条河，任是桥、是渡滩、是摆渡，谁都过不去。但她并非独居，那宅子也不是她自己的：因为同她住在一起的，是一位年纪正轻的姑娘，正是这宅子的主人，却举目无亲——父亲、母亲，连同其余的亲人，都已先后离世。因此老妪之所以来与她同住，多半是因为她疼爱这少女，愿意服侍她、为她出力。这也不足为奇：这少女如今出落得如此美貌，堪称众美之珠；而且性情欢愉仁善，亲厚爱人无以复加。所以凡是见到她的人，只要心中还有半点善念，便不能不爱她。

你们要问了：她既是这般人品，难道就没有哪个年轻男子落进她情爱的罗网里么？我却须告诉你们：这两人所居的庄舍偏僻荒凉，难得有什么人上门。然而我也不是说，左近住户中的年轻男子，便没有一个觉得能到那宅子里去，坐在她身旁同她说说话，是天大的美事；他们若敢，也早就同她亲嘴抚爱了。只是他们不敢：因为他们中间没有一个触动过她的心；她待他们每一个人固然都和气友善，但无论她的话语还是神色里，都没有半点能让他们认定，她肯容忍他们那些情爱的轻薄勾当。说句实话，少女早有了心上人：一位俊美健壮的青年，一条堂堂的好汉；他们俩说过的话不知凡几，可是她的手从未碰过他的手，他的唇也从未碰过她的唇：因为隔在这两人之间的，是一条世上少有的河——无桥可通，无滩可涉，无舟可渡。很少有人会想，这两人的身子有朝一日竟能彼此相触；可是老妪颇通些玄奥的学问，心里隐隐有个预感：尽管隔着这道可怕的水墙，这两个人终有一天会相会。


===Original===

Now it is to be said that oftenest the Maiden was patient, and abode the sundering with no ill cheer. But whiles her trouble was over heavy for her, and she would wander forth into the wood or the field, and go weeping and lamenting there; or she would sit in the chamber with the Carline, and cry out aloud on her love to come to her, and on all things on the earth and in the heavens, yea, the Great God himself sitting amongst the Cherubim, to help her, that for once, if once only before she died, she might feel her love’s arms about her and his face laid to hers.

Or again, she would, as it were, tell stories of how it would betide that at last they should meet, both grown old, and kiss once, and so walk hand in hand into the Paradise of the Blessed, there to grow young again amidst the undying spring, in the land where weariness is come to nought; and there would she sit and weep, as if there were no ending to the well of her tears.

At such times was the Carline sore grieved for her, and would strive to comfort her by giving her some little inkling of the hope which she, the old woman, had conceived in her heart, that the meeting of the those two should come about whiles they were yet young and lovely; more than that she might not tell the Maiden, lest the might should ebb from her. Thus wore the days between patience and despair, betwixt cheer and lamentation.

At last, when the Maiden was of some eighteen summers, great matters befell that countryside; for on a day came the alien reivers, such as are called the Red Skinners, with intent to rob and carry off all that was not too hot or too heavy for them, and to lay waste and destroy all that they might not bear away. But the folk of the land met them valiantly, and their friends on the other side of the fierce river aforesaid helped them what they might with the shot-battle; and great and grim was the murder, and the stour of the hardest.

===Chinese===

且说这位少女多半时候倒是忍耐的，怀着并不怨怼的心情承受着这隔断之苦。但有时她的愁烦实在沉重难当，她便独自踱进林中或田野，一路哭泣哀叹；或者同老妪一起坐在内室里，高声呼唤她的爱人到她身边来，呼唤地上天上的一切，是的，甚至呼唤端坐在基路伯中间的上帝亲自来救助她——只求在她死之前，哪怕仅仅一回，能感觉到爱人的手臂搂着她，他的脸贴着她的脸。

再不然，她又会像讲故事似的，说他们最终将如何相会：两人都已老态龙钟，亲吻一回，便手拉着手走进蒙福者的乐园，在那永不消逝的春天里，在那倦怠已化为乌有的国土上，重新变得年轻；而她坐在那里哭泣，仿佛她那眼泪的泉源永无枯竭之日。

每逢这种时候，老妪便为她心痛不已，总想方设法安慰她，把她这老婆婆心中怀着的那个指望，稍稍透露一点给她：这两人的相会，将在他们还年轻美貌的时候实现；再多她就不敢对少女说了，惟恐法力会因此消退。日子便这样在忍耐与绝望之间、在欢笑与哀哭之间消磨过去。

终于，在少女大约十八岁那年，那片乡野出了大事：有一天，外邦的抄掠之徒来了，就是那伙唤作红剥皮帮的，存心把一切不至于烫手、不至于太沉的东西都抢走掳走，凡是搬运不走的，便尽数焚毁破坏。但那片土地上的人们奋勇迎击，前述那条凶猛大河对岸的朋友们也竭力以箭斗相助；杀戮惨烈无比，战况酷烈到了极点。


===Original===

Now there were the Maiden and the Carline at their house, and nought easy was the rede for them. The Maiden bade flee to the next stead, which was some four miles thence, but the Carline bade abide, lest they be caught upon the way, which forsooth she deemed was most like to betide if they left the house, and that rede they took at the last. So they sat expecting what should befall them.

For a long while none of the aliens came anear them; but at last, when the battle was at its fiercest, rode up three men leading two unbacked horses, and they were of the mien and in the gear of the Red Skinners; and the Carline stood in the door to meet them, and she spake to them and said: “What will ye warriors? Why are ye not in the battle with your fellows?” Said one: “Because our errand is here and not there: neither are those men our fellows. We be the servants of that goodly merchant who guested here a while ago, and would have bought the maiden within there in all honour, and ye rewarded his good will with scorn, and mocks and japes and scurvy dealing. Wherefore he hath set these reivers on your folk, and hath sent us along with them to look to you. And twofold is our errand, to bear away the maiden without a price, and to slay thee. Hah! dost thou like it?”

Now the Carline remembered the coming of the said merchant, and how he had cast his love on the Maiden unhonestly and lustfully, and would have lain by her against her will had it not been for the lore of the said Carline, who letted him of his evil will and sent him away shamed.

===Chinese===

这时少女和老妪正在自己家里，一时也想不出什么好主意。少女主张逃到最近的庄舍去，离那里约有四英里；老妪却主张留守，惟恐半路被人截住——她确实认为，她们一离开宅子，最可能的结局便是如此。最后她们采纳了这个主张。于是两人坐着，静待祸福临头。

好半天没有一个外邦人走近她们；可是到末了，正当战斗最激烈的时候，有三个人骑马走来，还牵着两匹没上鞍的马，他们的相貌和装束都是红剥皮帮的做派。老妪站在门口迎着他们，开口说道：“诸位武士意欲何为？为何不与你们的同伙一同厮杀？”其中一人道：“因为我们的差事在这里，不在那里：那些人也并非我们的同伙。我们是那位体面货郎的仆人，他不久前在此做客，本想满有体面地把里面那位少女买下来，你们却以轻蔑报答他的好意，又嘲笑、又讥诮、又加以卑劣的作弄。因此他支使这伙抄掠之徒来对付你们乡人，又派我们随同前来照管你们。我们的差事有双重：一是把少女一文不花地带走，二是杀了你。哈！你可中意？”

老妪记起了那货郎上门的事：他当时怀着不正经的淫邪之心向少女求爱，若不是老妪有法术阻住了他的恶念，叫他含羞被逐而去，他早已不顾她的意愿强行玷污她了。


===Original===

But now she muttered something under her breath, and looked on those men, and made signs with her fingers, and then spake aloud: “Slay me speedily then, whiles ye are about it; for I take no great keep of life.” The men handled their weapons, but nothing came of it, and they sat in their saddles staring at the Carline as if they were mazed. And even therewith ran the Maiden forth from the house, and cast her arms about the Carline, and cried out: “Nay, nay! but ye shall not slay her! for as my mother hath she been, and none other have I had save her. But I pray you by your salvation to take this my mother with you, for I cannot do to be without her; and if I miss her, then shall I be of little use, miserable and forlorn, to that lord of yours that ye tell of so goodly.”

The old woman kissed her and embraced her, and then turned to those men and laughed in their faces; and they seemed presently as if awaking out of slumber, and one said: “Well, this may be; I see not why we should not slay thee there as well as here; and since the damsel would have it so, we will have thee along with us, and let the maiden settle it with our lord whether he will be wheedled by her or not. But come, to horse both of you! for time presses.”

So the two women were set a-horseback, and they men rode with a good pace out of the Dale toward the fells at the back thereof; and if at any time the women thought of turning rein and riding off, they had but to look at the men, how they were horsed, for their way-beasts were mighty strong steeds of good race, but the women were set on everyday nags, such as be seen on any highway.

===Chinese===

可这时她低声咕哝了一句什么，眼睛盯住那几个人，手指掐了个诀，然后朗声说道：“那么趁你们动手，就快些杀了我吧；我本不怎么爱惜性命。”那几个人摸起了兵刃，却毫无动静，只是坐在鞍上直勾勾地瞪着老妪，仿佛失魂落魄一般。恰在此时，少女从屋里奔出来，一把搂住老妪，喊道：“不，不！你们不许杀她！她于我恩同母亲，除了她我别无亲人。我凭你们的得救恳求你们，把这位我的母亲也一并带去吧，我没有法子离开她；若是失了她，我对你们说得那样体面的那位老爷，就成了个百无一用、凄惨无依的人了。”

老婆婆吻了她，抱住她，随后转身朝着那几个人，当面冲他们大笑；他们这时才像从睡梦中醒来似的，其中一人说道：“好吧，这样也使得；我看不出到了那边杀你，比在这里杀你有什么两样；既然姑娘这样求了，我们就把你一并带上，让姑娘自己去同我们老爷掰扯，看他肯不肯吃她的软话。来吧，你们两个都上马！时候紧迫了。”

于是两个女人被扶上马背，那些人便以不慢的步子驰出谷地，朝谷地背后的山地而去。两个女人但凡起过拨转缰绳逃走的念头，只消看看那些人的坐骑便罢——他们胯下的行路牲口都是良种的骏马，雄骏有力，而两个女人骑的却是寻常的驽马，大路上随处可见的那一种。


===Original===

After a while they came on to the broken ground at the foot of the fells, and all must needs ride slower; and then the Carline came sidling up to the Maiden, and saw how wan and woebegone was her face, and asked what ailed her; and she answered faintly at first, and then clearer and louder: “It is because I am thinking of him and his woe; and I wot well that now, so soon as the battle is over, there shall he stand yet and look over the Flood on to the field of deed, as if he were seeking after me dead among the corpses of the foe. And tomorrow he shall come down to the water’s edge while the dead yet lie there, and stand looking to see if I be not coming to meet him, as now I have been wont so many years. And the morrow of that morrow will he come, yea, and many a morrow, till his heart shall be outworn with longing and grief, and he will go away out of the Dale to escape from his sorrow, and shall nowise escape it. Ah, and how shall I know whither he will wend, or the place of the shifting dwelling of his wanderings? And I, and I, I wend away from him.”

Sore grieved was the Carline at her grief, and she said: “O my child, I pray thee keep up a good heart within thee, lest thou die of sorrow, and endure not the chances of the meeting. Who knows whether thou be wending away from him? Nay, to my mind thou art wending toward him, and he to thee; for never had ye come together hadst thou abided in thine old home and he in his.”

But the Maiden wept. But therewith rode along by them one of the men, and smote the Carline on the shoulders with his spear-staff, and bade her hold her peace, and not go on like a crazy hen.

===Chinese===

走了一程，他们上了山麓脚下乱石嵯峨的崎岖地带，人人只得放慢马步；这时老妪挨到少女身旁，见她脸色苍白、愁苦满面，便问她哪里不自在；她起初答得虚弱，随后才清晰响亮起来：“我这是在想他，想他的悲苦；我很明白，此刻，战斗一结束，他就会站在那里，隔着洪流眺望那片厮杀过的场地，仿佛要在敌人的尸堆里寻找死了的我。明天，趁着死人还躺在那里，他会走到水边，站在那里张望我会不会前去迎他，像这么多年来我惯常做的那样。明天的明天他还会来，是啊，一个明天又一个明天，直到他的心被渴念和悲伤磨蚀殆尽，他就会离开谷地去躲避他的愁苦，却怎么也躲不掉。啊，我又怎能知道他将漂泊到哪里，知道他萍踪无定的寓居之处？而我，而我，我竟是在离他而去。”

老妪见她悲伤，心中也大为难过，说道：“哦，我的孩子，我求你心里打起精神来，免得你死于忧愁，熬不到相会的机缘。谁知道你是不是在离他而去？不，依我看，你正是在朝他去，他也正朝着你来；因为倘若你守着你的老家、他守着他的老家，你们永远也不会相聚。”

少女只是哭泣。恰在这时，那伙人中的一个纵马凑到她们身旁，用长矛杆在老妪肩上敲了一下，喝令她住口，不许她再这样疯母鸡似地咯咯个没完。


===Original===

So they rode their ways till they had passed the straiter part of the pass that led through the fells, and there night began to fall on them (it was April-tide in those days); so the men-at-arms chose a place where was grass and water and three thick thorn-bushes, and made their harbour there. They took some pains to dight a shelter for the Maiden by spreading cloths betwixt a thorn and their spears stuck into the ground, but to the Carline, as was like, they gave no heed. But she laid her down peaceably within call of her dear fosterling, muttering as her head fell back: Here at any rate it is over-soon; let us get out of the mountains first. So they slept, yea, even the Maiden amidst her grief, so weary as she was. And when morning was they fared on, after a short tarrying for breakfast, whereof they gave of the best they had to the Maiden, but nought at all to the Carline. Nevertheless, when her fosterling fed her kindly from her abundance they naysaid it not.

This day is nought to tell of: toward sunset they came out of the mountains into a very fair green plain, wherein were neat and sheep a many; but though there were not a few houses of the herdsmen about, they made not for any of them, but took harbour in a little copse by a stream-side, and supped of such meat as they had; save that the two of them rode out into the plain and drove back with them a milch-cow, which they milked then and there for the Maiden’s behoof.

The next day they rode across the plain, and here and there fell in with some of the herdsmen by the way; but small greeting passed betwixt them, and the country-folk seemed well pleased that the men-at-arms had little to say to them. Before evening was they rode off the plain and into a land of little hills and streams, with green meadows for the most part, but here and there a little tillage, and a good many houses, yet these but the cots of husbandmen. This day they rode long and late, yea, till it had been dark night but for the rising of the moon upon them. At last said one of the men to another: “We shall not do it tonight; let us rest, and come in fresh a morning-tide.” So again that night they had the shelter of the trees and fields, but on the morrow betimes they were up and rode forward.

===Chinese===

他们就这样赶路，直到穿过那条穿越山地的隘路最狭窄的一段；这时夜幕开始向他们压下来（那时节正是四月）；于是甲士们拣了一处有草有水、还有三丛茂密荆棘的地方，在那里歇宿扎营。他们颇费了些手脚为少女张设安身的棚帐：在一丛荆棘和插进地里的长矛之间张开布幔；至于老妪，他们自然是不放在心上的。她却安然躺下，就睡在她心爱的养女听得见呼唤的地方，头往后一仰，口中喃喃道：“在这儿总还嫌太早；且先出了这山地再说。”于是众人都睡了，是的，连少女也忍着悲伤睡了，她实在累极了。天亮后他们略停了停，用过早饭便又上路；早饭他们把身边最好的吃食给了少女，却一点儿也不给老妪。不过，当她的养女亲切地从自己充裕的份里喂她时，他们倒也没有拦阻。

这一天没有什么可说的：将近日落时分，他们出了群山，来到一片非常美丽青翠的平原，原上牛羊成群；虽然四下里牧人的房舍不少，他们却一处也不投奔，只在溪边一座小树丛里歇了脚，就着随身的吃食用了晚饭；只是他们中有两人驰进平原，赶了一头奶牛回来，当场挤了奶给少女享用。

第二天他们横穿平原，一路不时遇上几个牧人；但彼此几乎不打招呼，乡人瞧着甲士们不同他们多话，倒像是乐得如此。天色未晚，他们便驰下平原，进入一片小丘与溪流之地，大多是青青草甸，间或有一小片耕作，房舍也不少，却都只是庄稼人的茅舍。这一天他们骑得又久又晚，是啊，若不是月亮升起来照着他们，早已是沉沉黑夜了。末了，其中一人对另一人说：“今夜是赶不到了；咱们歇了吧，明日清晨精神饱满地进发。”于是这一夜他们又借树木田野栖身；次日一早，天色未明，他们便起身赶路了。

## LV / 五十五

===Original===

The Blue Knight Buys the Maiden of the Chapman

They had ridden scarce a three hours ere they came through a cleft in the hills which here were grown somewhat higher and straiter, on to a very fair little valley, well-grassed, and with a stream of clear water running through it; and amidst of the said valley a fair white pavilion pitched, but no coat-armour done thereon. Then quoth one of the men to the Carline: “Lo, dame, how likest thou the sight of our master’s journey-house? Meseems in an hour’s time thou shalt be well on thy journey to hell.” The other men laughed, but the Carline answered them nought.

So down they went, and as they drew nigher they saw a tall black-bearded man standing before the tent door, and presently knew him for the chapman who had been such an ill guest to them at their own house. And the Maiden quaked and turned pale at the sight of him. But the Carline spake to her under her breath and said: “Fear not, we shall not abide long with this one.” Now he came forward to meet them; but when he saw the Carline he cried out wrathfully to his men and said: “Why have ye brought this accursed hag with you over all these many miles of way? Now must she be hewn down here, and her carcase will lie stinking at our door.” The men said nought, but sat in their saddles staring stupidly at him. But the Carline looked him hard in the face, and again made that muttering and the passing of her hands to and fro. The chapman said nought for a while, and then he spoke in a lower voice, wherein his pride seemed abated, and said: “Well, after all, the damsel must needs have some woman to wait upon her, and this one shall serve our turn for the present. Ho ye! come and take these women off their horses, and take them into the inner tent and give them to eat, and let them rest.” Then came forward two serving-men, who bore short-swords by their sides, and led the Carline and the Maiden through the big tent into the lesser one, and there brought water for their hands, and then victual and drink, and waited on them with honour; and the Carline laughed and said: “Lo my dear, here am I an honoured guest instead of a stinking corpse. Seest thou, the old woman is still good for something, and always to serve thee and help thee, my dear.” Then the Maiden kissed the Carline and caressed her, not without tears, and presently, being very weary with the way and the sorrow, laid her down on the bed and fell asleep. But the Carline sat watching heedfully all that went on, setting her eye to the default between the cloths of the tent, so that she could see all that was toward in the big tent, and somewhat the goings-on without.

Now it must be said the chapman, for as eager as had been his lust after the Maiden when he saw her at her house, found it somewhat abated when he saw her lighted down from her nag at his tent door. Forsooth she was worn with the travel, and yet more with the overmuch sorrow, so that she looked wan and haggard, and he said to himself that of all her beauty there was nought but the eyes of her left. But he thought: Let her rest a little, and be by herself if she will, and have good and pleasant meat and drink, and not be worried and troubled; and I will withhold the heat of my longing, and then in a day or two it will all come back again. So he bade his varlets deal with her as ye have heard, and suffered her to have the fellowship of the Carline her friend.

===Chinese===

蓝骑士从货郎手中买下少女

他们骑行了不过三个钟头，便穿过丘陵间的一道裂口——这一带的丘陵已长得略见高峻、略见逼窄——来到一条十分秀美的小谷，谷中牧草丰美，一道清溪穿流其间；谷地正中扎着一顶漂亮的白色大帐，帐上却不见张挂什么纹章。这时随从中一人对老妪说道：“夫人，你瞧我们主人这处行辕，可还入眼？依我看，再过一个钟头，你就要稳稳当当踏上通往地狱的旅程了。”其余的人都大笑，老妪却一言不答。

于是他们驰下谷去；将近帐前，只见一个高大黑须的汉子立在帐门口，众人立刻认出，这正是当初在他们自家宅子里做过一回恶客的那货郎。少女一见他，吓得发抖，脸色煞白。老妪却附在她耳边低声道：“别怕，咱们在这人手里待不了多久。”那货郎此时迎上前来；可他一见老妪，便怒气冲冲地呵斥手下人道：“你们为何把这该死的老妖婆一并带来，叫她跟着跑了这许多英里的路？如今只好把她砍翻在这里，叫她的尸首臭在我们的门口。”手下人一言不发，只坐在鞍上呆呆地瞪着他。老妪却紧紧盯住他的脸，又作起那番喃喃咒语，双手来回比划。货郎半晌无言，随后放低了声气——话音里的骄横似乎消减了几分——说道：“也罢，姑娘身边到底总得有个妇人伺候，眼下便让她顶这个用吧。喂，你们！来把这两个女人扶下马，领进里帐去，给她们吃的，让她们歇息。”随即走上前来两名仆役，腰间挎着短剑，领着老妪和少女穿过大帐进了小帐，先捧上净手的水，随后呈上酒饭，殷勤侍候。老妪笑道：“你瞧，亲爱的，我如今成了上宾，不是一具发臭的死尸了。你看明白了罢，这老婆子到底还有几分用处，总能伺候你、帮扶你，我的亲爱的。”少女吻了老妪，抚爱着她，不免落下泪来；不多时，她因一路辛苦、又伤心过度，便倒在床上睡着了。老妪却坐着，留心察看帐中一切动静，把眼睛凑在帐布之间的缝隙上，大帐里正在发生的事，她便能一览无余，帐外的动静也可略见几分。

却说那货郎，当初在少女家中见到她时，欲火何等炽烈；如今见她在帐门前从马背上下来，却觉得那股欲火已消减了几分。实在说，她已被旅途磨得憔悴，更被过度的悲伤磨得枯槁，形容苍白消瘦；他心里暗想，她那通身的美貌，如今只剩得一双眼睛了。可他又想道：且让她歇息片刻，她愿独处便由她独处，给她可口的好酒好饭，莫去搅扰她的心绪；我且按住胸中欲念的热火，过不了一两天，那一切自会卷土重来。于是他吩咐仆役们好生照应她——正如你们方才听到的那样——并许她同她的朋友老妪作伴。


===Original===

After this it befell that about noon the chapman and his men saw the riding of folk; so they looked to their weapons, and presently came riding up to the tent a Knight in bright armour, and two men-at-arms, and all of them right well arrayed. The Knight bore on his coat-armour wavy of blue and white, and he looked like to be a proper man of his hands.

Now when he had drawn rein at the tent door, and saw the men standing to their arms thereby, he seemed to be not thinking of battle with them, but he said: “The sele of the day to the men. Which of you is the master?” Then came forward the chapman, and sheathed his sword and said: “That am I, Sir Knight; and to make a long story short, I am no warrior or fighting man, but a merchant seeking gain from town to town and house to house. And I have some pretty things amongst my packs. Might I ask of your valour what thou wouldst have of me?”

The Knight, who by this time was off his horse, laughed and said: “Well, first we three would have meat and drink of you, and some horse-meat also, for we have ridden far this morning; and next, meseems, after what thou hast said, that it would help the victual down if I were to turn over some of those dear-bought and far-fetched wares of thine, even if I have to pay for peeping.”

Who then was was full of smiles and soft words save the chapman; he bade the Knight into his tent most sweetly, and set his folk to dighting a noble dinner. The Knight entered and did off his basnet, and showed a well-looking face, with good grey eyes like a hawk, and dark hair curling close to his head; there was nought cruel or base to be seen in his visage, though it had the fierceness of the warrior. So they sat down to meat, and talked the while of their eating; and a good deal of their talk was concerning the Knight of Longshaw, Sir Godrick, and his uprising, and what his chances might be of his outfacing all his foes, who, said the chapman, were many and great, and more belike than Sir Godrick wotted of. Quoth he: “And glad shall I be if he be overborne: for what should a knight do, to set him up against great and noble men, and wage all kinds of rascaile on behoof of a set of villeins and handicraftsmen!” And he looked on his guest as if he deemed he should please him by that word; but the other shook his head and said: “So should I not be glad; for Sir Godrick is both fearless and wise, and of good heart to such as need help. Yet I doubt me that he will be overthrown at last, such might as is arrayed against him. Forsooth could he get to him two or three like to himself, yea, or were it only one, then might he endure; but where shall he find such an one?”

===Chinese===

此后，约莫正午时分，货郎和他的手下望见有人骑马前来；他们便各自检视兵刃。不多时，一位身披鲜明甲胄的骑士驰到帐前，随行两名甲士，一行人人马鲜明、齐整精良。那骑士的纹章罩袍上是蓝白相间的波纹，看模样，是个武艺精熟的了得汉子。

骑士在帐门前收缰勒马，见众人持械立在近旁，却似乎并不想同他们厮杀，只说道：“诸位好汉，敬祝日安。你们中间哪一位是主人？”于是货郎走上前来，还剑入鞘，说道：“正是在下，骑士先生。长话短说，我不是武士，也不是战阵中人，不过是个商客，从一镇到一镇、一家到一家地逐利谋生。我的货担里倒有些精巧的玩意。敢问阁下，您要我效什么劳？”

这时骑士已下了马，闻言笑道：“好，头一件，我们三个要向你讨些饭食酒水，再添些马料——今儿早上我们赶了好些路；第二件，依我看，听你方才的话，我若把你那些重价搜求、远道贩来的货色翻检一番，倒能助着把饭食咽下肚去——哪怕只是瞧上一瞧便须破费，也是甘愿的。”

这时满面堆笑、满口软话的，不是货郎又是谁？他把骑士甜甜地让进帐中，吩咐手下张罗一席丰盛的正餐。骑士进帐，摘下战盔，露出一张眉目端正的脸：一双灰色的鹰眼，一头贴着头皮鬈曲的黑发；那面容虽带着武人的剽悍，却看不出半点残忍或卑鄙之相。于是两人入席用饭，边吃边谈；他们的话，多半说的是朗肖的骑士戈德里克爵士和他的起事，说他抗拒那许多仇敌、撑持到底的胜算能有几分——照货郎说来，那些仇敌人多势大，只怕比戈德里克爵士自己知道的还多。货郎道：“他若被压倒，我才高兴呢：一个骑士，去同那些高贵的大人物作对，为一伙庄稼汉和手艺人雇下各路流窜匪徒，这算哪门子营生！”说话时，他眼望着客人，只道这话必能讨他欢心；那一位却摇头道：“我却不会高兴；因为戈德里克爵士既无畏又多智，待有急需的人又一片热心。只是与他作对的大军这般强盛，我疑心他终归要倾覆。倘若他身边能得两三个同他一样的人物——哪怕只得一个——他便撑得住；可叫他往哪里寻这样的人去？”


===Original===

Quoth the chapman: “If ye bear the man such love and honour, mightest not thyself give thyself to him and be such an one to him as thou tellest of?” The Knight laughed: “Chapman,” said he, “of such mere skull-splitters as I be hath he enough amongst his men-at-arms, who, I must tell thee, be nowise rascaile, but valiant and well-ordered warriors. What he needeth is one fulfilled of the wisdom of war; yea, and of peace also, so as to know when to hold fast and when to let go, when to press hard on the foe and when to cast the golden bridge before them. Of such wisdom have I nought, and know little but of hard hitting and how to keep the face to the foe in the stour. Moreover, though in a way I wish him goodhap, yet it is such goodhap as one wishes a man who needs be a foe. For I must tell thee that I am of the Barons’ company and against Sir Godrick. Yet this I know, that if he fall at the last it shall not be till after he hath put us to the worse more than once or twice.”

Herewith their talk turned else-whither; but all this the Carline heard, and stored it up in her breast, and thought that she might hereafter get more tidings of Sir Godrick, and belike piece one thing to another till she had got somewhat which should be to her purpose.

So when they had done dinner the chapman opened some of his packs before the Knight (who is here called the Blue Knight), and the Knight cheapened here an ouch and there a finger-ring or a gold chain, and a piece of Saracen silk, and so forth; and all these he paid for down on the nail in pennies good and true, for he had with him a big pouch of money. Said he: “Thou seest I am rich in spending-silver, for I have been paid the ransom of three knights whom I took in sharp stour last autumn.”

But now as he was sitting turning over his fairings, a tidings befell. For the Carline, having well considered the looks of the Knight and having hearkened heedfully his speech, deemed that deliverance might come of him from the sordid wretch who had stolen the Maiden. So while the two were yet at table she roused her fosterling, and dight her attire as seemly as she might, and tired her hair and made it smooth and sleek; and just as the Blue Knight was about doing his marketings together, she brought the Maiden to the entry between the two tents and bade her stand there, and then drew the hangings apart to right and left and let the Maiden stand there as in a picture. The Knight looked up and saw it, and stared astonished, and was wordless a while; the chapman scowled, but durst not say aught, for he knew not how the Knight would take it; and as for the Knight, he leaned across to the chapman and spake to him softly, not taking his eyes off the Maiden the while: “Chapman, wilt thou tell me what this is, this wonder of women? Whether it is a queen of some far country, or an image made by wizardry?” The chapman, taken at unawares, had no lie handy, so he said: “This is my war-taken thrall, and she hath been with me some three hours.” Said the Knight, still speaking softly: “Thy thrall! Then mayst do with her what thou wilt. Tell me wilt thou not sell her, and to me?”

===Chinese===

货郎道：“你既这般敬爱这人，何不自家投到他麾下，做你所说的那样一个人呢？”骑士笑了：“货郎，”他说，“像我这等只会砸碎脑袋的莽汉，他手下的甲士里已尽够了——我还须告诉你，那些甲士绝非流窜匪徒之流，尽是英勇威猛、进退有法的战士。他所需要的，是一个满腹兵机韬略之人；而且还要兼通太平之道，知道何时该紧握、何时该放手，何时该对敌穷追猛打、何时该在敌人面前抛一座金桥。这样的智慧我一概没有，除了狠命砍杀、在战阵中面向敌人不转身之外，所知寥寥。再者，虽说我也可以盼他好运，但那好运，也不过如人们对一个势必为敌之人所盼的那一种。因为我须告诉你，我是男爵同盟一伙的人，与戈德里克爵士作对。不过有一点我知道：他即便终归败亡，也必先叫我们吃上不止一两回的败仗。”

说罢，话头便转到别处去了；可这些话老妪全都听见了，一一记在心里，心想日后或能再打听到些关于戈德里克爵士的消息，再把一件件事拼缀起来，也许便能凑出些合乎她用途的东西。

正餐既毕，货郎便在骑士面前打开了货担中的几包——这里的骑士，就是所称的蓝骑士——蓝骑士这边讲价买下一枚饰扣，那边又讲价买下一枚指环，还有一条金链、一幅萨拉森绸子，等等；这些他一概当场付讫，用的是足色的好便士，因为他身边带着一大袋银钱。他说道：“你瞧，我零花的银子多着呢；去年秋天一场恶战里，我擒了三名骑士，他们的赎金都已付到我的手上。”

可就在他坐着摆弄那些新买的玩意时，出了一桩事。原来老妪细看那骑士的相貌，又留心听他的言语，断定这人或能把少女从掳她的那个下流恶棍手里救出来。于是趁两人还在席上，她唤醒养女，尽力把她穿戴得齐整可人，又替她梳好头发，抿得光滑柔顺；恰在蓝骑士要把这笔笔买卖收拢成交的时候，老妪把少女领到两帐之间的过道口，叫她立在那里，随后把帷幔向左右一分，让少女立在那里，宛如画中人一般。骑士抬头望见，惊得直瞪双眼，一时说不出话来；货郎沉下脸，却不敢作声，因为他不知骑士会作何想。至于那骑士，则侧身凑近货郎，柔声问他，眼睛却始终不离少女：“货郎，你可愿告诉我，这是什么人，这女子中的奇珍？她是一位远邦的王后呢，还是法术造出来的一尊偶像？”货郎冷不防被问住，一时寻不出谎话，只得说：“这是我战阵中掳来的奴虏，到手才约莫三个钟头。”骑士仍是轻声说：“你的奴虏！那你便可任意处置她了。告诉我，你可愿把她卖了——卖给我？”


===Original===

The chapman was somewhat slow to answer, for he feared the Knight, and durst not buy the slaking of his lust with the peril of death. And moreover he deemed it a thing to be looked for that, if he sold her not, the bold Knight would take her from him perforce, so that he should lose both wealth and woman. Again, it came into his mind that if he sold her he might yet take an occasion to steal her again; so he said in a surly voice: “I took her not to sell her again, but to keep her and make her one of my household.”

“Yea,” said the Knight, “and wilt thou bring her to the church and wed her before the priest with ring and book?”

The chapman answered nought, and the Knight held his peace a while; but presently he spoke to the Maiden kindly, and said: “Sweet maiden, wouldst thou draw nigher to me, for I would speak with thee?” Then she left the fold of the tent and came and stood before him with no fear in her eyes.

Said the Blue Knight: “Tell me, fair damsel, is it true what this man says, that thou art his war-taken thrall?” Said she: “Three days ago I was stolen from mine own home by this man’s servants while the stout men of my folk were in battle with a sort of reivers who had fallen on our land. How might we defend us, two weak women against three weaponed men?”

===Chinese===

货郎半晌才答话：他惧怕那骑士，不敢拿性命之险去买他那淫欲的一晌满足。再者，他也想到，若是不卖，那大胆的骑士多半会硬把她夺了去，叫他人财两空。他又转念：若是卖了，日后还怕寻不出机会再把她偷回来？于是他粗声粗气地说道：“我掳她来不是为了转卖，是要留下她，叫她做我家宅里的人。”

“是么，”骑士道，“那你可愿领她上教堂，在神父面前凭戒指和圣书同她成婚？”

货郎无言可对；骑士也静了片刻，随即和颜悦色地对少女说道：“好姑娘，你可愿走近我些？我有话要同你说。”于是她走出帐幔的遮掩，上前立在他面前，眼中没有半点惧色。

蓝骑士道：“告诉我，好姑娘，这人的话可是实情——你是他战阵中掳来的奴虏？”她答道：“三天之前，趁我族中的健儿正同一伙犯境抄掠之徒厮杀，这人的仆役把我从我自家掳了来。两个弱女子对三个带兵刃的汉子，教我们如何自卫？”


===Original===

“Wert thou thrall or free before that day, damsel?” said the Knight. She flushed red, and said: “Never has there been an unfree man of our blood for generation after generation.” Said the Knight: “Now thou art here in this man’s tent, wilt thou go with him freely and of thine own will, if he swear to thee to take thee into his household and deal honestly by thee?” She reddened again: “But he will not deal honestly by me, lord,” she said, “and never will I go with him uncompelled.” “How knowest thou that he is not a true man?” said the Knight. “Fair sir,” she said, “hast thou looked in the face of him? Look now with what eyes he is beholding me!”

The Blue Knight was silent a while; then he said, but halted in his speech: “And with me﻿—wouldst thou go with me of thine own free will, if I swore to deal with thee in all honour?”

“Yea,” she said, “or without the swearing if thou make me the same offer after I have said a word to thee; to wit, that there is a young and goodly man whom I love, and he me again. And now I have lost him, and know not how to come to him, but I will seek him the world over till I find him, and he me: and if I find him not, then never shall I come into any man’s arms in this world. What sayest thou now?”

The Knight rose up and walked to and fro a while, casting a look on the chapman every now and then. At last he came to the Maiden, and said to her in a low voice: “I make thee the same offer, and will swear to thee on my father’s sword, which here is.” She looked on him, and the tears came into her eyes: nor forsooth were they very far from his. But she said: “This goes with it, that thou take along with thee my foster-mother, who is hereby, and suffer her to be ever with me if I will.” “That is soon yeasaid,” quoth he. Then he set her down in his chair, and said: “Fear nothing, I will see to this matter straightway.”

===Chinese===

“在那一天之前，姑娘，你是奴籍还是自由之身？”骑士问。她臊红了脸，说道：“我们这一脉血脉，世世代代从不曾出过不自由的人。”骑士道：“如今你既在这人的帐中，他若向你起誓，收你进他的家宅、以诚相待，你可愿凭自己的意愿，自由地随他去？”她的脸又红了：“可他不会以诚待我，老爷，”她说，“我决不受人强迫随他去。”“你怎么知道他不是个正派人？”骑士道。“好先生，”她说，“你可曾端详过他的脸？你且看看，他此刻正用什么眼睛望着我！”

蓝骑士默然片刻，随后开口，语声却有些迟疑：“那么随我——你可愿随我去，凭你自己的意愿，只要我起誓以全副的体面待你？”

“愿意，”她说，“而且不必起誓，只要你听我说完一句话，仍肯把方才的话向我应承：那就是，有一位年轻俊美的男子，我爱他，他也爱我。如今我失了他，不知怎样才能到他身边；可我要走遍天涯海角寻他，直到寻见为止，他也必寻我：我若寻他不着，今生今世便再不会投入任何男子的怀抱。这话你听了，如今怎么说？”

骑士站起身来，来回踱了一阵，时时把眼光扫向货郎。末了，他走到少女跟前，低声对她说：“我把方才的话向你应承，并愿凭我父亲的剑向你起誓——剑就在这里。”她望着他，泪水涌上了眼睛：说实话，他自己的双眼离泪水也不远了。但她说道：“这应承还须附带一事：你要把我的养母一并带走，她就在近旁；只要我愿意，就得容她永远伴随我。”“这个好说。”他应道。随后他让她坐在自己的椅子上，说道：“什么也别怕，我这就去把这件事办妥。”


===Original===

Then he turned to the chapman, who sat scowling on the Maiden, and said: “Now, chapman, wilt thou sell me thy thrall as thou hast sold me those pretty things?” The other answered him not a while, and the Knight said: “Nay, it avails nought to draw faces at me; one way or the other the thing can soon be settled. For look to it, that thy war-taken thrall may be mine by the same title. There are weapons enough hereby, and ye are five and we three; and thou shalt arm thee, or I will unarm me to my kirtle and my sword, and then let us out on to the green and fight for the Maiden.” The chapman said: “I see thou wilt take her perforce; so give me her price: but take heed that I sell her not uncompelled. And thou who hast eaten and drunk with me!”

“I would I might vomit up thy victuals,” said the Knight angrily; “for then I knew not that it was thy wont to carry off free women from their houses while other folk were fighting. But I will have no more words with thee, save this, that thou shalt sell me also two of thy nags, that we may all ride and be away hence the speedier. Ho Robert, go thou and take two fresh horses of the chapman and saddle them straightway.”

Now the chapman named his price, and it was a big one indeed, no less than an earl’s ransom; but the Blue Knight but nodded his head in token of yeasay, and the chapman said: “I suppose thou wilt not have all that gold in thy scrip; but thou mayst take thy bargain away, for as violently and strifefully as thou hast dealt with me, if thou wilt send the money in one month’s frist to the hostelry of the Wool-pack in the good town of Westcheaping hard by here, and let thy bearer ask for Gregory Haslock to give him quittance. But for thine ill-dealings with me I shall give thee no quittance, but shall watch my turn to do thee a service.”

The Knight said all shortly: “I shall send thy money as thou biddest;” and then turned away from him, and took the Maiden by the hand and led her out of the tent, and the Carline followed them. So they gat to horse and rode their ways. But so it was that the Carline rode the last of them; and when they were gone but a few yards the chapman ran to the tent door with a bent bow in his hand and an arrow notched to the string, and drew on the said Carline, who was but some ten yards from him by then. But, whether it were the caitiff’s evil shooting or the Carline’s wizardry, ye must choose between the two, the arrow flew wide of the mark, and the Carline laughed merrily as she rode along. Thus were those two quit of this felon for that time.

===Chinese===

于是他转向货郎——那货郎正坐在那里对少女怒目而视——说道：“如今，货郎，你可愿把你的奴虏卖给我，就像把那些精巧玩意卖给我一样？”那一位半晌不答，骑士便道：“不必对我摆脸色，那没有用处；这事走一条路也好、走另一条路也好，很快便能了断。你须想到，你凭战阵掳来的奴虏，我也可以用同样的名目掳到手。近旁兵刃尽有，你们五人，我们三人；你把甲胄穿起来吧——或者我把甲胄脱到只剩长袍和佩剑——然后我们到外面草地上去，为这少女决一胜负。”货郎说：“我明白了，你是要硬夺她去；那就把她的身价给我：只是你须知，我卖她并非甘愿。亏你还同我同桌吃喝过！”

“我恨不得把你那些饭食都吐出来，”骑士怒道，“当时我可不知道，你的惯技原来是趁别人厮杀的时候，从人家里把自由身的女子掳走。不过我也不再多同你废话，只除一件事：你还须再卖我两匹驽马，我们好都上了马，快些离开此地。喂，罗伯特，你去从货郎的马里牵两匹精神的好马，立刻备上鞍子。”

于是货郎开了价，那价码着实不小，抵得上一名伯爵的赎身；蓝骑士却只点了点头，表示应允。货郎便说：“我料想你那褡裢里也装不下这许多金子；只是你虽这般强横凶横地同我做成了买卖，我仍容你把货带走——只要你肯在一个月限期内，把钱送到近旁西集城里的‘羊毛包’客店，叫你的送钱人寻格雷戈里·哈斯洛克，由他交回收讫的字据。只是你这样恶待我，我却不会给你什么收讫；我要等着时机，好好回敬你一番。”

骑士只简短答道：“钱必依你所嘱送到。”说罢便转过身去，携起少女的手，领她出了帐幕，老妪也随他们出来。于是众人上马登程。偏生老妪走在最后；一行出去不过几步远，货郎便手挽弯弓、弦上搭箭，奔到帐门口，对着老妪开弓——那时她离他不过十来步远。可是，或许是那卑怯恶徒的箭法太臭，或许是老妪的法术灵验——这两条只好由你们自择其一——那支箭远远偏离了目标，老妪一路骑去，纵声大笑。这一来，那两人便就此暂且脱开了这恶徒的手。

## LVI / 五十六

===Original===

The Blue Knight Talks with the Maiden by the Way

The Blue Knight rode beside the Maiden, and it could be seen that in all ways he would take care of her and give her honour; but he was few-spoken at first, nor for a while had she much mind to speak. But after a little she looked on him aside, and seemed to think that he would be fain were she to cast a word to him. And she herself was grown of good cheer now, for she deemed herself delivered from captivity; and, however it were, she trusted in this man’s good faith and kindness. So she asked him some simple question about the way, and he started when he heard her voice, but turned and answered her frankly, and seemed as if he had liked it better if he might have made more of it. Then she said: “Fair sir, thou hast not yet told me whither we be going.”

“Nay,” he said, “that is true, and heedless it was of me, and I pray thee pardon me. We be bound for the Castle of Brookside, which is my chiefest manor house, though no great thing. But we shall not be there tonight, nor for many nights. Now if thou ask me what we shall find there, I shall tell thee that beside the serving-men and a few men-at-arms and sergeants, and three squires, thou shalt find little save my mother there, for I am unwedded as yet.”

===Chinese===

蓝骑士途中与少女交谈

蓝骑士骑行在少女身旁，看得出他处处留心照护她、敬重她；只是起初他寡言少语，她也有好一阵子不大想开口。可过了一小会儿，她斜眼觑他，觉得他倒像是很乐意她抛一句话给他。而她自己如今心情也已宽舒，只道自己已脱了囚困；再说无论如何，她信得过这人的忠厚与善良。于是她拣了一句关于路途的闲话问他；他听见她的声音，微微一怔，却回过头来坦然作答，那神气倒像是恨不得把这话说得更长久些才好。于是她说道：“好爵士，你还不曾告诉我，咱们这是要往哪里去呢。”

“是啊，”他说，“这话不假，是我疏忽了，求你恕我。咱们此去是溪畔堡，那是我头一份的庄园宅子，虽说算不得什么了不得的地方。可咱们今夜到不了那里，往后好几夜也到不了。你若问我在那儿能见到什么人，我便告诉你：除了家仆、几名甲士和军士、再有三名侍从之外，你差不多只能见到我的母亲，因为我至今尚未娶亲。”


===Original===

At that word the Maiden fell silent again, for she was wondering what like would be the Knight’s mother, and what days she was like to make for her. But presently she set all that aside, and fell to ask the Knight of other matters, such as the fashion of the countryside and the ways of the folk round about his castle, and freely he answered to everything; and so at last began to ask her concerning her land and folk, and her way of life, and she told him of all freely. But no word did she say to him of the man whom she loved; nay, when the talk seemed drawing near to such a point that it seemed he must be told of presently, she would break off and hold her peace straightway; neither did the Knight say aught, nor ask her wherefore she went not on with her tale, but let speech be till the spring thereof began to run again of its own will.

Thus then they wore the day, riding through a fair country of husbandry, not very thickly housed. None meddled with them, till at sunset they came to a goodly grange walled and moated; and the Blue Knight said: “If we take not harbour here we shall have to lie out in the field, for we shall fall in with no other house till the night is well deep.” Therewith he rode up to the door and lighted down, and so did they all; and there came forth a tall and somewhat goodly man of some fifty winters and bade Welcome, Sir Mark! And without more ado they entered the hall, which was fair and big and well-plenished. There presently they were feasted by the goodman and his sons and his folk, for Sir Mark the Blue Knight was well known to the said goodman. In due time withal the Maiden was shown to a fair chamber well hung and with a good bed therein, wherein she slept sweet without dreams. So was the ending of that day better than the beginning. They took to their road betimes on the morrow, and two of the goodman’s sons and three of his men rode with them, well armed; for though this was a peopled part, yet whiles reivers rode therein. But on the way the Blue Knight excused him to the Maiden for suffering this eking of his army, and he said: “Seest thou, lady, were I with my two lads here, or even were I riding birdalone, I would have bidden these five good fellows abide at home; but I fear for thee, lest the fewness of our company should draw on this rascaile to come within smiting distance, and then who knows what might betide? For a chance stroke might do all the scathe at once, and make me an unhappy man till the end of my days.”

She smiled on him friendly and said: “Sir Knight, there is no need to excuse thee; trust me I am nowise greedy of battle, and thank thee heartily for thinking of me.”

===Chinese===

听了这话，少女又沉默下来，因为她心里在琢磨：骑士的母亲是怎样一个人，往后会给她什么样的日子过。但她随即把这一切撇开，转而问起骑士别的种种，比如乡野的风物、他城堡左近一带人们的习俗；他样样都答得爽快。到后来，他倒问起她的土地和亲人、她的度日之道来，她便也无甚隐瞒，尽数告诉了他。只是关于她所爱的那人，她对他一字不提；不，每当话头仿佛快要逼到那一步、眼看转眼就要说到那人时，她便立即打住，当下缄口；骑士也一言不发，也不问她为何不把话讲下去，只由着话头搁着，直到那话的泉源自己重新流淌起来。

他们就这样消磨过这一天，一路骑过一片美好的农耕之乡，只是人烟不算稠密。无人前来搅扰，直到日落时分，他们来到一座很好的庄寨前，有围墙环护、壕沟围绕；蓝骑士说：“咱们若不在此投宿，便只得露宿野地里了，因为夜色深透之前，再撞不上一处人家。”说着他便驰到门前，翻身下马，众人也都下了马；里面出来一个身材高大、颇为体面的男子，约摸五十个冬天，口称“欢迎，马克爵士！”众人不再多礼，径自进了厅堂；那厅堂宽敞漂亮，陈设齐备。当下庄主便同他的儿子们、他的仆从们设宴款待他们，因为蓝骑士马克爵士是这位庄主素来相识的。到了时辰，少女也被引到一间上好的内室，壁上挂饰齐整，屋里有一张舒坦的床，她在那床上睡得香甜，一夜无梦。这一天，结局倒比开头来得好。次日他们一早登程，庄主的两个儿子和三名仆从披挂齐整，随他们同行；因为这一带虽有人烟，却时而也有抄掠之徒骑行出没。路上，蓝骑士为容自己这支队伍添了几个人，向少女告罪，说道：“小姐你瞧，若只是我带着身边这两个小伙子，甚或是我独自单骑，我原会叫这五个好汉子守在家里；可我为你担着心，惟恐咱们人马单薄，引得那些流窜匪徒凑到兵刃够得着的地方来，那时谁知道会出什么事？因为一记乱下的打击，兴许一下子便把祸事做全了，叫我做个伤心人，直到我老死那一天。”

她对他友好地嫣然一笑，说道：“骑士先生，你大可不必告罪；信我，我绝非贪恋厮杀之人，倒要真心谢谢你为我着想。”


===Original===

The Knight made as if he would have said something which would not come forth out of his mouth, and he turned very red, and so rode, but presently drew rein, and bade the others ride on and he would catch up with them. So they went on, and the Maiden would have ridden on also, but he said: “I beseech thee to abide with me, for I have a word or two to say to thee before we get on with this day’s journey.” She looked on him wonderingly, and was somewhat abashed, but turned to hearken to him, and he said, not speaking very glibly: “Thou thankest me for thinking of thee, but meseems I have nowise thought of thee enough. I have told thee that we be riding to my house of Brookside, but now I will ask thee if thou hast will to go thither?”

“Why not?” she said; “I deem not by thy looks and thy speech that thou wilt be hard or cruel with me, or do me wrong in any wise, or suffer others to do so.”

“Nay, by Allhallows,” said he; “but this I ask. Tell me right out if thou hast any will to go back to thine old home in the Dale. I beseech thee to tell me thy mind hereon; and if thou longest to go back, then will we turn bridle at once and seek to the stead where thou wert born and bred, and there will I say farewell to thee. For what! it may not be forever; I shall ride to see thee once and again, I promise thee.”

===Chinese===

骑士做出一副要说什么的样子，那话却出不了口；他脸涨得通红，只得闷头骑马，可不久便勒住缰绳，嘱咐其他人先行，说他自会赶上去。于是众人往前去了，少女也要跟着前行，他却说：“我求你留在我身边，趁今日的旅程还未继续，我有一两句话要对你说。”她诧异地望着他，微微有些羞窘，却还是回身听他说。他说得不甚利索：“你谢我为你着想，可依我看，我想你想得还万万不够。我告诉过你，咱们这是往我溪畔的宅子去；如今我要问你一句：你可愿意到那里去？”

“为什么不愿意？”她说，“看你的容貌，听你的言语，我不觉得你会对我严苛狠戾，会丝毫亏待我，或是容许旁人来亏待我。”

“绝不会，凭诸圣起誓，”他说，“可我要问的是这一件：请你直截了当告诉我，你可有一丝念头，想回谷地里你的老家去。我恳求你把心思明白告诉我；你若渴想着回去，那咱们这就拨转马头，径直寻你生身长成的那处庄舍去，我就在那里同你道别。这算得了什么！又不是今生今世永不再见；我答应你，我会一次又一次骑马来看你。”


===Original===

Now the Maiden flushed red and the tears gathered in her eyes, and she looked piteous-kind on him; but she said: “Thou art kind indeed; but that farewell in the Dale needeth not to be, for I have no will to go back home. Such an errand is laid on me that hath made me homeless now; for I must go seeking that which is lost, it may be, wide over the world; and if thou wilt shelter me a while in Brookside Castle I shall thank thee and bless thee as scarce a man hath yet been thanked since earth was new.”

The Knight hung down his head, but presently he raised it, and heaved a sigh as if a weight were lifted from his heart, and he said: “Let each of us take what content may be in the passing days.” Then he shook his rein, and they both sped on together till they caught up with their company.

That night they harboured at a husbandman’s cot, where was no room save for the two women, and the men lay out under the bare heaven, but all was done that might be for the easement of the Maiden. The franklin’s folk rode on with them on the morrow, and whereas they must needs wend a somewhat thick wood the more part of the day, they rode close, and had the Maiden in their midst, while the Blue Knight went the foremost of their company, and was as wary as might be. So whatever strong-thieves might have been lurking under cover of the thicket, they adventured them not against so stout and well-ordered a company, and they all came safely through the wood into a fair grassy valley some little time before sunset. But though the pasture was good there and the land well watered, there were no houses within sight, for it was overnigh to the wood for folk to venture their goods, yea and their lives, by dwelling in neighbourhood to such ill men as haunted the thickets of the forest. Wherefore this night all the company, women as well as men, must needs forego lying under rafters: albeit they dight some kind of tent within what cloths they had for the Maiden and her fosterer.

===Chinese===

这时少女脸上泛起红晕，眼泪涌上了眼眶，她凄然又温情地望着他；可她说：“你实在是好心；可谷地里那场道别是用不着的，因为我全然不想回家去。一桩差事落在我身上，如今已叫我没了家；因为我必须去寻找那失落的，兴许要寻遍天涯；你若肯收留我在溪畔堡将息些时日，我便会感激你、祝福你——自天地初开以来，怕还没有几个男子受过这样的感激和祝福呢。”

骑士垂下头去，可不久又抬了起来，长叹一声，仿佛心头卸下了一副重担，说道：“这流逝的日子里能得几分安稳，咱们便各自领受几分吧。”说罢他一抖缰绳，两人并辔疾驰，直到赶上他们的队伍。

这一夜他们宿在一户庄稼人的茅舍，那里除了两个女人再腾不出地方，男人们便露宿在光天之下；但凡能使少女安适的法子，都用尽了。次日，庄主家的人仍旧随他们同行；因为这一天大半路程都得穿行一片颇密的树林，他们便骑得紧，让少女居在队伍当中，蓝骑士则走在全队最前头，警觉得了不得。这样，任凭林莽掩护之下潜伏着什么样的剧盗，也不敢来招惹这般齐整雄壮的一队人马；日落前不多的时辰，他们全都安然穿过树林，进到一片美丽的青草谷地。只是那地方牧草虽好、水泽丰盈，放眼望去却无一户人家：因为它离树林太近，谁也不肯拿身家财物、乃至性命，去住得离那班出没林间的恶徒这般近。因此这一夜，全队人等，连女人带男人，都只得舍弃屋檐下的栖宿：不过他们还是拿手头所有的布幔，为少女和她的养母张设了一座简易的篷帐。


===Original===

The fourth day, as they rode the grassy fair valley, as it was noon, they saw somewhat aloof the riding of another company, which they deemed to be more than they. So they looked to their weapons and rode on steadily, but without haste, lest the others might deem they were fleeing them. So the others, when they had well espied their demeanour, passed on without meddling with them; and well-nigh the whole valley could be ridden, so there was nought to drive them to meet side by side in a strait road, wherefore they came not very nigh, but yet nigh enough to know the newcomers for such as would be evil way-fellows to any whom they feared not. As it was, the Blue Knight and his drew rein and turned a little toward them as they went by, to show that they feared them not, and Sir Mark rode forward before his folk and abode them with a sword in fist. But the newcomers did nought but set up a yelling and jeering, and rode on their way not over slowly.

Three hours thereafter they saw, a little mile aloof, a fair white house garnished with towers on a knoll, round about which ran a little river; so the Maiden, who was now again riding close beside the Blue Knight, asked him if that were Brookside, and he smiled and said: “Nay, my house is still five days’ ride away, but this house, which hight Warding Knowe, is the house of a friend, and there shall we have good guesting, whereof I rejoice for thy sake.” Then he was silent a while, and said thereafter: “Tell me, lady, dost thou wish those five days over?”

“Nay,” she said, “it is little matter to me where I am, and to say sooth, this riding through the fair land likes me well.”

===Chinese===

第四天，他们正骑行在那片青青美丽的谷地里，到了正午，望见稍远处另有一队人马在行进，看样子人数比他们还多。于是他们检视兵刃，从容前进，却不慌不忙，惟恐对方当他们是在逃走。对方把他们的这副气度瞧了个清楚，便自顾过去了，并不来搅扰；再说那谷地几乎处处可骑，没有什么逼得两队在窄路上并肩相撞，故此双方离得不很近，却又近得足以叫人认出来：这些新来的人，正是那种凡他们所不惧怕者，同他们打一路便决无好事的凶徒。当时的情形是：蓝骑士一行勒住缰绳，在对方经过时稍稍朝他们转了转，以示无所畏惧；马克爵士更纵马抢在自家人马之前，手握长剑，迎候他们。而那伙新来的人什么也没做，只发出一片吼叫嘲弄，不紧不慢地赶他们的路去了。

三个钟头之后，他们望见一英里开外，一座小冈上有一所漂亮的白房子，缀着几座角楼，一条小河绕着它流转；这时少女又骑到了蓝骑士近旁，问他那可是溪畔么，他微笑道：“不是，我的宅子离这里还有五天的骑程；不过这座名唤守望冈的宅子，是一位朋友的居所，咱们在那里会受到好好的款待——我为你欢喜。”随后他沉默了一阵，又说道：“告诉我，小姐，你可盼着这五天早些过完么？”

“不盼，”她说，“我在哪里都无甚要紧，说句实话，这样骑行在美好的国土上，我很是喜欢。”


===Original===

He sighed and said, yet slowly: “Well, for my part I would that the five days were fifty.” “Why?” she said heedlessly. He reddened and said: “I must needs tell thee since thou askest me. It is because I have got used to seeing these men and thy Carline about thee; neither does it irk me to see the folk that give us guesting gazing on thee or speaking to thee. But when we come to Brookside it will be all other than that; for there will be the folk all about, and some belike will make friends with thee; and there will be my mother. And look you, all and each of these folk shall have as much part and lot in thee as I shall have. Now, art thou angry that I have said this?”

“Nay,” she said, and knew not what more to say. And she looked at him covertly and saw grief and torment in him, and she was sorry for him. But within herself she said, Woe’s me! and how long it shall be belike ere I meet my beloved!

===Chinese===

他叹了口气，缓缓说道：“唉，至于我，倒宁愿这五天是五十天。”“为什么？”她随口问道。他红了脸，说道：“你既然问我，我便不能不告诉你。那是因为我已看惯了这些汉子和你那位老妪围在你身边；款待咱们的人家望着你、同你说话，我看在眼里也不难受。可等咱们到了溪畔，情形便全然两样了：那里四下里都是人，其中多半有些人会同你结好；还有我的母亲。你要知道，这些人个个都在你身上有一份，那一份额，都不下于我的一份。怎么样，我说了这话，你恼了么？”

“不恼，”她说，此外便不知再说什么好。她悄悄觑他，见他满心愁苦煎熬，心里为他难过。可她心中暗道：苦啊我！离我与我心爱之人相会，只怕还不知要多久呢！

## LVII / 五十七

===Original===

They Come to Brookside

They were not long ere they were before the gate of Warding Knowe, and the master thereof standing over against them, bidding them a free and fair welcome. He was well on in years, more than grizzled, but a stout and stark knight: he hight Sir Alwyn. He embraced Sir Mark as he got off his horse, for they were dear friends, and then looked keenly on the Maiden, and took her by the hand and led her in and treated her with all honour. Thereafter, before supper, while she was under the hands of the tire-women, the said lord took occasion to ask the Blue Knight if he had done well, so doing, or whether he should have given her less honour; and the Blue Knight said that he had done right well, and that he thanked him for it, for of all honour was she worthy.

Now the Maiden sat at table beside the lord and Sir Mark, and hearkened their talk, which at one time ran much upon that great captain of war whom they called Sir Godrick of Longshaw. And she might see of both of them that they thought much of his wisdom, and not little of his luck, and feared him what he would do to them of the Barons’ League, whereof were both those knights. And Sir Alwyn furthermore told the Blue Knight concerning tidings in the City of the Sundering Flood, and said that the King thereof was of little account before such a man as was Sir Godrick, for though he were well enough in a fray if the sword were put in his hand and the horse were between his knees, yet was he feathered-headed, stubborn in wrong, and hard-hearted. Said Sir Alwyn, that save the said King was in all things according with the best men of the City, as the Porte and the masters of the Great Crafts, he was undone.
