A tall man rose in his place, a grey cloak fell from him, and he was clad all in glittering armour, and there was none that did not know him for Osberne Wulfgrimsson, who has been called the Red Lad. And he said in a bold and free voice: “See, my masters and dear friends, if I have not kept tryst with you; for it is of a sooth five years well told since I departed from Wethermel with little hope in my heart. And now forsooth is no hope in my heart, for all the hope has budded and blossomed and fruited, and I am yours and ye are mine while the days last. And this is the woman that I have won; and O I would that it had been earlier, though God wot I laboured at it. And now I think ye will be good to her as ye will be good to me, and what tale shall there be except of peace and quiet in these faraway upland vales?”

So passed the hours into deep night at Wethermel, and folk went to sleep scarce trowing in the wonders that they had heard and seen. And there were few among them that did not long for the dawn and the daylight, that they might once again cast eyes upon Osberne and his beloved. And hard it were to say which of those twain was the loveliest. But surely about both of them there was then and always a sweet wisdom that never went beyond what was due and meet for the land they lived in or the people with whom they dwelt. So that all round them the folk grew better and not the worser.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩与埃尔弗希尔德向自家亲族亮明身份

话音落处，老妪坐了下来，厅中顿时一片欢腾喧嚷，众人纷纷猜想接下来还有什么好戏；但若说他们对已然降临之事毫无所觉，那也不是实话——他们心里多少都有了些影子。这时埃尔弗希尔德站起身来，褪下身上的灰衣，里面穿的竟是金绿二色、精细至极的华服；人人都说，厅堂之上从未见过这般美貌。有一阵子众人都屏住了呼吸，如同眼见一件奇珍，惟恐它转眼便要消逝。随即一声高呼震彻大厅——事情就此成了。一个高大的汉子在座中站起身来，一袭灰色披风从他身上滑落，露出周身灿亮的甲胄，竟没有一个人认不出他就是奥斯伯恩·伍尔夫格里姆森——那位人称红衣少年的。只听他以豪迈爽朗的声音说道：“看哪，诸位主人家、亲爱的朋友们，我这不是践了同你们的相会之约么？说句实话，自我怀着渺茫的指望离开韦瑟梅尔，屈指算来，足足五个年头了。如今我心里当真再无半点指望，因为所有的指望都已抽芽、开花、结出了果实；只要天长日久，我便是你们的，你们便是我的。这位便是我赢得的女子；啊，但愿这一天来得早些才好，虽然老天知道，我为此下了多少力气。如今我想，你们怎样善待我，也会怎样善待她；在这偏远的高地山谷之间，除了安宁清静的日子，还能讲出什么别的故事来呢？”

在韦瑟梅尔，时辰就这样过去，直到深夜；众人上床安歇时，还几乎不敢相信自己亲耳听到、亲眼见到的这些奇事。他们中间，很少有人不盼着破晓天明，好再一睹奥斯伯恩和他的爱人的丰采。要说这两人之中哪一个更可爱，那可难分高下。但千真万确，他们两人身上，从那时起直到永远，都有一种温厚的睿智，从不逾越他们所居的这片土地、所共处的这些乡人所应有的分寸。因此，在他们四周，人们都日渐向善，而非变坏。

## LXVI / 六十六

===Original===

The Lip of the Sundering Flood

When it was the morning and the sun shone through the house at Wethermel, those two arose and took each other by the hand, and no word they spake together, but went straight to the Sundering Flood, and there they walked slowly and daintily along the very lip thereof; and the day was the crown of all midsummer days, and it seemed to Elfhild that never on the other side had the flowers looked so fair and beautiful. So they went on till they came to the Bight of the Cloven Knoll, and there they looked across a while and yet said nothing. And Elfhild looked curiously toward that cave wherein Osberne first espied her, and she said: “How would it be if there were another one there?” He laughed and said: “There is not another one.” But she said: “Dost thou remember that game I played with the shepherd’s pipe, how that the sheep came all bundling towards me?” “Dearly I remember it,” said Osberne. “Now,” she said, “I will tell thee a thing. I have got the said pipe in my bosom now. It were good game to have it forth and try whether it has lost its power.” He said: “Well, try it.” She said: “Be there sheep about?” And there were sheep at no great distance.

And she drew forth the pipe and let her lips to it and played, and there came from it that very same sweet old tune that had joyed him so much long aforetime. But when they looked to see what would happen to the sheep, lo and behold they stirred not at all for the sweetness of the tune, nor made as if they heard it. So they laughed, albeit each of them, and Elfhild in especial, was a little grieved that the power had departed from the pipe. And they looked down towards the water, and Elfhild half thought to see a little brown man sitting at the door of the cave. But there was nothing; only it seemed to them both that there came up from the water a sound that said, Give it me back again. And Osberne said: “Didst thou hear that?” “Yea,” she said, “I thought I heard something. What shall we do?” Said he: “Why should he have his pipe back again?” She said: “Let us see what will happen if we cast it down to him.” “Good,” said Osberne, and he took the pipe, and as deftly as he might he cast it towards the mouth of the cave, but it fell a long way short.

But lo, as it was on the very point of striking the water it seemed that it was wafted up to the cave’s mouth, and it vanished away into the cave no slower than might have been looked for. And a faint voice came up from the water and said, I am pleased; good luck go with you.

So they sat down and pondered on these things a while, till at last Elfhild said: “Now will I tell thee a tale as in old days.” And he said: “That is good.” Then she began a tale which was sweet and pleasant, and little like to those terrible things that had happened to those two since they were sundered by the Flood. And it lasted long, and the afternoon was hot, and they were fain for coolness’ sake to creep into the shadow of certain bushes that grew a little off the lip of the Sundering Flood. There they rested them, and when the shadows began to lengthen, they arose and went back hand in hand to Wethermel as they had come.

===Chinese===

分离洪流的水唇

待到清晨，阳光照进韦瑟梅尔的宅屋，那两人便起身，彼此携了手，相对一言不发，径直往分离洪流去；到了那里，便贴着那道水唇缓缓而行，步履轻盈小心。这一日堪称历来仲夏诸日之冠；她只觉得，从前隔水相望时，对岸的花儿从来不曾显得这般鲜妍美丽。两人就这样一路行去，直到裂丘湾，在那里朝对岸眺望了一阵，仍旧相对无言。埃尔弗希尔德好奇地望向那座山洞——当初奥斯伯恩正是在那里头一回望见她的——开口说道：“倘若那里头还住着另一个，不知会怎样？”他笑道：“并没有另一个。”她却道：“你可记得我从前用牧笛玩的那个把戏？那些羊儿怎样一股脑儿全朝我拥来？”奥斯伯恩道：“我可是记得真切。”“喏，”她道，“我告诉你一件事。那支笛子如今就揣在我怀里呢。把它取出来试一试，看它的法力还在不在，倒是个好玩的把戏。”他道：“好，那就试试。”她道：“这左近可有羊儿么？”不远处正有一群羊。

于是她取出笛子，把嘴唇凑上去吹奏起来；笛中流出的，正是很久以前令他那样欢喜的那支甜蜜老调子。可当他们去看羊儿有何动静时，你道怎的，那曲子虽然甜美，羊儿竟纹丝不动，也不像是听见了的模样。两人便都笑了，只是彼此心里——埃尔弗希尔德尤其如此——都因笛子的法力已然消散而微微有些怅然。他们朝下望向水面，埃尔弗希尔德恍惚间只道会看见一个棕色的小人坐在山洞门口。却什么也没有；只是两人都仿佛觉得，水里传上来一个声音，说：“把它还给我吧。”奥斯伯恩道：“你听见了么？”她道：“听见了，我像是听见了点什么。咱们该怎么办呢？”他道：“他凭什么要讨回自己的笛子？”她道：“那便把它抛下去给他，看看会怎样。”奥斯伯恩道声“好”，接过笛子，用尽巧劲朝洞口掷去，却还差着老大一截。

但见那笛子眼看着就要落到水面，却仿佛被一股风托起，飘飘直上洞口，转眼便没入洞中，那份迅捷竟不输人意所料。随即水中传上来一个细微的声音，说道：“我心满意足；愿好运与你们同在。”

于是两人坐下来，对着这些奇事思量了一阵；末了埃尔弗希尔德说道：“如今我要像旧日那样，给你讲一个故事。”他道：“那再好不过。”她便讲起一个故事来，那故事甜美悦人，同他们两人自被洪流隔开以来所经历的种种可怖之事，几乎没有一点相像。故事讲了许久；午后天气炎热，两人贪着凉快，便挪进离分离洪流的水唇不远的几丛灌木的荫翳里。两人在那里安歇了身子；待到日影渐渐拉长，便起身手携手回韦瑟梅尔去，一如来时。

## LXVII / 六十七

===Original===

A Friend at Need

It was some three years after this that weaponed men came down into the Dale. It was told to Osberne, and he took his sword and went to meet them. He came across them as they fared slowly down the bent, looking weary and fordone. He looked at them, and he saw that there was nothing for it but that the chiefest of them, and there were but three, was the Knight of Longshaw. So he ran up to him, and cast his arms about him, and kissed him, and asked him what ailed. And the Knight said, and laughed withal: “That has befallen me which befalls most men: I have been overcome, and I believe that my foes are hard on my heels.”

“Will they be a many?” said Osberne. “Not in this first stour,” said the Knight. “Well,” said Osberne, “I will go and look to it to get a few men together to show them out of the Dale.” So he turned hand in hand with the Knight of Longshaw, and cried out to Stephen the Eater to gather forth; and in an hour or so they had enough men and to spare. By that time the pursuers came glittering over the bent, so Osberne and his gathered themselves together and stood till the others came. And when they were within hail, Osberne asked: “What would ye here in arms? We are peaceable men.” Said the pursuers: “We have nought to do with you, but we would have the body of a felon and a traitor hight the Knight of Longshaw.”

Osberne laughed and said: “Here he stands beside me; come and take him!” And the foe were some three score, all a-horseback. So they fell on without more words; but they made nothing of it, and the Wethermelers kept them aloof with spear and bill. Albeit Osberne did not draw his sword, nor did the Knight of Longshaw.

===Chinese===

患难之交

此后约莫过了三年，有一伙带兵刃的人下到谷里来。有人报知奥斯伯恩，他便带上剑前去迎候。他在草坡上迎面撞见他们，只见一行人缓缓下行，个个面带倦色、力竭神疲。他端详了一回，看出他们之中为首的不过三人，而居首的那位不是别人，正是朗肖骑士。于是他奔上前去，张开双臂抱住他，吻了吻他，问他遭了什么变故。朗肖骑士答道，说时犹自带笑：“落到我头上的，正是落到世间大多数人头上的事：我吃了败仗；而且我想，我的仇敌已经紧追在我脚跟后面了。”

“追来的多么？”奥斯伯恩问。“打头这一阵不算多。”骑士道。“好吧，”奥斯伯恩说，“我便去张罗着聚起些人手，送他们出谷去。”于是他与朗肖骑士手挽着手转过身来，高声唤饕客斯蒂芬出来集合人马；约莫一个钟头，人手便已足用而有余。正在那时，追兵甲光闪闪地翻过草坡而来，奥斯伯恩便将自己的人聚拢站定，等着他们到来。待他们行到呼喊可闻之处，奥斯伯恩问道：“你们执兵刃到此，意欲何为？我们乃是安分平和之人。”追兵答道：“我们与你们无干，只要一个恶徒叛逆的身子，那人名叫朗肖骑士。”

奥斯伯恩哈哈一笑，说道：“他就在我身旁站着；来拿他吧！”敌兵约有六十之众，全都骑着马。于是他们不再多言，径直扑了上来；然而全然讨不到便宜，韦瑟梅尔人以长枪钩戟将他们远远挡住。只是奥斯伯恩并未拔剑，朗肖骑士也不曾拔。

===Original===

Then the foemen held off a little, and they said: “Hark ye, ye up-countrymen, if ye do not give up this man, then will we burn your house to the threshold.”

“Yea,” said Osberne, “ye have all day long to do it in, make no delay therefore. Or did ye ever hear who I am?” And they said: “Nay, we know not.” Then he let his red cloak float over him and let his byrny show glittering, and he drew Boardcleaver and suddenly cried out, “The Red Lad! The Red Lad!” and all the others did in like wise. Then the foemen fled up the bent. And Osberne said: “Lightfoot men of Wethermel, here is a job for you: let not one of these men escape from out of the Dale.” So they fell to, and hard they worked at it, and so they wrought that they slew them every one.

Then Osberne went back to the Knight of Longshaw and said: “See, master, it is still a name to conjure with. And now what wilt thou do? Wilt thou gather men in the Dale here? We can find thee a ten score or thereabout of as good men as need be.”

“Nay,” said the Knight, “I will not have them, for meseems I am getting towards the end of my tether, and I will not carry away your good men and true from your wives and your children.” So therewith they went into the stead and were joyful together.

===Chinese===

这时敌兵稍稍退开，说道：“听好了，你们这些上乡人，若不把这个人交出来，我们就把你们的房舍烧个干净，直烧到门槛。”

“好啊，”奥斯伯恩道，“你们有一整天的工夫来办这件事，那就休要耽搁了。再不然——你们可曾听说过我是谁？”他们答道：“不曾，我们不认得。”于是他任那一领红披风在身上飘展，让锁子甲灿然显露，拔出断板剑，猛然高呼：“红衣少年！红衣少年！”其余众人也齐声照样呐喊。敌兵随即往草坡上头逃窜。奥斯伯恩便道：“韦瑟梅尔的捷足汉子们，这里有一桩差事交给你们：莫让这伙人中有一个逃出谷去。”于是众人动手干了起来，干得着实卖力，结果把这伙人杀得一人不剩。

随后奥斯伯恩回到朗肖骑士身旁，说道：“看哪，主人，这名号至今依旧一呼百应。如今你有何打算？可要在这谷中聚集人马？我们能替你寻得二百名上下的好汉，要多好有多好。”

“不必了，”骑士道，“我不要人马，因为依我看来，我已渐近穷途末路；我不愿把你们这些忠勇的好汉子，从妻儿老小身边带走。”于是一行人进了庄宅，欢欢喜喜地聚在一处。

## LXVIII / 六十八

===Original===

The Knight of Longshaw Gathereth Force

The Knight of Longshaw abode at Wethermel in much content, and much it pleased him to look upon the beauty of Elfhild and the fairness of the life that men lived in the Dale. At last he said: “Now I must shake off my sloth somewhat, and it will be a case of farewell.” “Will it?” said Osberne. “Yea,” said the Knight, “for I will to Eastcheaping, and there I will set me to gather men, and I look to it that, ere three months are over, I shall have a good host on foot.” “It is well,” said Osberne.

So in two days’ time the Knight went, with his two men that had fled into the Dale with him, to Eastcheaping, and Osberne rode with him. When they came to Eastcheaping the Knight said: “Now is the time for farewell.” “Nay, nay,” said Osberne, “there shall be no farewell this time at least; but I will help thee with the gathering of men, and when we have got an host I will be the leader thereof. This thou must not gainsay me.” Said the Knight: “But gainsay thee I will, for unless thou gettest thee back to thine own people I will break up my whole purpose.” “And why?” said Osberne. “Thou art blind not to see,” said the Knight. “I come and find thee here as happy as any man in the world, wedded to a fair wife, the lord of a stout and stalwart people who love thee above all things. And I have that in me that tells me that if I carry thee away I carry thee away to death. For I have seen thee in a dream of the night and in a dream of the day living at Wethermel and dying on a field near the City of the Sundering Flood.”

===Chinese===

朗肖骑士集结兵马

朗肖骑士在韦瑟梅尔住了些时日，甚是安适；眼见埃尔弗希尔德的美貌，又见谷中人过日子那般美好，他心中大感欣悦。末了他说道：“如今我须得稍稍抖落身上的疏懒，这该是道别的时候了。”“该是道别么？”奥斯伯恩道。“正是，”骑士说，“我要去东集，在那里着手聚集人马，我预计不出三个月，便可有一支像样的大军编成。”“这就好了。”奥斯伯恩道。

于是过了两天，骑士便带着当初随他逃进谷里来的那两名部下动身去东集，奥斯伯恩与他同行。到得东集，骑士说道：“如今是话别的时候了。”“不，不，”奥斯伯恩说，“至少这一回，再没有什么话别；聚集人马之事，我要助你一臂之力，待大军聚齐，我便做它的统领。这一点你不许回驳我。”骑士道：“我偏要回驳你，因为除非你回到你自己人身边去，我便把我全盘的打算作罢。”“这是为何？”奥斯伯恩问。“你竟瞧不出来，真是瞎了眼，”骑士道，“我来到这里，见你快活得赛过世上任何人：娶了美貌的妻子，做着一族坚毅壮勇之民的主上，而他们爱你又胜过一切。况且我心里有个声音告诉我，我若把你带走，便是把你往死路上带。因为我曾在夜半的梦里、白昼的梦里见过你：活着住在韦瑟梅尔，却死在洪流之城近旁的一片战场上。”

===Original===

Said Osberne: “And shall I choose dishonour then?”

“Nay,” he said, “where is the dishonour? Besides, take this for a gibe, that whereas time agone I could do but ill without thee, now I can do without thee well, for I have three or four fellows will come to my call as soon as they know that my banner is in the field again. Wherefore, I tell thee, thou must either be my unfriend, or get thee back home my friend and my lad.” So when Osberne saw it would no better be, he wept and bade farewell to the Knight of Longshaw, and went his ways back home. Six months hence he heard true tidings of the Knight, that he had gathered an host and fallen on his foes, and had fared nowhere save to thrive. And it is not said that he met the Knight of Longshaw face to face again in this life.

It is further to be told that once in every quarter Osberne went into that same dale wherein he first met Steelhead, and there he came to him, and they had converse together; and though Osberne changed the aspect of him from year to year, as for Steelhead he changed not at all, but was ever the same as when Osberne first saw him, and good love there was between those twain.

Now is there no more to say concerning the Sundering Flood and those that dwelt thereby.

===Chinese===

奥斯伯恩道：“那么，难道要我拣选耻辱不成？”

“不，”他说，“耻辱又从何说起？此外，你且把这话当作一句戏言：从前没有你，我凡事办不大好；如今没有你，我倒办得好了，因为我有三四个伙伴，只消得知我的大旗重又上了战阵，便会一召即至。所以我告诉你：你要么做我的仇敌，要么回你家里去，做我的朋友、我的孩子。”奥斯伯恩见事情再无别的转圜，便哭了，与朗肖骑士道了别，取路回家去了。过了六个月，他听到骑士的真实音讯：他已聚起一支大军，进击众仇敌，所到之处无不如意兴旺。至于他此生再与朗肖骑士当面相见，书中却没有这样的记载。

此外还有一节要说的：每逢一季，奥斯伯恩总要去一趟他初遇钢首的那条山谷，在那里与钢首相会，二人对坐交谈；尽管奥斯伯恩的容貌一年年变迁，钢首却丝毫不变，始终同奥斯伯恩初见他时一模一样，而那两人之间，情爱深笃。

至于分离洪流，和居住在它两岸的人们，如今再没有别的话可说了。
